\displaystyle=r\sum\limits_{\gamma\in\operatorname*{Comp}\nolimits_{n}}\underbrace{\left(-1\right)^{n}\left(-1\right)^{n-\ell\left(\alpha\right)-\left|D\left(\gamma\right)\setminus D\left(\operatorname*{rev}\alpha\right)\right|}}_{=\left(-1\right)^{\ell\left(\alpha\right)}\left(-1\right)^{\left|D\left(\gamma\right)\setminus D\left(\operatorname*{rev}\alpha\right)\right|}}\underbrace{q^{\ell\left(\alpha\right)-1-\left|D\left(\gamma\right)\cap D\left(\operatorname*{rev}\alpha\right)\right|}}_{\begin{subarray}{c}=q^{\ell\left(\alpha\right)}q^{-1}q^{-\left|D\left(\gamma\right)\cap D\left(\operatorname*{rev}\alpha\right)\right|}\end{subarray}}L_{\gamma}

\displaystyle=r\sum\limits_{\gamma\in\operatorname*{Comp}\nolimits_{n}}\left(-1\right)^{\ell\left(\alpha\right)}\left(-1\right)^{\left|D\left(\gamma\right)\setminus D\left(\operatorname*{rev}\alpha\right)\right|}q^{\ell\left(\alpha\right)}q^{-1}q^{-\left|D\left(\gamma\right)\cap D\left(\operatorname*{rev}\alpha\right)\right|}L_{\gamma}

\displaystyle=\underbrace{rq^{-1}}_{\begin{subarray}{c}=p+1\\
\text{(by (\ref{pf.prop.eta.S.r=}))}\end{subarray}}\underbrace{\left(-1\right)^{\ell\left(\alpha\right)}q^{\ell\left(\alpha\right)}}_{=\left(-q\right)^{\ell\left(\alpha\right)}}\sum\limits_{\gamma\in\operatorname*{Comp}\nolimits_{n}}\left(-1\right)^{\left|D\left(\gamma\right)\setminus D\left(\operatorname*{rev}\alpha\right)\right|}\underbrace{q^{-\left|D\left(\gamma\right)\cap D\left(\operatorname*{rev}\alpha\right)\right|}}_{\begin{subarray}{c}=\left(q^{-1}\right)^{\left|D\left(\gamma\right)\cap D\left(\operatorname*{rev}\alpha\right)\right|}\\
=p^{\left|D\left(\gamma\right)\cap D\left(\operatorname*{rev}\alpha\right)\right|}\\
\text{(since }q^{-1}=p\text{)}\end{subarray}}L_{\gamma}

\displaystyle=\left(p+1\right)\left(-q\right)^{\ell\left(\alpha\right)}\sum\limits_{\gamma\in\operatorname*{Comp}\nolimits_{n}}\left(-1\right)^{\left|D\left(\gamma\right)\setminus D\left(\operatorname*{rev}\alpha\right)\right|}p^{\left|D\left(\gamma\right)\cap D\left(\operatorname*{rev}\alpha\right)\right|}L_{\gamma}

\displaystyle=\left(-q\right)^{\ell\left(\alpha\right)}\underbrace{\left(p+1\right)\sum\limits_{\gamma\in\operatorname*{Comp}\nolimits_{n}}\left(-1\right)^{\left|D\left(\gamma\right)\setminus D\left(\operatorname*{rev}\alpha\right)\right|}p^{\left|D\left(\gamma\right)\cap D\left(\operatorname*{rev}\alpha\right)\right|}L_{\gamma}}_{\begin{subarray}{c}=\eta_{\operatorname*{rev}\alpha}^{\left(p\right)}\\
\text{(by (\ref{pf.prop.eta.S.O1.etarev}))}\end{subarray}}

\displaystyle=\left(-q\right)^{\ell\left(\alpha\right)}\eta_{\operatorname*{rev}\alpha}^{\left(p\right)}.

This proves Theorem
3.22
.

Theorem
3.22
generalizes
[
Hsiao07
, Proposition 2.9]
.

Our second formula for the antipode of
\eta_{\alpha}^{\left(q\right)}

comes with no requirement on
q
, but is somewhat more complicated:

Theorem 3.24
.

Let
n\in\mathbb{N}
. Let
\alpha\in\operatorname*{Comp}\nolimits_{n}
. Then, the antipode
S
of
\operatorname*{QSym}
satisfies

S\left(\eta_{\alpha}^{\left(q\right)}\right)=\left(-1\right)^{\ell\left(\alpha\right)}\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\beta\in\operatorname*{Comp}\nolimits_{n};\\
D\left(\beta\right)\subseteq D\left(\operatorname*{rev}\alpha\right)\end{subarray}}\left(q-1\right)^{\ell\left(\alpha\right)-\ell\left(\beta\right)}\eta_{\beta}^{\left(q\right)}.

We will prove this theorem using the following summation lemma:

Lemma 3.25
.

Let
n\in\mathbb{N}
. Let
\alpha\in\operatorname*{Comp}\nolimits_{n}
and
\gamma\in\operatorname*{Comp}\nolimits_{n}
be such that
D\left(\gamma\right)\subseteq D\left(\alpha\right)
. Then:

(a)

For any
u,v\in\mathbf{k}
, we have

\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\beta\in\operatorname*{Comp}\nolimits_{n};\\
D\left(\beta\right)\subseteq D\left(\alpha\right)\text{ and }D\left(\gamma\right)\subseteq D\left(\beta\right)\end{subarray}}u^{\ell\left(\beta\right)-\ell\left(\gamma\right)}v^{\ell\left(\alpha\right)-\ell\left(\beta\right)}=\left(u+v\right)^{\ell\left(\alpha\right)-\ell\left(\gamma\right)}.

(b)

For any
u\in\mathbf{k}
, we have

\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\beta\in\operatorname*{Comp}\nolimits_{n};\\
D\left(\beta\right)\subseteq D\left(\alpha\right)\text{ and }D\left(\gamma\right)\subseteq D\left(\beta\right)\end{subarray}}u^{\ell\left(\beta\right)}=\left(u+1\right)^{\ell\left(\alpha\right)-\ell\left(\gamma\right)}u^{\ell\left(\gamma\right)}.

(c)

For any
v\in\mathbf{k}
, we have

\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\beta\in\operatorname*{Comp}\nolimits_{n};\\
D\left(\beta\right)\subseteq D\left(\alpha\right)\text{ and }D\left(\gamma\right)\subseteq D\left(\beta\right)\end{subarray}}v^{\ell\left(\alpha\right)-\ell\left(\beta\right)}=\left(1+v\right)^{\ell\left(\alpha\right)-\ell\left(\gamma\right)}.

Proof 3.26

(Proof of Lemma
3.25
.)
.

(a)
Let
u,v\in\mathbf{k}
. Set
A:=D\left(\alpha\right)
and
C:=D\left(\gamma\right)
. Then,
A=D\left(\alpha\right)\in\mathcal{P}\left(\left[n-1\right]\right)
(since
\alpha\in\operatorname*{Comp}\nolimits_{n}
, but

D:\operatorname*{Comp}\nolimits_{n}\rightarrow\mathcal{P}\left(\left[n-1\right]\right)
is a map). In other words,
A\subseteq\left[n-1\right]
. Furthermore,
C=D\left(\gamma\right)\subseteq D\left(\alpha\right)=A\subseteq\left[n-1\right]
.

From
C\subseteq A
, we obtain

\displaystyle\left|A\setminus C\right|

\displaystyle=\left|A\right|-\left|C\right|=\left|D\left(\alpha\right)\right|-\left|D\left(\gamma\right)\right|\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \left(\text{since
}A=D\left(\alpha\right)\text{ and }C=D\left(\gamma\right)\right)

\displaystyle=\ell\left(\alpha\right)-\ell\left(\gamma\right)

(since Lemma
3.10

(b)
yields
\ell\left(\alpha\right)-\ell\left(\gamma\right)=\left|D\left(\alpha\right)\right|-\left|D\left(\gamma\right)\right|
).

Now,

\displaystyle\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\beta\in\operatorname*{Comp}\nolimits_{n};\\
D\left(\beta\right)\subseteq D\left(\alpha\right)\text{ and }D\left(\gamma\right)\subseteq D\left(\beta\right)\end{subarray}}\underbrace{u^{\ell\left(\beta\right)-\ell\left(\gamma\right)}}_{\begin{subarray}{c}=u^{\left|D\left(\beta\right)\right|-\left|D\left(\gamma\right)\right|}\\
\text{(since Lemma \ref{lem.comps.l-vs-size} {(b)}}\\
\text{yields }\ell\left(\beta\right)-\ell\left(\gamma\right)=\left|D\left(\beta\right)\right|-\left|D\left(\gamma\right)\right|\text{)}\end{subarray}}\ \ \underbrace{v^{\ell\left(\alpha\right)-\ell\left(\beta\right)}}_{\begin{subarray}{c}=v^{\left|D\left(\alpha\right)\right|-\left|D\left(\beta\right)\right|}\\
\text{(since Lemma \ref{lem.comps.l-vs-size} {(b)}}\\
\text{yields }\ell\left(\alpha\right)-\ell\left(\beta\right)=\left|D\left(\alpha\right)\right|-\left|D\left(\beta\right)\right|\text{)}\end{subarray}}

\displaystyle=\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\beta\in\operatorname*{Comp}\nolimits_{n};\\
D\left(\beta\right)\subseteq D\left(\alpha\right)\text{ and }D\left(\gamma\right)\subseteq D\left(\beta\right)\end{subarray}}u^{\left|D\left(\beta\right)\right|-\left|D\left(\gamma\right)\right|}v^{\left|D\left(\alpha\right)\right|-\left|D\left(\beta\right)\right|}

\displaystyle=\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\beta\in\operatorname*{Comp}\nolimits_{n};\\
D\left(\beta\right)\subseteq A\text{ and }C\subseteq D\left(\beta\right)\end{subarray}}u^{\left|D\left(\beta\right)\right|-\left|C\right|}v^{\left|A\right|-\left|D\left(\beta\right)\right|}\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \left(\text{since }D\left(\gamma\right)=C\text{ and
}D\left(\alpha\right)=A\right)

\displaystyle=\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}I\subseteq\left[n-1\right];\\
I\subseteq A\text{ and
}C\subseteq I\end{subarray}}u^{\left|I\right|-\left|C\right|}v^{\left|A\right|-\left|I\right|}\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \left(\begin{array}[c]{c}\text{here, we have substituted }I\text{ for }D\left(\beta\right)\\
\text{in the sum, since the}\\
\text{map }D:\operatorname*{Comp}\nolimits_{n}\rightarrow\mathcal{P}\left(\left[n-1\right]\right)\\
\text{is a bijection}\end{array}\right)

\displaystyle=\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}I\subseteq A;\\
C\subseteq I\end{subarray}}u^{\left|I\right|-\left|C\right|}v^{\left|A\right|-\left|I\right|}

(33)

(since the condition “
I\subseteq\left[n-1\right]
” under the sum is redundant (because the condition
“
I\subseteq A
” already yields
I\subseteq A\subseteq\left[n-1\right]
)).

Now, each subset
I
of
A
that satisfies
C\subseteq I
can be written as

C\cup Z
for some unique subset
Z\subseteq A\setminus C
. Hence, we can
substitute
C\cup Z
for
I
in the sum
\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}I\subseteq A;\\
C\subseteq I\end{subarray}}u^{\left|I\right|-\left|C\right|}v^{\left|A\right|-\left|I\right|}
. As a consequence, we
obtain

\displaystyle\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}I\subseteq A;\\
C\subseteq I\end{subarray}}u^{\left|I\right|-\left|C\right|}v^{\left|A\right|-\left|I\right|}

\displaystyle=\sum\limits_{Z\subseteq A\setminus C}\underbrace{u^{\left|C\cup Z\right|-\left|C\right|}v^{\left|A\right|-\left|C\cup Z\right|}}_{\begin{subarray}{c}=u^{\left(\left|C\right|+\left|Z\right|\right)-\left|C\right|}v^{\left|A\right|-\left(\left|C\right|+\left|Z\right|\right)}\\
\text{(since }\left|C\cup Z\right|=\left|C\right|+\left|Z\right|\\
\text{(because }Z\subseteq A\setminus C\text{ shows that}\\
\text{the sets }C\text{ and }Z\text{ are disjoint))}\end{subarray}}

\displaystyle=\sum\limits_{Z\subseteq A\setminus C}\underbrace{u^{\left(\left|C\right|+\left|Z\right|\right)-\left|C\right|}}_{=u^{\left|Z\right|}}\underbrace{v^{\left|A\right|-\left(\left|C\right|+\left|Z\right|\right)}}_{\begin{subarray}{c}=v^{\left|A\right|-\left|C\right|-\left|Z\right|}=v^{\left|A\setminus C\right|-\left|Z\right|}\\
\text{(since }\left|A\right|-\left|C\right|=\left|A\setminus C\right|\text{)}\end{subarray}}

\displaystyle=\sum\limits_{Z\subseteq A\setminus C}u^{\left|Z\right|}v^{\left|A\setminus C\right|-\left|Z\right|}=\sum\limits_{k=0}^{\left|A\setminus C\right|}\dbinom{\left|A\setminus C\right|}{k}u^{k}v^{\left|A\setminus C\right|-k}

(here, we have split the sum according to the size
\left|Z\right|

of the subset
Z
, and observed that for each given
k\in\mathbb{N}
, there
are exactly
\dbinom{\left|A\setminus C\right|}{k}
many subsets

Z\subseteq A\setminus C
that have size
k
). Comparing this with

\left(u+v\right)^{\left|A\setminus C\right|}=\sum\limits_{k=0}^{\left|A\setminus C\right|}\dbinom{\left|A\setminus C\right|}{k}u^{k}v^{\left|A\setminus C\right|-k}\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \left(\text{by the binomial formula}\right),

we obtain

\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}I\subseteq A;\\
C\subseteq I\end{subarray}}u^{\left|I\right|-\left|C\right|}v^{\left|A\right|-\left|I\right|}=\left(u+v\right)^{\left|A\setminus C\right|}=\left(u+v\right)^{\ell\left(\alpha\right)-\ell\left(\gamma\right)}

(since
\left|A\setminus C\right|=\ell\left(\alpha\right)-\ell\left(\gamma\right)
). Hence, (
33
)
becomes

\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\beta\in\operatorname*{Comp}\nolimits_{n};\\
D\left(\beta\right)\subseteq D\left(\alpha\right)\text{ and }D\left(\gamma\right)\subseteq D\left(\beta\right)\end{subarray}}u^{\ell\left(\beta\right)-\ell\left(\gamma\right)}v^{\ell\left(\alpha\right)-\ell\left(\beta\right)}=\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}I\subseteq A;\\
C\subseteq I\end{subarray}}u^{\left|I\right|-\left|C\right|}v^{\left|A\right|-\left|I\right|}=\left(u+v\right)^{\ell\left(\alpha\right)-\ell\left(\gamma\right)}.

This proves Lemma
3.25

(a)
.

(b)
This follows by applying Lemma
3.25

(a)
to
v=1
and then multiplying both sides by
u^{\ell\left(\gamma\right)}
.

(c)
This follows by applying Lemma
3.25

(a)
to
u=1
.

Proof 3.27

(Proof of Theorem
3.24
.)
.

We replace
\alpha
by

\operatorname*{rev}\alpha
. Thus,
\alpha
and
\operatorname*{rev}\alpha

become
\operatorname*{rev}\alpha
and
\alpha
, respectively, while the
length
\ell\left(\alpha\right)
stays unchanged. Hence, the claim we must
prove becomes

S\left(\eta_{\operatorname*{rev}\alpha}^{\left(q\right)}\right)=\left(-1\right)^{\ell\left(\alpha\right)}\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\beta\in\operatorname*{Comp}\nolimits_{n};\\
D\left(\beta\right)\subseteq D\left(\alpha\right)\end{subarray}}\left(q-1\right)^{\ell\left(\alpha\right)-\ell\left(\beta\right)}\eta_{\beta}^{\left(q\right)}.

(34)

It is this equality that we will be proving.

First, we observe that every
\beta\in\operatorname*{Comp}\nolimits_{n}

satisfies

S\left(M_{\beta}\right)=\left(-1\right)^{\ell\left(\beta\right)}\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\gamma\in\operatorname*{Comp}\nolimits_{n};\\
D\left(\gamma\right)\subseteq D\left(\operatorname*{rev}\beta\right)\end{subarray}}M_{\gamma}.

(35)

(Indeed, this is just the formula (
21
), applied to
\beta

instead of
\alpha
and restated using Definition
3.21
.) Substituting

\operatorname*{rev}\beta
for
\beta
in (
35
), we
obtain the following: Every
\beta\in\operatorname*{Comp}\nolimits_{n}

satisfies

\displaystyle S\left(M_{\operatorname*{rev}\beta}\right)

\displaystyle=\left(-1\right)^{\ell\left(\operatorname*{rev}\beta\right)}\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\gamma\in\operatorname*{Comp}\nolimits_{n};\\
D\left(\gamma\right)\subseteq D\left(\operatorname*{rev}\left(\operatorname*{rev}\beta\right)\right)\end{subarray}}M_{\gamma}\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \left(\text{since }\operatorname*{rev}\beta\in\operatorname*{Comp}\nolimits_{n}\right)

\displaystyle=\left(-1\right)^{\ell\left(\beta\right)}\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\gamma\in\operatorname*{Comp}\nolimits_{n};\\
D\left(\gamma\right)\subseteq D\left(\beta\right)\end{subarray}}M_{\gamma}

(36)

(since
\operatorname*{rev}\left(\operatorname*{rev}\beta\right)=\beta

and
\ell\left(\operatorname*{rev}\beta\right)=\ell\left(\beta\right)
).

Next, we recall two simple facts from
[
GriVas23
]
. First,
[
GriVas23
, Corollary
3.10]
says that the map

\displaystyle\operatorname*{Comp}\nolimits_{n}

\displaystyle\rightarrow\operatorname*{Comp}\nolimits_{n},

\displaystyle\delta

\displaystyle\mapsto\operatorname*{rev}\delta

is a bijection. Furthermore,
[
GriVas23
, Proposition 3.11]
shows that if

\beta\in\operatorname*{Comp}\nolimits_{n}
is arbitrary, then we have the
logical equivalence

\left(D\left(\operatorname*{rev}\beta\right)\subseteq D\left(\operatorname*{rev}\alpha\right)\right)\ \Longleftrightarrow\ \left(D\left(\beta\right)\subseteq D\left(\alpha\right)\right).

(37)

The definition of
\eta_{\operatorname*{rev}\alpha}^{\left(q\right)}

yields

\displaystyle\eta_{\operatorname*{rev}\alpha}^{\left(q\right)}

\displaystyle=\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\beta\in\operatorname*{Comp}\nolimits_{n};\\
D\left(\beta\right)\subseteq D\left(\operatorname*{rev}\alpha\right)\end{subarray}}r^{\ell\left(\beta\right)}M_{\beta}=\underbrace{\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\beta\in\operatorname*{Comp}\nolimits_{n};\\
D\left(\operatorname*{rev}\beta\right)\subseteq D\left(\operatorname*{rev}\alpha\right)\end{subarray}}}_{\begin{subarray}{c}=\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\beta\in\operatorname*{Comp}\nolimits_{n};\\
D\left(\beta\right)\subseteq D\left(\alpha\right)\end{subarray}}\\
\text{(by the equivalence
(\ref{pf.thm.eta.S2.short.equiv}))}\end{subarray}}\underbrace{r^{\ell\left(\operatorname*{rev}\beta\right)}}_{\begin{subarray}{c}=r^{\ell\left(\beta\right)}\\
\text{(since }\ell\left(\operatorname*{rev}\beta\right)=\ell\left(\beta\right)\text{)}\end{subarray}}M_{\operatorname*{rev}\beta}

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \left(\begin{array}[c]{c}\text{here, we have substituted }\operatorname*{rev}\beta\text{ for }\beta\text{ in the sum,}\\
\text{since the map }\operatorname*{Comp}\nolimits_{n}\rightarrow\operatorname*{Comp}\nolimits_{n},\ \delta\mapsto\operatorname*{rev}\delta\\
\text{is a bijection}\end{array}\right)

\displaystyle=\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\beta\in\operatorname*{Comp}\nolimits_{n};\\
D\left(\beta\right)\subseteq D\left(\alpha\right)\end{subarray}}r^{\ell\left(\beta\right)}M_{\operatorname*{rev}\beta}.

Applying the
\mathbf{k}
-linear map
S
to both sides of this equality, we
obtain

\displaystyle S\left(\eta_{\operatorname*{rev}\alpha}^{\left(q\right)}\right)

\displaystyle=\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\beta\in\operatorname*{Comp}\nolimits_{n};\\
D\left(\beta\right)\subseteq D\left(\alpha\right)\end{subarray}}r^{\ell\left(\beta\right)}S\left(M_{\operatorname*{rev}\beta}\right)

\displaystyle=\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\beta\in\operatorname*{Comp}\nolimits_{n};\\
D\left(\beta\right)\subseteq D\left(\alpha\right)\end{subarray}}r^{\ell\left(\beta\right)}\left(-1\right)^{\ell\left(\beta\right)}\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\gamma\in\operatorname*{Comp}\nolimits_{n};\\
D\left(\gamma\right)\subseteq D\left(\beta\right)\end{subarray}}M_{\gamma}\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \left(\text{by
(\ref{pf.thm.eta.S2.short.S2})}\right)

\displaystyle=\underbrace{\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\beta\in\operatorname*{Comp}\nolimits_{n};\\
D\left(\beta\right)\subseteq D\left(\alpha\right)\end{subarray}}\ \ \sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\gamma\in\operatorname*{Comp}\nolimits_{n};\\
D\left(\gamma\right)\subseteq D\left(\beta\right)\end{subarray}}}_{=\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\gamma\in\operatorname*{Comp}\nolimits_{n};\\
D\left(\gamma\right)\subseteq D\left(\alpha\right)\end{subarray}}\ \ \sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\beta\in\operatorname*{Comp}\nolimits_{n};\\
D\left(\beta\right)\subseteq D\left(\alpha\right)\text{ and }D\left(\gamma\right)\subseteq D\left(\beta\right)\end{subarray}}}\underbrace{r^{\ell\left(\beta\right)}\left(-1\right)^{\ell\left(\beta\right)}}_{=\left(-r\right)^{\ell\left(\beta\right)}}M_{\gamma}

\displaystyle=\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\gamma\in\operatorname*{Comp}\nolimits_{n};\\
D\left(\gamma\right)\subseteq D\left(\alpha\right)\end{subarray}}\ \ \underbrace{\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\beta\in\operatorname*{Comp}\nolimits_{n};\\
D\left(\beta\right)\subseteq D\left(\alpha\right)\text{ and }D\left(\gamma\right)\subseteq D\left(\beta\right)\end{subarray}}\left(-r\right)^{\ell\left(\beta\right)}}_{\begin{subarray}{c}=\left(-r+1\right)^{\ell\left(\alpha\right)-\ell\left(\gamma\right)}\left(-r\right)^{\ell\left(\gamma\right)}\\
\text{(by Lemma \ref{lem.Comp-sandwich-sum} {(b)})}\end{subarray}}M_{\gamma}

\displaystyle=\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\gamma\in\operatorname*{Comp}\nolimits_{n};\\
D\left(\gamma\right)\subseteq D\left(\alpha\right)\end{subarray}}\left(\underbrace{-r+1}_{\begin{subarray}{c}=-q\\
\text{(since }r=q+1\text{)}\end{subarray}}\right)^{\ell\left(\alpha\right)-\ell\left(\gamma\right)}\left(-r\right)^{\ell\left(\gamma\right)}M_{\gamma}

\displaystyle=\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\gamma\in\operatorname*{Comp}\nolimits_{n};\\
D\left(\gamma\right)\subseteq D\left(\alpha\right)\end{subarray}}\underbrace{\left(-q\right)^{\ell\left(\alpha\right)-\ell\left(\gamma\right)}\left(-r\right)^{\ell\left(\gamma\right)}}_{=\left(-1\right)^{\ell\left(\alpha\right)}q^{\ell\left(\alpha\right)-\ell\left(\gamma\right)}r^{\ell\left(\gamma\right)}}M_{\gamma}

\displaystyle=\left(-1\right)^{\ell\left(\alpha\right)}\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\gamma\in\operatorname*{Comp}\nolimits_{n};\\
D\left(\gamma\right)\subseteq D\left(\alpha\right)\end{subarray}}q^{\ell\left(\alpha\right)-\ell\left(\gamma\right)}r^{\ell\left(\gamma\right)}M_{\gamma}.

In view of

\displaystyle\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\beta\in\operatorname*{Comp}\nolimits_{n};\\
D\left(\beta\right)\subseteq D\left(\alpha\right)\end{subarray}}\left(q-1\right)^{\ell\left(\alpha\right)-\ell\left(\beta\right)}\underbrace{\eta_{\beta}^{\left(q\right)}}_{\begin{subarray}{c}=\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\gamma\in\operatorname*{Comp}\nolimits_{n};\\
D\left(\gamma\right)\subseteq D\left(\beta\right)\end{subarray}}r^{\ell\left(\gamma\right)}M_{\gamma}\\
\text{(by
(\ref{eq.def.etaalpha.def}))}\end{subarray}}

\displaystyle=\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\beta\in\operatorname*{Comp}\nolimits_{n};\\
D\left(\beta\right)\subseteq D\left(\alpha\right)\end{subarray}}\left(q-1\right)^{\ell\left(\alpha\right)-\ell\left(\beta\right)}\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\gamma\in\operatorname*{Comp}\nolimits_{n};\\
D\left(\gamma\right)\subseteq D\left(\beta\right)\end{subarray}}r^{\ell\left(\gamma\right)}M_{\gamma}

\displaystyle=\underbrace{\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\beta\in\operatorname*{Comp}\nolimits_{n};\\
D\left(\beta\right)\subseteq D\left(\alpha\right)\end{subarray}}\ \ \sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\gamma\in\operatorname*{Comp}\nolimits_{n};\\
D\left(\gamma\right)\subseteq D\left(\beta\right)\end{subarray}}}_{=\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\gamma\in\operatorname*{Comp}\nolimits_{n};\\
D\left(\gamma\right)\subseteq D\left(\alpha\right)\end{subarray}}\ \ \sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\beta\in\operatorname*{Comp}\nolimits_{n};\\
D\left(\beta\right)\subseteq D\left(\alpha\right)\text{ and }D\left(\gamma\right)\subseteq D\left(\beta\right)\end{subarray}}}\left(q-1\right)^{\ell\left(\alpha\right)-\ell\left(\beta\right)}r^{\ell\left(\gamma\right)}M_{\gamma}

\displaystyle=\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\gamma\in\operatorname*{Comp}\nolimits_{n};\\
D\left(\gamma\right)\subseteq D\left(\alpha\right)\end{subarray}}\ \ \underbrace{\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\beta\in\operatorname*{Comp}\nolimits_{n};\\
D\left(\beta\right)\subseteq D\left(\alpha\right)\text{ and }D\left(\gamma\right)\subseteq D\left(\beta\right)\end{subarray}}\left(q-1\right)^{\ell\left(\alpha\right)-\ell\left(\beta\right)}}_{\begin{subarray}{c}=\left(1+\left(q-1\right)\right)^{\ell\left(\alpha\right)-\ell\left(\gamma\right)}\\
\text{(by Lemma \ref{lem.Comp-sandwich-sum} {(c)})}\end{subarray}}r^{\ell\left(\gamma\right)}M_{\gamma}

\displaystyle=\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\gamma\in\operatorname*{Comp}\nolimits_{n};\\
D\left(\gamma\right)\subseteq D\left(\alpha\right)\end{subarray}}\left(\underbrace{1+\left(q-1\right)}_{=q}\right)^{\ell\left(\alpha\right)-\ell\left(\gamma\right)}r^{\ell\left(\gamma\right)}M_{\gamma}=\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\gamma\in\operatorname*{Comp}\nolimits_{n};\\
D\left(\gamma\right)\subseteq D\left(\alpha\right)\end{subarray}}q^{\ell\left(\alpha\right)-\ell\left(\gamma\right)}r^{\ell\left(\gamma\right)}M_{\gamma},

we can rewrite this as

S\left(\eta_{\operatorname*{rev}\alpha}^{\left(q\right)}\right)=\left(-1\right)^{\ell\left(\alpha\right)}\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\beta\in\operatorname*{Comp}\nolimits_{n};\\
D\left(\beta\right)\subseteq D\left(\alpha\right)\end{subarray}}\left(q-1\right)^{\ell\left(\alpha\right)-\ell\left(\beta\right)}\eta_{\beta}^{\left(q\right)}.

Thus, (
34
) is proved. As we explained, this proves
Theorem
3.24
.

3.5
The coproduct of
\eta_{\alpha}^{\left(q\right)}

The
concatenation
of two compositions
\beta=\left(\beta_{1},\beta_{2},\ldots,\beta_{i}\right)
and
\gamma=\left(\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2},\ldots,\gamma_{j}\right)
is defined to be the composition

\left(\beta_{1},\beta_{2},\ldots,\beta_{i},\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2},\ldots,\gamma_{j}\right)
. It is denoted by
\beta\gamma
.

The coproduct of the Hopf algebra
\operatorname*{QSym}
is a
\mathbf{k}
-linear map
\Delta:\operatorname*{QSym}\rightarrow\operatorname*{QSym}\otimes\operatorname*{QSym}
that can be described by the formula

\Delta\left(M_{\alpha}\right)=\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\beta,\gamma\in\operatorname*{Comp};\\
\alpha=\beta\gamma\end{subarray}}M_{\beta}\otimes M_{\gamma},

(38)

which holds for all
\alpha\in\operatorname*{Comp}
. (See
[
GriRei20
, §5.1]
for the definition of
\Delta
, and see
[
GriRei20
, Proposition
5.1.7]
for a proof of (
38
).)

We claim the following simple formula for
\Delta\left(\eta_{\alpha}^{\left(q\right)}\right)
(analogous to (
38
)):

Theorem 3.28
.

Let
\alpha\in\operatorname*{Comp}
. Then,

\Delta\left(\eta_{\alpha}^{\left(q\right)}\right)=\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\beta,\gamma\in\operatorname*{Comp};\\
\alpha=\beta\gamma\end{subarray}}\eta_{\beta}^{\left(q\right)}\otimes\eta_{\gamma}^{\left(q\right)}.

This generalizes
[
Hsiao07
, Corollary 2.7]
.

We shall first give a direct proof of Theorem
3.28
; later we
will outline another one, which is more circuitous.

The direct proof uses the following notion:

Definition 3.29
.

Let
\gamma
be a composition. Then,
C\left(\gamma\right)
shall denote
the set of all compositions
\beta\in\operatorname*{Comp}\nolimits_{\left|\gamma\right|}
satisfying
D\left(\beta\right)\subseteq D\left(\gamma\right)
. (The compositions belonging to
C\left(\gamma\right)

are often called the
coarsenings
of
\gamma
.)

For instance,
C\left(2,1,3\right)=\left\{\left(2,1,3\right),\ \left(3,3\right),\ \left(2,4\right),\ \left(6\right)\right\}
.

Using the notion of
C\left(\gamma\right)
, we can restate the definition
of
\eta_{\gamma}^{\left(q\right)}
:

Proposition 3.30
.

For any
\gamma\in\operatorname*{Comp}
, we
have

\eta_{\gamma}^{\left(q\right)}=\sum\limits_{\nu\in C\left(\gamma\right)}r^{\ell\left(\nu\right)}M_{\nu}.

Proof 3.31

(Proof of Proposition
3.30
.)
.

Let
\gamma\in\operatorname*{Comp}
. Then,
\gamma\in\operatorname*{Comp}\nolimits_{\left|\gamma\right|}
. Thus, (
5
)
(applied to
\left|\gamma\right|
and
\gamma
instead of
n
and

\alpha
) yields

\eta_{\gamma}^{\left(q\right)}=\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\beta\in\operatorname*{Comp}\nolimits_{\left|\gamma\right|};\\
D\left(\beta\right)\subseteq D\left(\gamma\right)\end{subarray}}r^{\ell\left(\beta\right)}M_{\beta}=\sum\limits_{\beta\in C\left(\gamma\right)}r^{\ell\left(\beta\right)}M_{\beta}

(since the compositions
\beta
that the previous sum was ranging over are
precisely the elements of
C\left(\gamma\right)
). Renaming the summation
index
\beta
as
\nu
on the right hand side of this equality, we obtain

\eta_{\gamma}^{\left(q\right)}=\sum\limits_{\nu\in C\left(\gamma\right)}r^{\ell\left(\nu\right)}M_{\nu}.

This proves Proposition
3.30
.

We shall also use a simple summation formula (
[
GriVas23
, Proposition 5.17]
):

Proposition 3.32
.

Let
\left(A,+,0\right)
be an abelian group.
Let
u_{\mu,\nu}
be an element of
A
for each pair
\left(\mu,\nu\right)\in\operatorname*{Comp}\times\operatorname*{Comp}
of two compositions. Let

\alpha\in\operatorname*{Comp}
. Then,

\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mu,\nu\in\operatorname*{Comp};\\
\mu\nu\in C\left(\alpha\right)\end{subarray}}u_{\mu,\nu}=\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\beta,\gamma\in\operatorname*{Comp};\\
\beta\gamma=\alpha\end{subarray}}\ \ \sum\limits_{\mu\in C\left(\beta\right)}\ \ \sum\limits_{\nu\in C\left(\gamma\right)}u_{\mu,\nu}.

We are now ready to prove Theorem
3.28
:

Proof 3.33

(Proof of Theorem
3.28
.)
.

Proposition

3.30
(applied to
\gamma=\alpha
) yields

\eta_{\alpha}^{\left(q\right)}=\sum\limits_{\nu\in C\left(\alpha\right)}r^{\ell\left(\nu\right)}M_{\nu}=\sum\limits_{\lambda\in C\left(\alpha\right)}r^{\ell\left(\lambda\right)}M_{\lambda}.

Applying the map
\Delta
to both sides of this equality, we find

\displaystyle\Delta\left(\eta_{\alpha}^{\left(q\right)}\right)

\displaystyle=\Delta\left(\sum\limits_{\lambda\in C\left(\alpha\right)}r^{\ell\left(\lambda\right)}M_{\lambda}\right)=\sum\limits_{\lambda\in C\left(\alpha\right)}r^{\ell\left(\lambda\right)}\underbrace{\Delta\left(M_{\lambda}\right)}_{\begin{subarray}{c}=\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mu,\nu\in\operatorname*{Comp};\\
\lambda=\mu\nu\end{subarray}}M_{\mu}\otimes M_{\nu}\\
\text{(by (\ref{eq.Delta-M}), with the}\\
\text{letters }\alpha,\beta,\gamma\text{ renamed as }\lambda,\mu,\nu\text{)}\end{subarray}}

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \left(\text{since the map }\Delta\text{ is }\mathbf{k}\text{-linear}\right)

\displaystyle=\sum\limits_{\lambda\in C\left(\alpha\right)}r^{\ell\left(\lambda\right)}\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mu,\nu\in\operatorname*{Comp};\\
\lambda=\mu\nu\end{subarray}}M_{\mu}\otimes M_{\nu}=\underbrace{\sum\limits_{\lambda\in C\left(\alpha\right)}\ \ \sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mu,\nu\in\operatorname*{Comp};\\
\lambda=\mu\nu\end{subarray}}}_{=\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mu,\nu\in\operatorname*{Comp};\\
\mu\nu\in C\left(\alpha\right)\end{subarray}}}\underbrace{r^{\ell\left(\lambda\right)}}_{\begin{subarray}{c}=r^{\ell\left(\mu\nu\right)}\\
\text{(since }\lambda=\mu\nu\text{)}\end{subarray}}M_{\mu}\otimes M_{\nu}

\displaystyle=\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mu,\nu\in\operatorname*{Comp};\\
\mu\nu\in C\left(\alpha\right)\end{subarray}}r^{\ell\left(\mu\nu\right)}M_{\mu}\otimes M_{\nu}

\displaystyle=\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\beta,\gamma\in\operatorname*{Comp};\\
\beta\gamma=\alpha\end{subarray}}\ \ \sum\limits_{\mu\in C\left(\beta\right)}\ \ \sum\limits_{\nu\in C\left(\gamma\right)}r^{\ell\left(\mu\nu\right)}M_{\mu}\otimes M_{\nu}

(39)

(by Proposition
3.32
, applied to
A=\operatorname*{QSym}\otimes\operatorname*{QSym}
and
u_{\mu,\nu}=r^{\ell\left(\mu\nu\right)}M_{\mu}\otimes M_{\nu}
).

On the other hand, if
\mu,\nu\in\operatorname*{Comp}
are any two
compositions, then

\ell\left(\mu\nu\right)=\ell\left(\mu\right)+\ell\left(\nu\right)\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \left(\text{by \cite[cite]{[\@@bibref{}{comps}{}{}, Proposition 5.2 {(a)}]}}\right)

and thus

r^{\ell\left(\mu\nu\right)}=r^{\ell\left(\mu\right)+\ell\left(\nu\right)}=r^{\ell\left(\mu\right)}r^{\ell\left(\nu\right)}.

(40)

Now,

\displaystyle\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\beta,\gamma\in\operatorname*{Comp};\\
\alpha=\beta\gamma\end{subarray}}\underbrace{\eta_{\beta}^{\left(q\right)}}_{\begin{subarray}{c}=\sum\limits_{\mu\in C\left(\beta\right)}r^{\ell\left(\mu\right)}M_{\mu}\\
\text{(by
Proposition \ref{prop.eta.through-Cgamma})}\end{subarray}}\otimes\underbrace{\eta_{\gamma}^{\left(q\right)}}_{\begin{subarray}{c}=\sum\limits_{\nu\in C\left(\gamma\right)}r^{\ell\left(\nu\right)}M_{\nu}\\
\text{(by Proposition
\ref{prop.eta.through-Cgamma})}\end{subarray}}

\displaystyle=\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\beta,\gamma\in\operatorname*{Comp};\\
\alpha=\beta\gamma\end{subarray}}\underbrace{\left(\sum\limits_{\mu\in C\left(\beta\right)}r^{\ell\left(\mu\right)}M_{\mu}\right)\otimes\left(\sum\limits_{\nu\in C\left(\gamma\right)}r^{\ell\left(\nu\right)}M_{\nu}\right)}_{=\sum\limits_{\mu\in C\left(\beta\right)}\ \ \sum\limits_{\nu\in C\left(\gamma\right)}r^{\ell\left(\mu\right)}r^{\ell\left(\nu\right)}M_{\mu}\otimes M_{\nu}}

\displaystyle=\underbrace{\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\beta,\gamma\in\operatorname*{Comp};\\
\alpha=\beta\gamma\end{subarray}}}_{=\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\beta,\gamma\in\operatorname*{Comp};\\
\beta\gamma=\alpha\end{subarray}}}\ \ \sum\limits_{\mu\in C\left(\beta\right)}\ \ \sum\limits_{\nu\in C\left(\gamma\right)}\underbrace{r^{\ell\left(\mu\right)}r^{\ell\left(\nu\right)}}_{\begin{subarray}{c}=r^{\ell\left(\mu\nu\right)}\\
\text{(by (\ref{pf.thm.Delta-eta.rr}))}\end{subarray}}M_{\mu}\otimes M_{\nu}

\displaystyle=\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\beta,\gamma\in\operatorname*{Comp};\\
\beta\gamma=\alpha\end{subarray}}\ \ \sum\limits_{\mu\in C\left(\beta\right)}\ \ \sum\limits_{\nu\in C\left(\gamma\right)}r^{\ell\left(\mu\nu\right)}M_{\mu}\otimes M_{\nu}.

Comparing this with (
39
), we obtain

\Delta\left(\eta_{\alpha}^{\left(q\right)}\right)=\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\beta,\gamma\in\operatorname*{Comp};\\
\alpha=\beta\gamma\end{subarray}}\eta_{\beta}^{\left(q\right)}\otimes\eta_{\gamma}^{\left(q\right)}.

This proves Theorem
3.28
.

3.6
The coalgebra morphism
T_{r}

We define a
\mathbf{k}
-linear map
T_{r}:\operatorname*{QSym}\rightarrow\operatorname*{QSym}
by setting

T_{r}\left(M_{\alpha}\right)=r^{\ell\left(\alpha\right)}M_{\alpha}\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \text{for each }\alpha\in\operatorname*{Comp}.

This definition is legitimate, since
\left(M_{\alpha}\right)_{\alpha\in\operatorname*{Comp}}
is a basis of the
\mathbf{k}
-module

\operatorname*{QSym}
(and since a
\mathbf{k}
-linear map on a free

\mathbf{k}
-module can be defined by specifying its values on a basis). The
map
T_{r}
is usually not a
\mathbf{k}
-algebra homomorphism, but it is
always a
\mathbf{k}
-coalgebra homomorphism. This chiefly relies on the
following lemma:

Lemma 3.34
.

We have
\Delta\circ T_{r}=\left(T_{r}\otimes T_{r}\right)\circ\Delta
as maps from
\operatorname*{QSym}
to

\operatorname*{QSym}\otimes\operatorname*{QSym}
.

Proof 3.35

(Proof of Lemma
3.34
.)
.

Let
\alpha\in\operatorname*{Comp}
be
arbitrary. The definition of
T_{r}
yields
T_{r}\left(M_{\alpha}\right)=r^{\ell\left(\alpha\right)}M_{\alpha}
. Applying the map
\Delta
to both
sides of this identity, we obtain

\displaystyle\Delta\left(T_{r}\left(M_{\alpha}\right)\right)

\displaystyle=\Delta\left(r^{\ell\left(\alpha\right)}M_{\alpha}\right)=r^{\ell\left(\alpha\right)}\Delta\left(M_{\alpha}\right)=r^{\ell\left(\alpha\right)}\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\beta,\gamma\in\operatorname*{Comp};\\
\alpha=\beta\gamma\end{subarray}}M_{\beta}\otimes M_{\gamma}\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \left(\text{by (\ref{eq.Delta-M})}\right)

\displaystyle=\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\beta,\gamma\in\operatorname*{Comp};\\
\alpha=\beta\gamma\end{subarray}}\underbrace{r^{\ell\left(\alpha\right)}}_{\begin{subarray}{c}=r^{\ell\left(\beta\gamma\right)}\\
\text{(since }\alpha=\beta\gamma\text{)}\end{subarray}}M_{\beta}\otimes M_{\gamma}=\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\beta,\gamma\in\operatorname*{Comp};\\
\alpha=\beta\gamma\end{subarray}}\underbrace{r^{\ell\left(\beta\gamma\right)}}_{\begin{subarray}{c}=r^{\ell\left(\beta\right)+\ell\left(\gamma\right)}\\
\text{(since the definition}\\
\text{of concatenation}\\
\text{yields }\ell\left(\beta\gamma\right)=\ell\left(\beta\right)+\ell\left(\gamma\right)\text{)}\end{subarray}}M_{\beta}\otimes M_{\gamma}

\displaystyle=\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\beta,\gamma\in\operatorname*{Comp};\\
\alpha=\beta\gamma\end{subarray}}\underbrace{r^{\ell\left(\beta\right)+\ell\left(\gamma\right)}M_{\beta}\otimes M_{\gamma}}_{=r^{\ell\left(\beta\right)}M_{\beta}\otimes r^{\ell\left(\gamma\right)}M_{\gamma}}=\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\beta,\gamma\in\operatorname*{Comp};\\
\alpha=\beta\gamma\end{subarray}}r^{\ell\left(\beta\right)}M_{\beta}\otimes r^{\ell\left(\gamma\right)}M_{\gamma}.

Comparing this with

\displaystyle\left(T_{r}\otimes T_{r}\right)\left(\Delta\left(M_{\alpha}\right)\right)

\displaystyle=\left(T_{r}\otimes T_{r}\right)\left(\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\beta,\gamma\in\operatorname*{Comp};\\
\alpha=\beta\gamma\end{subarray}}M_{\beta}\otimes M_{\gamma}\right)\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \left(\text{by (\ref{eq.Delta-M})}\right)

\displaystyle=\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\beta,\gamma\in\operatorname*{Comp};\\
\alpha=\beta\gamma\end{subarray}}\underbrace{T_{r}\left(M_{\beta}\right)}_{\begin{subarray}{c}=r^{\ell\left(\beta\right)}M_{\beta}\\
\text{(by the definition of }T_{r}\text{)}\end{subarray}}\otimes\underbrace{T_{r}\left(M_{\gamma}\right)}_{\begin{subarray}{c}=r^{\ell\left(\gamma\right)}M_{\gamma}\\
\text{(by the definition of }T_{r}\text{)}\end{subarray}}

\displaystyle=\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\beta,\gamma\in\operatorname*{Comp};\\
\alpha=\beta\gamma\end{subarray}}r^{\ell\left(\beta\right)}M_{\beta}\otimes r^{\ell\left(\gamma\right)}M_{\gamma},

we obtain

\Delta\left(T_{r}\left(M_{\alpha}\right)\right)=\left(T_{r}\otimes T_{r}\right)\left(\Delta\left(M_{\alpha}\right)\right)=\left(\left(T_{r}\otimes T_{r}\right)\circ\Delta\right)\left(M_{\alpha}\right).

Hence,
\left(\Delta\circ T_{r}\right)\left(M_{\alpha}\right)=\Delta\left(T_{r}\left(M_{\alpha}\right)\right)=\left(\left(T_{r}\otimes T_{r}\right)\circ\Delta\right)\left(M_{\alpha}\right)
.

Forget that we fixed
\alpha
. We thus have proved that
\left(\Delta\circ T_{r}\right)\left(M_{\alpha}\right)=\left(\left(T_{r}\otimes T_{r}\right)\circ\Delta\right)\left(M_{\alpha}\right)
for each

\alpha\in\operatorname*{Comp}
. In other words, the two maps
\Delta\circ T_{r}
and
\left(T_{r}\otimes T_{r}\right)\circ\Delta
agree on every
element of the basis
\left(M_{\alpha}\right)_{\alpha\in\operatorname*{Comp}}
of
\operatorname*{QSym}
. Since these two maps both
are
\mathbf{k}
-linear, this entails that they are completely identical. In
other words, we have
\Delta\circ T_{r}=\left(T_{r}\otimes T_{r}\right)\circ\Delta
. This proves Lemma
3.34
.

Proposition 3.36
.

The map
T_{r}:\operatorname*{QSym}\rightarrow\operatorname*{QSym}
is a
\mathbf{k}
-coalgebra homomorphism.

Proof 3.37

(Proof of Proposition
3.36
.)
.

Let
\varepsilon:\operatorname*{QSym}\rightarrow\mathbf{k}
be the counit of the
\mathbf{k}
-coalgebra
\operatorname*{QSym}
. It is well-known (and easy to see) that
each composition
\alpha\in\operatorname*{Comp}
satisfies
\varepsilon\left(M_{\alpha}\right)=\left[\alpha=\varnothing\right]
(where we are using
Convention
3.2
again). This, in turn, makes it straightforward
to check that
\left(\varepsilon\circ T_{r}\right)\left(M_{\alpha}\right)=\varepsilon\left(M_{\alpha}\right)
for each
\alpha\in\operatorname*{Comp}
. In other words, the two maps
\varepsilon\circ T_{r}
and
\varepsilon
agree on every element of the basis
\left(M_{\alpha}\right)_{\alpha\in\operatorname*{Comp}}
of
\operatorname*{QSym}
. Since these two maps both are
\mathbf{k}
-linear, this entails that they
are completely identical. In other words, we have
\varepsilon\circ T_{r}=\varepsilon
. Combining this with the equality
\Delta\circ T_{r}=\left(T_{r}\otimes T_{r}\right)\circ\Delta
from Lemma

3.34
, we conclude that the linear map
T_{r}
is a
\mathbf{k}
-coalgebra homomorphism. This proves Proposition
3.36
.

To us, the map
T_{r}
becomes useful thanks to the following slick expression
for
\eta_{\alpha}^{\left(q\right)}
that it allows:

Theorem 3.38
.

Let
S:\operatorname*{QSym}\rightarrow\operatorname*{QSym}

be the antipode of the Hopf algebra
\operatorname*{QSym}
. Let
\alpha\in\operatorname*{Comp}
. Then,

\eta_{\alpha}^{\left(q\right)}=\left(-1\right)^{\ell\left(\alpha\right)}T_{r}\left(S\left(M_{\operatorname*{rev}\alpha}\right)\right).

Proof 3.39

(Proof of Theorem
3.38
.)
.

Write the composition
\alpha
in the form

\alpha=\left(\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2},\ldots,\alpha_{\ell}\right)
. Thus,
the definition of
\ell\left(\alpha\right)
yields
\ell\left(\alpha\right)=\ell
, whereas the definition of
\operatorname*{rev}\alpha

yields
\operatorname*{rev}\alpha=\left(\alpha_{\ell},\alpha_{\ell-1},\ldots,\alpha_{1}\right)
. Moreover, a trivial fact (
[
GriVas23
, Proposition
3.3]
) yields
\left|\operatorname*{rev}\alpha\right|=\left|\alpha\right|
.

Set
n=\left|\alpha\right|
. Thus,
\alpha\in\operatorname*{Comp}\nolimits_{n}
. Furthermore,
\left|\operatorname*{rev}\alpha\right|=\left|\alpha\right|=n
, so that
\operatorname*{rev}\alpha\in\operatorname*{Comp}\nolimits_{n}
. Also, as we know, we have

\operatorname*{rev}\alpha=\left(\alpha_{\ell},\alpha_{\ell-1},\ldots,\alpha_{1}\right)
. Hence, (
21
) (applied to

\operatorname*{rev}\alpha
and
\alpha_{\ell+1-i}
instead of
\alpha
and

\alpha_{i}
) yields

S\left(M_{\operatorname*{rev}\alpha}\right)=\left(-1\right)^{\ell}\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\gamma\in\operatorname*{Comp}\nolimits_{n};\\
D\left(\gamma\right)\subseteq D\left(\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2},\ldots,\alpha_{\ell}\right)\end{subarray}}M_{\gamma}=\left(-1\right)^{\ell}\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\gamma\in\operatorname*{Comp}\nolimits_{n};\\
D\left(\gamma\right)\subseteq D\left(\alpha\right)\end{subarray}}M_{\gamma}

(since
\left(\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2},\ldots,\alpha_{\ell}\right)=\alpha
).
Applying the map
T_{r}
to both sides of this equality, we obtain

\displaystyle T_{r}\left(S\left(M_{\operatorname*{rev}\alpha}\right)\right)

\displaystyle=T_{r}\left(\left(-1\right)^{\ell}\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\gamma\in\operatorname*{Comp}\nolimits_{n};\\
D\left(\gamma\right)\subseteq D\left(\alpha\right)\end{subarray}}M_{\gamma}\right)=\left(-1\right)^{\ell}\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\gamma\in\operatorname*{Comp}\nolimits_{n};\\
D\left(\gamma\right)\subseteq D\left(\alpha\right)\end{subarray}}\underbrace{T_{r}\left(M_{\gamma}\right)}_{\begin{subarray}{c}=r^{\ell\left(\gamma\right)}M_{\gamma}\\
\text{(by the definition of }T_{r}\text{)}\end{subarray}}

\displaystyle=\left(-1\right)^{\ell}\underbrace{\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\gamma\in\operatorname*{Comp}\nolimits_{n};\\
D\left(\gamma\right)\subseteq D\left(\alpha\right)\end{subarray}}r^{\ell\left(\gamma\right)}M_{\gamma}}_{\begin{subarray}{c}=\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\beta\in\operatorname*{Comp}\nolimits_{n};\\
D\left(\beta\right)\subseteq D\left(\alpha\right)\end{subarray}}r^{\ell\left(\beta\right)}M_{\beta}\\
=\eta_{\alpha}^{\left(q\right)}\\
\text{(by
(\ref{eq.def.etaalpha.def}))}\end{subarray}}=\left(-1\right)^{\ell}\eta_{\alpha}^{\left(q\right)}.

Multiplying both sides of this equality by
\left(-1\right)^{\ell\left(\alpha\right)}
, we obtain

\left(-1\right)^{\ell\left(\alpha\right)}T_{r}\left(S\left(M_{\operatorname*{rev}\alpha}\right)\right)=\underbrace{\left(-1\right)^{\ell\left(\alpha\right)}}_{\begin{subarray}{c}=\left(-1\right)^{\ell}\\
\text{(since }\ell\left(\alpha\right)=\ell\text{)}\end{subarray}}\left(-1\right)^{\ell}\eta_{\alpha}^{\left(q\right)}=\underbrace{\left(-1\right)^{\ell}\left(-1\right)^{\ell}}_{\begin{subarray}{c}=\left(-1\right)^{\ell+\ell}=1\\
\text{(since }\ell+\ell=2\ell\text{ is even)}\end{subarray}}\eta_{\alpha}^{\left(q\right)}=\eta_{\alpha}^{\left(q\right)}.

This proves Theorem
3.38
.

3.7
Another proof for
\Delta\left(\eta_{\alpha}^{\left(q\right)}\right)

Let us now outline another proof of Theorem
3.28
.

Proof 3.40

(Second proof of Theorem
3.28
(sketched).)
.

For every
\alpha\in\operatorname*{Comp}
, we have

\displaystyle\Delta\left(M_{\operatorname*{rev}\alpha}\right)

\displaystyle=\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\beta,\gamma\in\operatorname*{Comp};\\
\operatorname*{rev}\alpha=\beta\gamma\end{subarray}}M_{\beta}\otimes M_{\gamma}\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \left(\text{by
(\ref{eq.Delta-M})}\right)

\displaystyle=\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\beta,\gamma\in\operatorname*{Comp};\\
\operatorname*{rev}\alpha=\left(\operatorname*{rev}\gamma\right)\left(\operatorname*{rev}\beta\right)\end{subarray}}M_{\operatorname*{rev}\gamma}\otimes M_{\operatorname*{rev}\beta}\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \left(\begin{array}[c]{c}\text{here, we have}\\
\text{substituted }\operatorname*{rev}\gamma\text{ and }\operatorname*{rev}\beta\\
\text{for }\beta\text{ and }\gamma\text{ in the sum}\end{array}\right)

\displaystyle=\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\beta,\gamma\in\operatorname*{Comp};\\
\operatorname*{rev}\alpha=\operatorname*{rev}\left(\beta\gamma\right)\end{subarray}}M_{\operatorname*{rev}\gamma}\otimes M_{\operatorname*{rev}\beta}\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \left(\begin{array}[c]{c}\text{since it is easy to see}\\
\text{that }\left(\operatorname*{rev}\gamma\right)\left(\operatorname*{rev}\beta\right)=\operatorname*{rev}\left(\beta\gamma\right)\\
\text{for any }\beta,\gamma\in\operatorname*{Comp}\end{array}\right)

\displaystyle=\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\beta,\gamma\in\operatorname*{Comp};\\
\alpha=\beta\gamma\end{subarray}}M_{\operatorname*{rev}\gamma}\otimes M_{\operatorname*{rev}\beta}

(47)

(since the condition “
\operatorname*{rev}\alpha=\operatorname*{rev}\left(\beta\gamma\right)
” is
equivalent to “
\alpha=\beta\gamma
”).

Let
S:\operatorname*{QSym}\rightarrow\operatorname*{QSym}
be the antipode of
the Hopf algebra
\operatorname*{QSym}
. A classical result (see, e.g.,

[
GriRei20
, Exercise 1.4.28]
) says that the antipode of any Hopf algebra is
a
\mathbf{k}
-coalgebra anti-endomorphism (see
[
GriRei20
, Definition
1.4.8]
for the definition of this concept). Thus, in particular, the
antipode
S
of
\operatorname*{QSym}
is a
\mathbf{k}
-coalgebra
anti-endomorphism. In particular, this says that

\Delta\circ S=T\circ\left(S\otimes S\right)\circ\Delta,

where
T:\operatorname*{QSym}\otimes\operatorname*{QSym}\rightarrow\operatorname*{QSym}\otimes\operatorname*{QSym}
is the
\mathbf{k}
-linear
map that sends each pure tensor
x\otimes y
to
y\otimes x
. Applying both
sides of this equality to
M_{\operatorname*{rev}\alpha}
, we obtain

\displaystyle\left(\Delta\circ S\right)\left(M_{\operatorname*{rev}\alpha}\right)

\displaystyle=\left(T\circ\left(S\otimes S\right)\circ\Delta\right)\left(M_{\operatorname*{rev}\alpha}\right)=T\left(\left(S\otimes S\right)\left(\Delta\left(M_{\operatorname*{rev}\alpha}\right)\right)\right)

\displaystyle=T\left(\left(S\otimes S\right)\left(\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\beta,\gamma\in\operatorname*{Comp};\\
\alpha=\beta\gamma\end{subarray}}M_{\operatorname*{rev}\gamma}\otimes M_{\operatorname*{rev}\beta}\right)\right)\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \left(\text{by (\ref{pf.thm.Delta-eta.short.1})}\right)

\displaystyle=\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\beta,\gamma\in\operatorname*{Comp};\\
\alpha=\beta\gamma\end{subarray}}S\left(M_{\operatorname*{rev}\beta}\right)\otimes S\left(M_{\operatorname*{rev}\gamma}\right)

(48)

(by the definitions of the maps
T
and
S\otimes S
).

However, Lemma
3.34
yields
\Delta\circ T_{r}=\left(T_{r}\otimes T_{r}\right)\circ\Delta
, so that

\displaystyle\left(\Delta\circ T_{r}\right)\left(S\left(M_{\operatorname*{rev}\alpha}\right)\right)

\displaystyle=\left(\left(T_{r}\otimes T_{r}\right)\circ\Delta\right)\left(S\left(M_{\operatorname*{rev}\alpha}\right)\right)=\left(T_{r}\otimes T_{r}\right)\left(\left(\Delta\circ S\right)\left(M_{\operatorname*{rev}\alpha}\right)\right)

\displaystyle=\left(T_{r}\otimes T_{r}\right)\left(\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\beta,\gamma\in\operatorname*{Comp};\\
\alpha=\beta\gamma\end{subarray}}S\left(M_{\operatorname*{rev}\beta}\right)\otimes S\left(M_{\operatorname*{rev}\gamma}\right)\right)\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \left(\text{by (\ref{pf.thm.Delta-eta.short.4})}\right)

\displaystyle=\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\beta,\gamma\in\operatorname*{Comp};\\
\alpha=\beta\gamma\end{subarray}}\underbrace{T_{r}\left(S\left(M_{\operatorname*{rev}\beta}\right)\right)}_{\begin{subarray}{c}=\eta_{\beta}^{\left(q\right)}/\left(-1\right)^{\ell\left(\beta\right)}\\
\text{(since Theorem \ref{thm.Tr.eta}}\\
\text{yields }\eta_{\beta}^{\left(q\right)}=\left(-1\right)^{\ell\left(\beta\right)}T_{r}\left(S\left(M_{\operatorname*{rev}\beta}\right)\right)\text{)}\end{subarray}}\otimes\underbrace{T_{r}\left(S\left(M_{\operatorname*{rev}\gamma}\right)\right)}_{\begin{subarray}{c}=\eta_{\gamma}^{\left(q\right)}/\left(-1\right)^{\ell\left(\gamma\right)}\\
\text{(since Theorem \ref{thm.Tr.eta}}\\
\text{yields }\eta_{\gamma}^{\left(q\right)}=\left(-1\right)^{\ell\left(\gamma\right)}T_{r}\left(S\left(M_{\operatorname*{rev}\gamma}\right)\right)\text{)}\end{subarray}}

\displaystyle=\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\beta,\gamma\in\operatorname*{Comp};\\
\alpha=\beta\gamma\end{subarray}}\left(\eta_{\beta}^{\left(q\right)}/\left(-1\right)^{\ell\left(\beta\right)}\right)\otimes\left(\eta_{\gamma}^{\left(q\right)}/\left(-1\right)^{\ell\left(\gamma\right)}\right)

\displaystyle=\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\beta,\gamma\in\operatorname*{Comp};\\
\alpha=\beta\gamma\end{subarray}}\left(\eta_{\beta}^{\left(q\right)}\otimes\eta_{\gamma}^{\left(q\right)}\right)/\underbrace{\left(-1\right)^{\ell\left(\beta\right)+\ell\left(\gamma\right)}}_{\begin{subarray}{c}=\left(-1\right)^{\ell\left(\alpha\right)}\\
\text{(since it is easy to see that }\ell\left(\beta\right)+\ell\left(\gamma\right)=\ell\left(\beta\gamma\right)\text{,}\\
\text{but using }\alpha=\beta\gamma\text{ we can
rewrite}\\
\text{this as }\ell\left(\beta\right)+\ell\left(\gamma\right)=\ell\left(\alpha\right)\text{)}\end{subarray}}

\displaystyle=\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\beta,\gamma\in\operatorname*{Comp};\\
\alpha=\beta\gamma\end{subarray}}\left(\eta_{\beta}^{\left(q\right)}\otimes\eta_{\gamma}^{\left(q\right)}\right)/\left(-1\right)^{\ell\left(\alpha\right)}.

Multiplying this equality by
\left(-1\right)^{\ell\left(\alpha\right)}
, we find

\left(-1\right)^{\ell\left(\alpha\right)}\left(\Delta\circ T_{r}\right)\left(S\left(M_{\operatorname*{rev}\alpha}\right)\right)=\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\beta,\gamma\in\operatorname*{Comp};\\
\alpha=\beta\gamma\end{subarray}}\eta_{\beta}^{\left(q\right)}\otimes\eta_{\gamma}^{\left(q\right)},

so that

\displaystyle\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\beta,\gamma\in\operatorname*{Comp};\\
\alpha=\beta\gamma\end{subarray}}\eta_{\beta}^{\left(q\right)}\otimes\eta_{\gamma}^{\left(q\right)}

\displaystyle=\left(-1\right)^{\ell\left(\alpha\right)}\left(\Delta\circ T_{r}\right)\left(S\left(M_{\operatorname*{rev}\alpha}\right)\right)

\displaystyle=\left(-1\right)^{\ell\left(\alpha\right)}\Delta\left(T_{r}\left(S\left(M_{\operatorname*{rev}\alpha}\right)\right)\right)

\displaystyle=\Delta\left(\underbrace{\left(-1\right)^{\ell\left(\alpha\right)}T_{r}\left(S\left(M_{\operatorname*{rev}\alpha}\right)\right)}_{\begin{subarray}{c}=\eta_{\alpha}^{\left(q\right)}\\
\text{(by Theorem
\ref{thm.Tr.eta})}\end{subarray}}\right)=\Delta\left(\eta_{\alpha}^{\left(q\right)}\right).

This proves Theorem
3.28
again.

4
The dual eta basis of
\operatorname*{NSym}

4.1
\operatorname*{NSym}
and the duality pairing

Let
\operatorname*{NSym}
denote the free
\mathbf{k}
-algebra with
generators
H_{1},H_{2},H_{3},\ldots
(that is, the tensor algebra of the free

\mathbf{k}
-module with basis
\left(H_{1},H_{2},H_{3},\ldots\right)
).
This
\mathbf{k}
-algebra
\operatorname*{NSym}
is known as the
ring of
noncommutative symmetric functions
over
\mathbf{k}
. We refer to

[
GriRei20
, §5.4]
,
[
GKLLRT94
]
and
[
Meliot17
, §6.1]
for more
about this
\mathbf{k}
-algebra
9
9

9

We note some notational differences:

•

What we call
H_{\alpha}
is called
S_{\alpha}
in
[
GKLLRT94
]
and
in
[
Meliot17
]
.

•

The algebra
\operatorname*{NSym}
is denoted by
\operatorname*{NCSym}

in
[
Meliot17
]
(unfortunately, since
\operatorname*{NCSym}
also has a
different meaning).

; we will only need a few basic properties.

We set
H_{0}:=1\in\operatorname*{NSym}
. Thus, an element
H_{n}
of

\operatorname*{NSym}
is defined for each
n\in\mathbb{N}
. For any
composition
\alpha=\left(\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2},\ldots,\alpha_{k}\right)\in\operatorname*{Comp}
, we set

H_{\alpha}:=H_{\alpha_{1}}H_{\alpha_{2}}\cdots H_{\alpha_{k}}\in\operatorname*{NSym}.

The family
\left(H_{\alpha}\right)_{\alpha\in\operatorname*{Comp}}
is
then a basis of the
\mathbf{k}
-module
\operatorname*{NSym}
. (Note that

H_{\left(n\right)}=H_{n}
for each
n>0
.)

The
\mathbf{k}
-algebra
\operatorname*{NSym}
is graded, with each generator

H_{n}
being homogeneous of degree
n
(and thus each basis element

H_{\alpha}
being homogeneous of degree
\left|\alpha\right|
). It
becomes a connected graded
\mathbf{k}
-bialgebra if we define its coproduct

\Delta:\operatorname*{NSym}\rightarrow\operatorname*{NSym}\otimes\operatorname*{NSym}
and its counit
\varepsilon:\operatorname*{NSym}\rightarrow\mathbf{k}
as follows:

•

The coproduct
\Delta:\operatorname*{NSym}\rightarrow\operatorname*{NSym}\otimes\operatorname*{NSym}
is the
\mathbf{k}
-algebra
homomorphism that sends each generator
H_{n}
to
\sum\limits_{i=0}^{n}H_{i}\otimes H_{n-i}
.

•

The counit
\varepsilon:\operatorname*{NSym}\rightarrow\mathbf{k}
is
the
\mathbf{k}
-algebra homomorphism that sends each generator
H_{n}
(with

n>0
) to
0
.

Therefore,
\operatorname*{NSym}
becomes a Hopf algebra (since any connected
graded
\mathbf{k}
-bialgebra is a Hopf algebra). Its antipode
S
is
described in
[
GriRei20
, (5.4.12)]
.

Most importantly to us, the Hopf algebra
\operatorname*{NSym}
is isomorphic
to the graded dual of
\operatorname*{QSym}
. Specifically, we can define a

\mathbf{k}
-bilinear form
\left\langle\cdot,\cdot\right\rangle:\operatorname*{NSym}\times\operatorname*{QSym}\rightarrow\mathbf{k}
by
requiring that

\left\langle H_{\alpha},M_{\beta}\right\rangle=\left[\alpha=\beta\right]

(49)

for all
\alpha,\beta\in\operatorname*{Comp}
(where we are using Convention

3.2
)
10
10

10

This bilinear form
\left\langle\cdot,\cdot\right\rangle
is denoted by
\left(\cdot,\cdot\right)
in

[
GriRei20
, §5.4]
.
. It can be seen that this
\mathbf{k}
-bilinear form
produces a canonical isomorphism

\displaystyle\operatorname*{NSym}

\displaystyle\rightarrow\operatorname*{QSym}\nolimits^{o},

\displaystyle f

\displaystyle\mapsto\left\langle f,\cdot\right\rangle

of graded Hopf algebras, where
\operatorname*{QSym}\nolimits^{o}
is the
graded dual of the Hopf algebra
\operatorname*{QSym}
. Thus, we can identify

\operatorname*{NSym}
with the graded dual of the Hopf algebra

\operatorname*{QSym}
. (In
[
GriRei20
, §5.4]
, this is used as a
definition of
\operatorname*{NSym}
, while the properties that we used to
define
\operatorname*{NSym}
above are stated as
[
GriRei20
, Theorem 5.4.2]
.)

4.2
The dual eta basis

We shall now construct a basis of
\operatorname*{NSym}
that is dual to the
basis
\left(\eta_{\alpha}^{\left(q\right)}\right)_{\alpha\in\operatorname*{Comp}}
of
\operatorname*{QSym}
. This requires the
assumption that
r
is invertible (since this assumption ensures that
\left(\eta_{\alpha}^{\left(q\right)}\right)_{\alpha\in\operatorname*{Comp}}

is a basis of
\operatorname*{QSym}
in the first place
11
11

11

by Theorem

3.13
). Thus, we make the following convention:

Convention 4.1
.

For the rest of Section
4
, we assume that
r

is invertible in
\mathbf{k}
.

Definition 4.1
.

For each
n\in\mathbb{N}
and each composition
\alpha
of

n
, we define an element

\eta_{\alpha}^{\ast\left(q\right)}:=\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\beta\in\operatorname*{Comp}\nolimits_{n};\\
D\left(\alpha\right)\subseteq D\left(\beta\right)\end{subarray}}\dfrac{1}{r^{\ell\left(\beta\right)}}\left(-1\right)^{\ell\left(\beta\right)-\ell\left(\alpha\right)}H_{\beta}\in\operatorname*{NSym}.

Example 4.2
.

We have

\displaystyle\eta_{\left({}\right)}^{\ast\left(q\right)}

\displaystyle=H_{\left({}\right)}=1_{\operatorname*{NSym}};

\displaystyle\eta_{\left(1\right)}^{\ast\left(q\right)}

\displaystyle=\dfrac{1}{r}H_{\left(1\right)};

\displaystyle\eta_{\left(2\right)}^{\ast\left(q\right)}

\displaystyle=\dfrac{1}{r}H_{\left(2\right)}-\dfrac{1}{r^{2}}H_{\left(1,1\right)};

\displaystyle\eta_{\left(1,1\right)}^{\ast\left(q\right)}

\displaystyle=\dfrac{1}{r^{2}}H_{\left(1,1\right)}.

We now claim the following:

Proposition 4.3
.

(a)

The family
\left(\eta_{\alpha}^{\ast\left(q\right)}\right)_{\alpha\in\operatorname*{Comp}}
is the basis of

\operatorname*{NSym}
dual to the basis
\left(\eta_{\alpha}^{\left(q\right)}\right)_{\alpha\in\operatorname*{Comp}}
of
\operatorname*{QSym}
with respect to the bilinear form
\left\langle\cdot,\cdot\right\rangle
.

Here, the notion of a “dual basis” should
be understood in the graded sense, as explained in
[
GriRei20
, §1.6]
.
Concretely, our claim is saying that
\left(\eta_{\alpha}^{\ast\left(q\right)}\right)_{\alpha\in\operatorname*{Comp}}
is a graded basis of

\operatorname*{NSym}
and satisfies

\left\langle\eta_{\alpha}^{\ast\left(q\right)},\eta_{\beta}^{\left(q\right)}\right\rangle=\left[\alpha=\beta\right]

(50)

for all
\alpha,\beta\in\operatorname*{Comp}
.

(b)

Let
n\in\mathbb{N}
. Consider the
n
-th graded
components
\operatorname*{QSym}\nolimits_{n}
and
\operatorname*{NSym}\nolimits_{n}
of the graded
\mathbf{k}
-modules
\operatorname*{QSym}
and

\operatorname*{NSym}
. Then, the family
\left(\eta_{\alpha}^{\ast\left(q\right)}\right)_{\alpha\in\operatorname*{Comp}\nolimits_{n}}
is the
basis of
\operatorname*{NSym}\nolimits_{n}
dual to the basis
\left(\eta_{\alpha}^{\left(q\right)}\right)_{\alpha\in\operatorname*{Comp}\nolimits_{n}}
of
\operatorname*{QSym}\nolimits_{n}
with respect to the
bilinear form
\left\langle\cdot,\cdot\right\rangle
.

To prove this, we will use a general fact about dual bases of
\mathbf{k}
-modules that should be known from linear algebra, but is hard to find
explicitly in the literature. This fact is a close relative of the classical
linear-algebraic result that the transpose of a matrix represents the adjoint
of its linear map:

Lemma 4.4
.

Let
F
and
U
be two free
\mathbf{k}
-modules,
and let
\left\langle\cdot,\cdot\right\rangle:F\times U\rightarrow\mathbf{k}
be a
\mathbf{k}
-bilinear form. Let
A
be a finite set. Let

\left(f_{\alpha}\right)_{\alpha\in A}
be a basis of the
\mathbf{k}
-module
F
, and let
\left(g_{\alpha}\right)_{\alpha\in A}
be a further
family of elements of
F
. Let
\left(u_{\alpha}\right)_{\alpha\in A}
and

\left(v_{\alpha}\right)_{\alpha\in A}
be two bases of the
\mathbf{k}
-module
U
.

Assume that the basis
\left(u_{\alpha}\right)_{\alpha\in A}
of
U
is
dual to the basis
\left(f_{\alpha}\right)_{\alpha\in A}
of
F
; in other
words, assume that

\left\langle f_{\beta},u_{\alpha}\right\rangle=\left[\beta=\alpha\right]

(51)

for all
\beta,\alpha\in A
.

Furthermore, let
c_{\alpha,\beta}
be an element of
\mathbf{k}
for each
pair
\left(\alpha,\beta\right)\in A\times A
. Assume that

u_{\beta}=\sum\limits_{\alpha\in A}c_{\alpha,\beta}v_{\alpha}\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \text{for each }\beta\in A.

(52)

Assume furthermore that

g_{\alpha}=\sum\limits_{\beta\in A}c_{\alpha,\beta}f_{\beta}\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \text{for each }\alpha\in A.

(53)

Then, the families
\left(v_{\alpha}\right)_{\alpha\in A}
and
\left(g_{\alpha}\right)_{\alpha\in A}
are mutually dual bases of the
\mathbf{k}
-modules
U
and
F
, respectively.

Proof 4.5

(Proof of Lemma
4.4
.)
.

We shall prove several claims:

Claim 1:
Each
w\in F
satisfies

w=\sum\limits_{\alpha\in A}\left\langle w,u_{\alpha}\right\rangle f_{\alpha}.

(54)

Proof 4.6

(Proof of Claim 1.)
.

Let
w\in F
. The equality (
54
)
is a well-known property of dual bases (since the basis
\left(u_{\alpha}\right)_{\alpha\in A}
of
U
is dual to the basis
\left(f_{\alpha}\right)_{\alpha\in A}
of
F
), but let us recall its proof for its sake of completeness.

The family
\left(f_{\alpha}\right)_{\alpha\in A}
is a basis of
F
, thus
spans
F
. Hence,
w\in F
can be written as a
\mathbf{k}
-linear combination
of this family. In other words, there exists a family
\left(d_{\alpha}\right)_{\alpha\in A}\in\mathbf{k}^{A}
of coefficients such that

w=\sum\limits_{\alpha\in A}d_{\alpha}f_{\alpha}.

(55)

Consider this family
\left(d_{\alpha}\right)_{\alpha\in A}
.
Then,

w=\sum\limits_{\alpha\in A}d_{\alpha}f_{\alpha}=\sum\limits_{\beta\in A}d_{\beta}f_{\beta}

(here, we have renamed the summation index
\alpha
as
\beta
). Hence, each

\alpha\in A
satisfies

\left\langle w,u_{\alpha}\right\rangle=\left\langle\sum\limits_{\beta\in A}d_{\beta}f_{\beta},u_{\alpha}\right\rangle=\sum\limits_{\beta\in A}d_{\beta}\underbrace{\left\langle f_{\beta},u_{\alpha}\right\rangle}_{\begin{subarray}{c}=\left[\beta=\alpha\right]\\
\text{(by
(\ref{eq.lem.dual-bases-crit.dual}))}\end{subarray}}=\sum\limits_{\beta\in A}d_{\beta}\left[\beta=\alpha\right]=d_{\alpha}

(since the only nonzero addend in the sum
\sum\limits_{\beta\in A}d_{\beta}\left[\beta=\alpha\right]
is the addend for
\beta=\alpha
, and this latter
addend equals
d_{\alpha}
). Hence, we can rewrite
(
55
) as

w=\sum\limits_{\alpha\in A}\left\langle w,u_{\alpha}\right\rangle f_{\alpha}.

This proves (
54
). Thus, Claim 1 is proved.

Claim 2:
Each
w\in F
satisfies

w=\sum\limits_{\alpha\in A}\left\langle w,v_{\alpha}\right\rangle g_{\alpha}.

(56)

Proof 4.7

(Proof of Claim 2.)
.

Let
w\in F
. Then,

\displaystyle\sum\limits_{\alpha\in A}\left\langle w,v_{\alpha}\right\rangle\underbrace{g_{\alpha}}_{\begin{subarray}{c}=\sum\limits_{\beta\in A}c_{\alpha,\beta}f_{\beta}\\
\text{(by
(\ref{eq.lem.dual-bases-crit.g-f}))}\end{subarray}}

\displaystyle=\sum\limits_{\alpha\in A}\left\langle w,v_{\alpha}\right\rangle\sum\limits_{\beta\in A}c_{\alpha,\beta}f_{\beta}=\sum\limits_{\beta\in A}\ \ \underbrace{\sum\limits_{\alpha\in A}c_{\alpha,\beta}\left\langle w,v_{\alpha}\right\rangle}_{=\left\langle w,\sum\limits_{\alpha\in A}c_{\alpha,\beta}v_{\alpha}\right\rangle}f_{\beta}

\displaystyle=\sum\limits_{\beta\in A}\left\langle w,\underbrace{\sum\limits_{\alpha\in A}c_{\alpha,\beta}v_{\alpha}}_{\begin{subarray}{c}=u_{\beta}\\
\text{(by
(\ref{eq.lem.dual-bases-crit.u-v}))}\end{subarray}}\right\rangle f_{\beta}=\sum\limits_{\beta\in A}\left\langle w,u_{\beta}\right\rangle f_{\beta}

\displaystyle=\sum\limits_{\alpha\in A}\left\langle w,u_{\alpha}\right\rangle f_{\alpha}=w\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \left(\text{by (\ref{pf.lem.dual-bases-crit.1})}\right).

This proves Claim 2.

Claim 3:
The family
\left(g_{\alpha}\right)_{\alpha\in A}
spans
the
\mathbf{k}
-module
F
.

Proof 4.8

(Proof of Claim 3.)
.

Let
w\in F
. Then, Claim 2 yields
w=\sum\limits_{\alpha\in A}\left\langle w,v_{\alpha}\right\rangle g_{\alpha}
. Hence,
w
belongs to
the span of the family
\left(g_{\alpha}\right)_{\alpha\in A}
.

Forget that we fixed
w
. We thus have proved that each
w\in F
belongs to
the span of the family
\left(g_{\alpha}\right)_{\alpha\in A}
. Hence,
this family
\left(g_{\alpha}\right)_{\alpha\in A}
spans the
\mathbf{k}
-module
F
. This proves Claim 3.

Claim 4:
The family
\left(g_{\alpha}\right)_{\alpha\in A}
is

\mathbf{k}
-linearly independent.

Proof 4.9

(Proof of Claim 4.)
.

Let
\left(m_{\alpha}\right)_{\alpha\in A}\in\mathbf{k}^{A}
be a family of scalars such that
\sum\limits_{\alpha\in A}m_{\alpha}g_{\alpha}=0
. We shall show that
m_{\alpha}=0
for each
\alpha\in A
.

Recall that
\left(v_{\alpha}\right)_{\alpha\in A}
is a basis of the

\mathbf{k}
-module
U
. Hence, we can define a
\mathbf{k}
-linear map

M:U\rightarrow\mathbf{k}
by requiring that

M\left(v_{\alpha}\right)=m_{\alpha}\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \text{for each
}\alpha\in A

(because a
\mathbf{k}
-linear map from a module can be uniquely defined by
specifying its values on a given basis of this module). Consider this map
M
.

Now, for each
\beta\in A
, we can apply the map
M
to both sides of
(
52
), and obtain

\displaystyle M\left(u_{\beta}\right)

\displaystyle=M\left(\sum\limits_{\alpha\in A}c_{\alpha,\beta}v_{\alpha}\right)=\sum\limits_{\alpha\in A}c_{\alpha,\beta}\underbrace{M\left(v_{\alpha}\right)}_{\begin{subarray}{c}=m_{\alpha}\\
\text{(by the definition of
}M\text{)}\end{subarray}}

\displaystyle=\sum\limits_{\alpha\in A}c_{\alpha,\beta}m_{\alpha}.

(57)

From
\sum\limits_{\alpha\in A}m_{\alpha}g_{\alpha}=0
, we obtain

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle=\sum\limits_{\alpha\in A}m_{\alpha}g_{\alpha}=\sum\limits_{\alpha\in A}m_{\alpha}\sum\limits_{\beta\in A}c_{\alpha,\beta}f_{\beta}\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \left(\text{by
(\ref{eq.lem.dual-bases-crit.g-f})}\right)

\displaystyle=\sum\limits_{\beta\in A}\underbrace{\left(\sum\limits_{\alpha\in A}c_{\alpha,\beta}m_{\alpha}\right)}_{\begin{subarray}{c}=M\left(u_{\beta}\right)\\
\text{(by
(\ref{pf.pf.lem.dual-bases-crit.5.short.3}))}\end{subarray}}f_{\beta}=\sum\limits_{\beta\in A}M\left(u_{\beta}\right)f_{\beta}.

In other words,

\sum\limits_{\beta\in A}M\left(u_{\beta}\right)f_{\beta}=0.

(58)

However, the family
\left(f_{\alpha}\right)_{\alpha\in A}
is a basis of
the
\mathbf{k}
-module
F
. In other words, the family
\left(f_{\beta}\right)_{\beta\in A}
is a basis of the
\mathbf{k}
-module
F
(here, we
have renamed the index
\alpha
as
\beta
). Hence, this family is

\mathbf{k}
-linearly independent. In other words, if
\left(n_{\beta}\right)_{\beta\in A}\in\mathbf{k}^{A}
is a family of scalars satisfying

\sum\limits_{\beta\in A}n_{\beta}f_{\beta}=0
, then
n_{\beta}=0
for each
\beta\in A
.

We can apply this to
n_{\beta}=M\left(u_{\beta}\right)
(since the family

\left(M\left(u_{\beta}\right)\right)_{\beta\in A}\in\mathbf{k}^{A}

satisfies
\sum\limits_{\beta\in A}M\left(u_{\beta}\right)f_{\beta}=0
(by
(
58
))). Thus, we conclude that

M\left(u_{\beta}\right)=0\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \text{for each }\beta\in A.

(59)

Recall that the family
\left(u_{\alpha}\right)_{\alpha\in A}
is a basis
of the
\mathbf{k}
-module
U
. In other words, the family
\left(u_{\beta}\right)_{\beta\in A}
is a basis of the
\mathbf{k}
-module
U
(here, we
have renamed the index
\alpha
as
\beta
). Hence, this family spans
U
.

Now, recall a general fact from linear algebra: If a
\mathbf{k}
-linear map

f:V\rightarrow W
sends a basis of its domain
V
to
0
(that is, if it
satisfies
f\left(v\right)=0
for all vectors
v
in a certain basis of

V
), then
f=0
as a map. The equality (
59
)
shows that the
\mathbf{k}
-linear map
M:U\rightarrow\mathbf{k}
sends a
basis of
U
to
0
(namely, the basis
\left(u_{\beta}\right)_{\beta\in A}
); therefore, the previous sentence shows that
M=0
as a map.

Now, let
\alpha\in A
be arbitrary. Then,
M\left(v_{\alpha}\right)=0

(since we have just shown that
M=0
). However, the definition of
M
yields

M\left(v_{\alpha}\right)=m_{\alpha}
. Thus,
m_{\alpha}=M\left(v_{\alpha}\right)=0
.

Forget that we fixed
\alpha
. We thus have shown that
m_{\alpha}=0
for each

\alpha\in A
.

Forget that we fixed
\left(m_{\alpha}\right)_{\alpha\in A}
. We thus have
proved that if
\left(m_{\alpha}\right)_{\alpha\in A}\in\mathbf{k}^{A}
is
a family of scalars such that
\sum\limits_{\alpha\in A}m_{\alpha}g_{\alpha}=0
, then

m_{\alpha}=0
for each
\alpha\in A
. In other words, the family
\left(g_{\alpha}\right)_{\alpha\in A}
is
\mathbf{k}
-linearly independent. This
proves Claim 4.

Now, we know that the family
\left(g_{\alpha}\right)_{\alpha\in A}
spans
the
\mathbf{k}
-module
F
(by Claim 3) and is
\mathbf{k}
-linearly
independent (by Claim 4). In other words, this family is a basis of
F
.

Next, we claim the following:

Claim 5:
We have
\left\langle g_{\beta},v_{\alpha}\right\rangle=\left[\beta=\alpha\right]
for all
\alpha,\beta\in A
.

Proof 4.10

(Proof of Claim 5.)
.

Fix
\beta\in A
.

The sum
\sum\limits_{\alpha\in A}\left[\beta=\alpha\right]g_{\alpha}
equals

g_{\beta}
, since all its addends except for the
\alpha=\beta
addend are

0
. Thus,

\sum\limits_{\alpha\in A}\left[\beta=\alpha\right]g_{\alpha}=g_{\beta}=\sum\limits_{\alpha\in A}\left\langle g_{\beta},v_{\alpha}\right\rangle g_{\alpha}

(60)

(by Claim 2, applied to
w=g_{\beta}
).

Now, Claim 4 says that the family
\left(g_{\alpha}\right)_{\alpha\in A}

is
\mathbf{k}
-linearly independent. In other words, if
\left(m_{\alpha}\right)_{\alpha\in A}\in\mathbf{k}^{A}
and
\left(n_{\alpha}\right)_{\alpha\in A}\in\mathbf{k}^{A}
are two families of scalars such that

\sum\limits_{\alpha\in A}m_{\alpha}g_{\alpha}=\sum\limits_{\alpha\in A}n_{\alpha}g_{\alpha}
, then
m_{\alpha}=n_{\alpha}
for each
\alpha\in A
. Applying this to

m_{\alpha}=\left\langle g_{\beta},v_{\alpha}\right\rangle
and
n_{\alpha}=\left[\beta=\alpha\right]
, we conclude that

\left\langle g_{\beta},v_{\alpha}\right\rangle=\left[\beta=\alpha\right]\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \text{for each }\alpha\in A

(since (
60
) yields
\sum\limits_{\alpha\in A}\left\langle g_{\beta},v_{\alpha}\right\rangle g_{\alpha}=\sum\limits_{\alpha\in A}\left[\beta=\alpha\right]g_{\alpha}
). This proves Claim 5.

Now, recall that
\left(g_{\alpha}\right)_{\alpha\in A}
is a basis of the

\mathbf{k}
-module
F
, whereas
\left(v_{\alpha}\right)_{\alpha\in A}

is a basis of the
\mathbf{k}
-module
U
. Furthermore, Claim 5 shows that the
bases
\left(v_{\alpha}\right)_{\alpha\in A}
and
\left(g_{\alpha}\right)_{\alpha\in A}
of
U
and
F
are mutually dual. This completes the
proof of Lemma
4.4
.

We are now ready to prove Proposition
4.3
:

Proof 4.11

(Proof of Proposition
4.3
.)
.

(b)
The scalar
r
is invertible (by Convention
4.1
).
Thus, we define an element

c_{\alpha,\beta}:=\begin{cases}\dfrac{1}{r^{\ell\left(\beta\right)}}\left(-1\right)^{\ell\left(\beta\right)-\ell\left(\alpha\right)},&\text{if }D\left(\alpha\right)\subseteq D\left(\beta\right);\\
0,&\text{otherwise}\end{cases}

of
\mathbf{k}
for every
\alpha,\beta\in\operatorname*{Comp}\nolimits_{n}
.

Now, let
\beta\in\operatorname*{Comp}\nolimits_{n}
. Then, Proposition

3.7
shows that

r^{\ell\left(\beta\right)}M_{\beta}=\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha\in\operatorname*{Comp}\nolimits_{n};\\
D\left(\alpha\right)\subseteq D\left(\beta\right)\end{subarray}}\left(-1\right)^{\ell\left(\beta\right)-\ell\left(\alpha\right)}\eta_{\alpha}^{\left(q\right)}.

(61)

Dividing both sides of this equality by
r^{\ell\left(\beta\right)}
, we obtain

M_{\beta}=\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha\in\operatorname*{Comp}\nolimits_{n};\\
D\left(\alpha\right)\subseteq D\left(\beta\right)\end{subarray}}\dfrac{1}{r^{\ell\left(\beta\right)}}\left(-1\right)^{\ell\left(\beta\right)-\ell\left(\alpha\right)}\eta_{\alpha}^{\left(q\right)}=\sum\limits_{\alpha\in\operatorname*{Comp}\nolimits_{n}}c_{\alpha,\beta}\eta_{\alpha}^{\left(q\right)}

(since our definition of
c_{\alpha,\beta}
ensures that all addends of the
sum
\sum\limits_{\alpha\in\operatorname*{Comp}\nolimits_{n}}c_{\alpha,\beta}\eta_{\alpha}^{\left(q\right)}
vanish except for the addends that satisfy

D\left(\alpha\right)\subseteq D\left(\beta\right)
, but these latter
addends are precisely the addends of
\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha\in\operatorname*{Comp}\nolimits_{n};\\
D\left(\alpha\right)\subseteq D\left(\beta\right)\end{subarray}}\dfrac{1}{r^{\ell\left(\beta\right)}}\left(-1\right)^{\ell\left(\beta\right)-\ell\left(\alpha\right)}\eta_{\alpha}^{\left(q\right)}
).

Forget that we fixed
\beta
. We thus have proved that

M_{\beta}=\sum\limits_{\alpha\in\operatorname*{Comp}\nolimits_{n}}c_{\alpha,\beta}\eta_{\alpha}^{\left(q\right)}

(62)

holds for each
\beta\in\operatorname*{Comp}\nolimits_{n}
.

Furthermore, for each
\alpha\in\operatorname*{Comp}\nolimits_{n}
, we have

\displaystyle\sum\limits_{\beta\in\operatorname*{Comp}\nolimits_{n}}c_{\alpha,\beta}H_{\beta}

\displaystyle=\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\beta\in\operatorname*{Comp}\nolimits_{n};\\
D\left(\alpha\right)\subseteq D\left(\beta\right)\end{subarray}}\dfrac{1}{r^{\ell\left(\beta\right)}}\left(-1\right)^{\ell\left(\beta\right)-\ell\left(\alpha\right)}H_{\beta}\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \left(\text{by the definition
of the }c_{\alpha,\beta}\right)

\displaystyle=\eta_{\alpha}^{\ast\left(q\right)}\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \left(\text{by
Definition \ref{def.etastar}}\right)

and thus

\eta_{\alpha}^{\ast\left(q\right)}=\sum\limits_{\beta\in\operatorname*{Comp}\nolimits_{n}}c_{\alpha,\beta}H_{\beta}.

(63)

Altogether, we now know the following:

•

The
\mathbf{k}
-modules
\operatorname*{NSym}\nolimits_{n}
and

\operatorname*{QSym}\nolimits_{n}
are free, and
\left\langle\cdot,\cdot\right\rangle:\operatorname*{NSym}\nolimits_{n}\times\operatorname*{QSym}\nolimits_{n}\rightarrow\mathbf{k}
is a
\mathbf{k}
-bilinear form.

•

The set
\operatorname*{Comp}\nolimits_{n}
is a finite set.

•

The family
\left(H_{\alpha}\right)_{\alpha\in\operatorname*{Comp}\nolimits_{n}}
is a basis of the
\mathbf{k}
-module
\operatorname*{NSym}\nolimits_{n}
, and the family
\left(\eta_{\alpha}^{\ast\left(q\right)}\right)_{\alpha\in\operatorname*{Comp}\nolimits_{n}}
is a further family
of elements of
\operatorname*{NSym}\nolimits_{n}
(since
(
63
) readily yields that
\eta_{\alpha}^{\ast\left(q\right)}\in\operatorname*{NSym}\nolimits_{n}
for each

\alpha\in\operatorname*{Comp}\nolimits_{n}
).

•

The families
\left(M_{\alpha}\right)_{\alpha\in\operatorname*{Comp}\nolimits_{n}}
and
\left(\eta_{\alpha}^{\left(q\right)}\right)_{\alpha\in\operatorname*{Comp}\nolimits_{n}}
are two bases of the

\mathbf{k}
-module
\operatorname*{QSym}\nolimits_{n}
(by Theorem

3.13

(b)
).

•

The basis
\left(M_{\alpha}\right)_{\alpha\in\operatorname*{Comp}\nolimits_{n}}
of
\operatorname*{QSym}\nolimits_{n}
is dual to the basis

\left(H_{\alpha}\right)_{\alpha\in\operatorname*{Comp}\nolimits_{n}}
of

\operatorname*{NSym}\nolimits_{n}
(because of (
49
)).

•

The elements
c_{\alpha,\beta}\in\mathbf{k}
are defined for all

\left(\alpha,\beta\right)\in\operatorname*{Comp}\nolimits_{n}\times\operatorname*{Comp}\nolimits_{n}
, and satisfy
(
62
) for each
\beta\in\operatorname*{Comp}\nolimits_{n}
and (
63
) for each
\alpha\in\operatorname*{Comp}\nolimits_{n}
.

Thus, Lemma
4.4
(applied to
F=\operatorname*{NSym}\nolimits_{n}
,
U=\operatorname*{QSym}\nolimits_{n}
,
A=\operatorname*{Comp}\nolimits_{n}
,
f_{\alpha}=H_{\alpha}
,
g_{\alpha}=\eta_{\alpha}^{\ast\left(q\right)}
,
u_{\alpha}=M_{\alpha}
and
v_{\alpha}=\eta_{\alpha}^{\left(q\right)}
) shows that the families
\left(\eta_{\alpha}^{\left(q\right)}\right)_{\alpha\in\operatorname*{Comp}\nolimits_{n}}
and
\left(\eta_{\alpha}^{\ast\left(q\right)}\right)_{\alpha\in\operatorname*{Comp}\nolimits_{n}}
are mutually dual bases of

\operatorname*{QSym}\nolimits_{n}
and
\operatorname*{NSym}\nolimits_{n}
,
respectively. In other words, the family
\left(\eta_{\alpha}^{\ast\left(q\right)}\right)_{\alpha\in\operatorname*{Comp}\nolimits_{n}}
is the
basis of
\operatorname*{NSym}\nolimits_{n}
dual to the basis
\left(\eta_{\alpha}^{\left(q\right)}\right)_{\alpha\in\operatorname*{Comp}\nolimits_{n}}
of
\operatorname*{QSym}\nolimits_{n}
. This proves
Proposition
4.3

(b)
.

(a)
This follows from part
(b)
by taking the direct sum over
all
n
.

4.3
The dual eta basis: product

We shall now study the multiplicative structure of the dual eta basis
\left(\eta_{\alpha}^{\ast\left(q\right)}\right)_{\alpha\in\operatorname*{Comp}}
. First, we introduce a notation for the simplest entries of this basis:

Definition 4.12
.

For each positive integer
n
, we let

\displaystyle\eta_{n}^{\ast\left(q\right)}:=

\displaystyle\ \eta_{\left(n\right)}^{\ast\left(q\right)}=\sum\limits_{\beta\in\operatorname*{Comp}\nolimits_{n}}\dfrac{1}{r^{\ell\left(\beta\right)}}\left(-1\right)^{\ell\left(\beta\right)-1}H_{\beta}

(64)

\displaystyle\in

\displaystyle\ \operatorname*{NSym}.

(The second equality sign here is easy to check.
12
12

12

Indeed, Definition
4.1
yields

\eta_{\left(n\right)}^{\ast\left(q\right)}=\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\beta\in\operatorname*{Comp}\nolimits_{n};\\
D\left(\left(n\right)\right)\subseteq D\left(\beta\right)\end{subarray}}\dfrac{1}{r^{\ell\left(\beta\right)}}\left(-1\right)^{\ell\left(\beta\right)-\ell\left(\left(n\right)\right)}H_{\beta}.

However, every
\beta\in\operatorname*{Comp}\nolimits_{n}
automatically
satisfies
D\left(\left(n\right)\right)\subseteq D\left(\beta\right)
(because
D\left(\left(n\right)\right)=\varnothing
).
Hence, the condition “
D\left(\left(n\right)\right)\subseteq D\left(\beta\right)
” under the summation sign
is superfluous. Also,
\ell\left(\left(n\right)\right)=1
. Thus, the
above equality simplifies to

\eta_{\left(n\right)}^{\ast\left(q\right)}=\sum\limits_{\beta\in\operatorname*{Comp}\nolimits_{n}}\dfrac{1}{r^{\ell\left(\beta\right)}}\left(-1\right)^{\ell\left(\beta\right)-1}H_{\beta}.

)

It turns out that we can easily express
\eta_{\alpha}^{\ast\left(q\right)}
for any composition
\alpha
using these
\eta_{n}^{\ast\left(q\right)}
:

Proposition 4.13
.

We have

\eta_{\alpha}^{\ast\left(q\right)}=\eta_{\alpha_{1}}^{\ast\left(q\right)}\eta_{\alpha_{2}}^{\ast\left(q\right)}\cdots\eta_{\alpha_{k}}^{\ast\left(q\right)}\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \text{for each composition
}\alpha=\left(\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2},\ldots,\alpha_{k}\right).

The main idea of the proof of Proposition
4.13
is to
recognize that if
n=\left|\alpha\right|
, then the compositions

\beta\in\operatorname*{Comp}\nolimits_{n}
satisfying
D\left(\alpha\right)\subseteq D\left(\beta\right)
are precisely the
compositions obtained from
\alpha
by breaking up each entry of
\alpha
into
pieces. A slicker way to formalize this proof proceeds using the notion of concatenation:

Definition 4.14
.

If
\alpha=\left(\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2},\ldots,\alpha_{\ell}\right)
and

\beta=\left(\beta_{1},\beta_{2},\ldots,\beta_{k}\right)
are two
compositions, then the composition
\alpha\beta
is defined by

\alpha\beta=\left(\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2},\ldots,\alpha_{\ell},\beta_{1},\beta_{2},\ldots,\beta_{k}\right).

This composition
\alpha\beta
is called the
concatenation
of
\alpha

and
\beta
. The operation of concatenation (sending any two compositions

\alpha
and
\beta
to
\alpha\beta
) is associative, and the empty
composition
\varnothing
is a neutral element for it; thus, the set of all
compositions is a monoid under this operation.

The following proposition is saying (in the jargon of combinatorial Hopf
algebras) that the basis
\left(\eta_{\alpha}^{\ast\left(q\right)}\right)_{\alpha\in\operatorname*{Comp}}
of
\operatorname*{NSym}
is multiplicative:

Proposition 4.15
.

Let
\alpha
and
\beta
be two compositions.
Then,

\eta_{\alpha}^{\ast\left(q\right)}\eta_{\beta}^{\ast\left(q\right)}=\eta_{\alpha\beta}^{\ast\left(q\right)}.

In order to prove this, we will use the comultiplication
\Delta:\operatorname*{QSym}\rightarrow\operatorname*{QSym}\otimes\operatorname*{QSym}
of the Hopf algebra
\operatorname*{QSym}
as well as
the duality between
\operatorname*{NSym}
and
\operatorname*{QSym}
:

Lemma 4.16
.

Let
f,g\in\operatorname*{NSym}
and
h\in\operatorname*{QSym}
be arbitrary. Let the tensor
\Delta\left(h\right)\in\operatorname*{QSym}\otimes\operatorname*{QSym}
be written in the form

\Delta\left(h\right)=\sum\limits_{i\in I}s_{i}\otimes t_{i}
, where
I
is a
finite set and where
s_{i},t_{i}\in\operatorname*{QSym}
for each
i\in I
.
Then,

\left\langle fg,h\right\rangle=\sum\limits_{i\in I}\left\langle f,s_{i}\right\rangle\left\langle g,t_{i}\right\rangle.

Proof 4.17
.

Recall that the
\mathbf{k}
-bilinear form
\left\langle\cdot,\cdot\right\rangle
identifies
\operatorname*{NSym}
with the graded dual

\operatorname*{QSym}\nolimits^{o}
as Hopf algebras. Thus, in particular, the
multiplication of
\operatorname*{NSym}
and the comultiplication of

\operatorname*{QSym}
are mutually adjoint with respect to this form. In
other words, if
f,g\in\operatorname*{NSym}
and
h\in\operatorname*{QSym}
,
then

\left\langle fg,h\right\rangle=\sum\limits_{\left(h\right)}\left\langle f,h_{\left(1\right)}\right\rangle\left\langle g,h_{\left(2\right)}\right\rangle,

where we are using the Sweedler notation
\sum\limits_{\left(h\right)}h_{\left(1\right)}\otimes h_{\left(2\right)}
for
\Delta\left(h\right)

(see, e.g.,
[
GriRei20
, (1.2.3)]
). Lemma
4.16
is just
restating this fact without using the Sweedler notation.

Proof 4.18

(Proof of Proposition
4.15
.)
.

This follows by dualization
from Theorem
3.28
. Here are the details:

Forget that we fixed
\alpha
and
\beta
. Proposition

4.3

(a)
shows that the families
\left(\eta_{\alpha}^{\ast\left(q\right)}\right)_{\alpha\in\operatorname*{Comp}}
and
\left(\eta_{\alpha}^{\left(q\right)}\right)_{\alpha\in\operatorname*{Comp}}
are mutually dual bases of
\operatorname*{NSym}

and
\operatorname*{QSym}
with respect to the bilinear form
\left\langle\cdot,\cdot\right\rangle
. This shows that

\left\langle\eta_{\lambda}^{\ast\left(q\right)},\eta_{\mu}^{\left(q\right)}\right\rangle=\left[\lambda=\mu\right]

(65)

for all
\lambda,\mu\in\operatorname*{Comp}
. But another consequence of this
duality is that the bilinear form
\left\langle\cdot,\cdot\right\rangle
is
nondegenerate (since only nondegenerate forms have dual bases), and that the
family
\left(\eta_{\alpha}^{\left(q\right)}\right)_{\alpha\in\operatorname*{Comp}}
is a basis of
\operatorname*{QSym}
. Hence, in
order to prove that two elements
f,g\in\operatorname*{NSym}
are equal, it
suffices to show that
\left\langle f,\eta_{\gamma}^{\left(q\right)}\right\rangle=\left\langle g,\eta_{\gamma}^{\left(q\right)}\right\rangle
holds for each
\gamma\in\operatorname*{Comp}
.

We shall use this strategy to prove
\eta_{\alpha}^{\ast\left(q\right)}\eta_{\beta}^{\ast\left(q\right)}=\eta_{\alpha\beta}^{\ast\left(q\right)}
for all
\alpha,\beta\in\operatorname*{Comp}
. Thus, we need to
show that
\left\langle\eta_{\alpha}^{\ast\left(q\right)}\eta_{\beta}^{\ast\left(q\right)},\eta_{\gamma}^{\left(q\right)}\right\rangle=\left\langle\eta_{\alpha\beta}^{\ast\left(q\right)},\eta_{\gamma}^{\left(q\right)}\right\rangle
holds for all
\alpha,\beta,\gamma\in\operatorname*{Comp}
.

To show this, we fix
\alpha,\beta,\gamma\in\operatorname*{Comp}
. Theorem

3.28
(with the letters
\alpha,\beta,\gamma
replaced by

\gamma,\varphi,\psi
) says that

\Delta\left(\eta_{\gamma}^{\left(q\right)}\right)=\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\varphi,\psi\in\operatorname*{Comp};\\
\gamma=\varphi\psi\end{subarray}}\eta_{\varphi}^{\left(q\right)}\otimes\eta_{\psi}^{\left(q\right)}.

Hence, Lemma
4.16
(applied to
f=\eta_{\alpha}^{\ast\left(q\right)}
and
g=\eta_{\beta}^{\ast\left(q\right)}
and

h=\eta_{\gamma}^{\left(q\right)}
and

I=\left\{\left(\varphi,\psi\right)\in\operatorname*{Comp}\times\operatorname*{Comp}\ \mid\ \gamma=\varphi\psi\right\}

and
