s_{\left(\varphi,\psi\right)}=\eta_{\varphi}^{\left(q\right)}

and
t_{\left(\varphi,\psi\right)}=\eta_{\psi}^{\left(q\right)}
)
yields

\displaystyle\left\langle\eta_{\alpha}^{\ast\left(q\right)}\eta_{\beta}^{\ast\left(q\right)},\eta_{\gamma}^{\left(q\right)}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\varphi,\psi\in\operatorname*{Comp};\\
\gamma=\varphi\psi\end{subarray}}\underbrace{\left\langle\eta_{\alpha}^{\ast\left(q\right)},\eta_{\varphi}^{\left(q\right)}\right\rangle}_{\begin{subarray}{c}=\left[\alpha=\varphi\right]\\
\text{(by (\ref{pf.prop.etastar.concat2.duality}))}\end{subarray}}\underbrace{\left\langle\eta_{\beta}^{\ast\left(q\right)},\eta_{\psi}^{\left(q\right)}\right\rangle}_{\begin{subarray}{c}=\left[\beta=\psi\right]\\
\text{(by (\ref{pf.prop.etastar.concat2.duality}))}\end{subarray}}=\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\varphi,\psi\in\operatorname*{Comp};\\
\gamma=\varphi\psi\end{subarray}}\underbrace{\left[\alpha=\varphi\right]\cdot\left[\beta=\psi\right]}_{=\left[\alpha=\varphi\text{ and }\beta=\psi\right]}

\displaystyle=\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\varphi,\psi\in\operatorname*{Comp};\\
\gamma=\varphi\psi\end{subarray}}\left[\alpha=\varphi\text{ and }\beta=\psi\right].

(66)

The sum on the right hand side of this equality has at most one nonzero addend
– namely the addend for
\varphi=\alpha
and
\psi=\beta
, if this addend
exists. Of course, this addend exists if and only if
\gamma=\alpha\beta
, and
equals
1
in this case. Thus, the sum equals
1
if
\gamma=\alpha\beta
, and
otherwise equals
0
. In other words, this sum equals the truth value
\left[\gamma=\alpha\beta\right]
. Hence, we can rewrite
(
66
) as

\left\langle\eta_{\alpha}^{\ast\left(q\right)}\eta_{\beta}^{\ast\left(q\right)},\eta_{\gamma}^{\left(q\right)}\right\rangle=\left[\gamma=\alpha\beta\right].

Comparing this with

\displaystyle\left\langle\eta_{\alpha\beta}^{\ast\left(q\right)},\eta_{\gamma}^{\left(q\right)}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\left[\alpha\beta=\gamma\right]\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \left(\text{by (\ref{pf.prop.etastar.concat2.duality})}\right)

\displaystyle=\left[\gamma=\alpha\beta\right],

we obtain
\left\langle\eta_{\alpha}^{\ast\left(q\right)}\eta_{\beta}^{\ast\left(q\right)},\eta_{\gamma}^{\left(q\right)}\right\rangle=\left\langle\eta_{\alpha\beta}^{\ast\left(q\right)},\eta_{\gamma}^{\left(q\right)}\right\rangle
.

Forget that we fixed
\gamma
. We thus have shown that
\left\langle\eta_{\alpha}^{\ast\left(q\right)}\eta_{\beta}^{\ast\left(q\right)},\eta_{\gamma}^{\left(q\right)}\right\rangle=\left\langle\eta_{\alpha\beta}^{\ast\left(q\right)},\eta_{\gamma}^{\left(q\right)}\right\rangle
for each
\gamma\in\operatorname*{Comp}
. Since
\left(\eta_{\gamma}^{\left(q\right)}\right)_{\gamma\in\operatorname*{Comp}}

is a basis of the
\mathbf{k}
-module
\operatorname*{QSym}
, and since the
bilinear form
\left\langle\cdot,\cdot\right\rangle
is nondegenerate, we
thus conclude that
\eta_{\alpha}^{\ast\left(q\right)}\eta_{\beta}^{\ast\left(q\right)}=\eta_{\alpha\beta}^{\ast\left(q\right)}
. This
proves Proposition
4.15
.

Corollary 4.19
.

Let
\beta_{1},\beta_{2},\ldots,\beta_{k}
be
finitely many compositions. Then,

\eta_{\beta_{1}}^{\ast\left(q\right)}\eta_{\beta_{2}}^{\ast\left(q\right)}\cdots\eta_{\beta_{k}}^{\ast\left(q\right)}=\eta_{\beta_{1}\beta_{2}\cdots\beta_{k}}^{\ast\left(q\right)}.

Proof 4.20
.

This follows by induction on
k
using Proposition
4.15
.
(The base case,
k=0
, follows from
\eta_{\left({}\right)}^{\ast\left(q\right)}=1
.)

Proof 4.21

(Proof of Proposition
4.13
.)
.

Let
\alpha=\left(\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2},\ldots,\alpha_{k}\right)
be a composition. Then, applying
Corollary
4.19
to the
1
-element compositions
\beta_{i}=\left(\alpha_{i}\right)
, we obtain

\eta_{\left(\alpha_{1}\right)}^{\ast\left(q\right)}\eta_{\left(\alpha_{2}\right)}^{\ast\left(q\right)}\cdots\eta_{\left(\alpha_{k}\right)}^{\ast\left(q\right)}=\eta_{\left(\alpha_{1}\right)\left(\alpha_{2}\right)\cdots\left(\alpha_{k}\right)}^{\ast\left(q\right)}=\eta_{\alpha}^{\ast\left(q\right)}

(since the concatenation
\left(\alpha_{1}\right)\left(\alpha_{2}\right)\cdots\left(\alpha_{k}\right)
equals
\left(\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2},\ldots,\alpha_{k}\right)=\alpha
). Thus,

\eta_{\alpha}^{\ast\left(q\right)}=\eta_{\left(\alpha_{1}\right)}^{\ast\left(q\right)}\eta_{\left(\alpha_{2}\right)}^{\ast\left(q\right)}\cdots\eta_{\left(\alpha_{k}\right)}^{\ast\left(q\right)}=\eta_{\alpha_{1}}^{\ast\left(q\right)}\eta_{\alpha_{2}}^{\ast\left(q\right)}\cdots\eta_{\alpha_{k}}^{\ast\left(q\right)}

(since
\eta_{\left(n\right)}^{\ast\left(q\right)}=\eta_{n}^{\ast\left(q\right)}
for each
n>0
). This proves Proposition

4.13
.

4.4
The dual eta basis: generating function

We shall now work in the ring
\operatorname*{NSym}\left[\left[t\right]\right]
of formal power series in the indeterminate
t
over the ring

\operatorname*{NSym}
. This ring
\operatorname*{NSym}\left[\left[t\right]\right]
is noncommutative (since
\operatorname*{NSym}
is), but
the indeterminate
t
commutes with all its elements.

We furthermore define two special series in this ring:

Definition 4.22
.

Define the formal power series

H\left(t\right):=\sum\limits_{n\geq 0}H_{n}t^{n}\in\operatorname*{NSym}\left[\left[t\right]\right]

and

G\left(t\right):=\sum\limits_{n\geq 1}\eta_{n}^{\ast\left(q\right)}t^{n}\in\operatorname*{NSym}\left[\left[t\right]\right].

Now, it is easy to see the following:

Proposition 4.23
.

We have

G\left(t\right)=1-\dfrac{1}{1+\dfrac{H\left(t\right)-1}{r}}=\dfrac{H\left(t\right)-1}{H\left(t\right)+q}.

Proof 4.24
.

The power series
H\left(t\right)
has constant term
H_{0}=1
. Thus, the
power series
H\left(t\right)-1
has constant term
0
. Hence, the power
series
1+\dfrac{H\left(t\right)-1}{r}
has constant term
1+\dfrac{0}{r}=1
, and thus is invertible (since every formal power series with constant
term
1
is invertible). The fraction
\dfrac{1}{1+\dfrac{H\left(t\right)-1}{r}}
is thus well-defined.

If
u\in\operatorname*{NSym}\left[\left[t\right]\right]
is a formal
power series with constant term
0
, then the geometric series formula yields

\dfrac{1}{1-u}=\sum\limits_{k\geq 0}u^{k}=\underbrace{u^{0}}_{=1}+\sum\limits_{k\geq 1}u^{k}=1+\sum\limits_{k\geq 1}u^{k},

so that

\sum\limits_{k\geq 1}u^{k}=\dfrac{1}{1-u}-1.

(67)

We shall use this result in a somewhat modified form: If
u\in\operatorname*{NSym}\left[\left[t\right]\right]
is a formal power
series with constant term
0
, then
\dfrac{-u}{r}
is also a formal power
series with constant term
0
, and we have

\displaystyle\sum\limits_{k\geq 1}\dfrac{1}{r^{k}}\underbrace{\left(-1\right)^{k-1}}_{=-\left(-1\right)^{k}}u^{k}

\displaystyle=-\sum\limits_{k\geq 1}\underbrace{\dfrac{1}{r^{k}}\left(-1\right)^{k}u^{k}}_{=\left(\dfrac{-u}{r}\right)^{k}}=-\underbrace{\sum\limits_{k\geq 1}\left(\dfrac{-u}{r}\right)^{k}}_{\begin{subarray}{c}=\dfrac{1}{1-\dfrac{-u}{r}}-1\\
\text{(by (\ref{pf.prop.Gt-through-Ht.sumuk=}),}\\
\text{applied to }\dfrac{-u}{r}\text{ instead of }u\text{)}\end{subarray}}

\displaystyle=-\left(\dfrac{1}{1-\dfrac{-u}{r}}-1\right)=1-\dfrac{1}{1-\dfrac{-u}{r}}

\displaystyle=1-\dfrac{1}{1+\dfrac{u}{r}}.

(68)

We have
H\left(t\right)=\sum\limits_{n\geq 0}H_{n}t^{n}=\underbrace{H_{0}}_{=1}\underbrace{t^{0}}_{=1}+\sum\limits_{n\geq 1}H_{n}t^{n}=1+\sum\limits_{n\geq 1}H_{n}t^{n}
, so that

\sum\limits_{n\geq 1}H_{n}t^{n}=H\left(t\right)-1.

The definition of
G\left(t\right)
yields

\displaystyle G\left(t\right)

\displaystyle=\sum\limits_{n\geq 1}\eta_{n}^{\ast\left(q\right)}t^{n}

\displaystyle=\sum\limits_{n\geq 1}\left(\sum\limits_{\beta\in\operatorname*{Comp}\nolimits_{n}}\dfrac{1}{r^{\ell\left(\beta\right)}}\left(-1\right)^{\ell\left(\beta\right)-1}H_{\beta}\right)t^{n}\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \left(\text{by
(\ref{eq.def.etastarn.eq})}\right)

\displaystyle=\sum\limits_{n\geq 1}\ \ \sum\limits_{\beta\in\operatorname*{Comp}\nolimits_{n}}\dfrac{1}{r^{\ell\left(\beta\right)}}\left(-1\right)^{\ell\left(\beta\right)-1}H_{\beta}\underbrace{t^{n}}_{\begin{subarray}{c}=t^{\left|\beta\right|}\\
\text{(since }\beta\in\operatorname*{Comp}\nolimits_{n}\text{)}\end{subarray}}

\displaystyle=\underbrace{\sum\limits_{n\geq 1}\ \ \sum\limits_{\beta\in\operatorname*{Comp}\nolimits_{n}}}_{\begin{subarray}{c}=\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\beta\in\operatorname*{Comp};\\
\left|\beta\right|\geq 1\end{subarray}}\\
=\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\beta\in\operatorname*{Comp};\\
\ell\left(\beta\right)\geq 1\end{subarray}}\\
\text{(because for a
composition }\beta\text{,}\\
\text{the condition \textquotedblleft}\left|\beta\right|\geq 1\text{\textquotedblright\ is}\\
\text{equivalent to
\textquotedblleft}\ell\left(\beta\right)\geq 1\text{\textquotedblright)}\end{subarray}}\dfrac{1}{r^{\ell\left(\beta\right)}}\left(-1\right)^{\ell\left(\beta\right)-1}H_{\beta}t^{\left|\beta\right|}

\displaystyle=\underbrace{\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\beta\in\operatorname*{Comp};\\
\ell\left(\beta\right)\geq 1\end{subarray}}}_{=\sum\limits_{k\geq 1}\ \ \sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\beta\in\operatorname*{Comp};\\
\ell\left(\beta\right)=k\end{subarray}}}\dfrac{1}{r^{\ell\left(\beta\right)}}\left(-1\right)^{\ell\left(\beta\right)-1}H_{\beta}t^{\left|\beta\right|}

\displaystyle=\sum\limits_{k\geq 1}\ \ \sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\beta\in\operatorname*{Comp};\\
\ell\left(\beta\right)=k\end{subarray}}\underbrace{\dfrac{1}{r^{\ell\left(\beta\right)}}\left(-1\right)^{\ell\left(\beta\right)-1}}_{\begin{subarray}{c}=\dfrac{1}{r^{k}}\left(-1\right)^{k-1}\\
\text{(since }\ell\left(\beta\right)=k\text{)}\end{subarray}}H_{\beta}t^{\left|\beta\right|}

\displaystyle=\sum\limits_{k\geq 1}\dfrac{1}{r^{k}}\left(-1\right)^{k-1}\underbrace{\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\beta\in\operatorname*{Comp};\\
\ell\left(\beta\right)=k\end{subarray}}H_{\beta}t^{\left|\beta\right|}}_{\begin{subarray}{c}=\sum\limits_{\left(n_{1},n_{2},\ldots,n_{k}\right)\in\operatorname*{Comp}}H_{\left(n_{1},n_{2},\ldots,n_{k}\right)}t^{n_{1}+n_{2}+\cdots+n_{k}}\\
\text{(here,
we have renamed the summation index }\beta\text{ as }\left(n_{1},n_{2},\ldots,n_{k}\right)\text{)}\end{subarray}}

\displaystyle=\sum\limits_{k\geq 1}\dfrac{1}{r^{k}}\left(-1\right)^{k-1}\underbrace{\sum\limits_{\left(n_{1},n_{2},\ldots,n_{k}\right)\in\operatorname*{Comp}}}_{=\sum\limits_{n_{1},n_{2},\ldots,n_{k}\geq 1}}\underbrace{H_{\left(n_{1},n_{2},\ldots,n_{k}\right)}t^{n_{1}+n_{2}+\cdots+n_{k}}}_{\begin{subarray}{c}=\left(H_{n_{1}}H_{n_{2}}\cdots H_{n_{k}}\right)\left(t^{n_{1}}t^{n_{2}}\cdots t^{n_{k}}\right)\\
=\left(H_{n_{1}}t^{n_{1}}\right)\left(H_{n_{2}}t^{n_{2}}\right)\cdots\left(H_{n_{k}}t^{n_{k}}\right)\end{subarray}}

\displaystyle=\sum\limits_{k\geq 1}\dfrac{1}{r^{k}}\left(-1\right)^{k-1}\underbrace{\sum\limits_{n_{1},n_{2},\ldots,n_{k}\geq 1}\left(H_{n_{1}}t^{n_{1}}\right)\left(H_{n_{2}}t^{n_{2}}\right)\cdots\left(H_{n_{k}}t^{n_{k}}\right)}_{\begin{subarray}{c}=\left(\sum\limits_{n\geq 1}H_{n}t^{n}\right)^{k}\\
\text{(by the
product rule)}\end{subarray}}

\displaystyle=\sum\limits_{k\geq 1}\dfrac{1}{r^{k}}\left(-1\right)^{k-1}\left(\underbrace{\sum\limits_{n\geq 1}H_{n}t^{n}}_{=H\left(t\right)-1}\right)^{k}=\sum\limits_{k\geq 1}\dfrac{1}{r^{k}}\left(-1\right)^{k-1}\left(H\left(t\right)-1\right)^{k}

\displaystyle=1-\dfrac{1}{1+\dfrac{H\left(t\right)-1}{r}}\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \left(\text{by (\ref{pf.prop.Gt-through-Ht.sum-uk=}), applied to }u=H\left(t\right)-1\right)

\displaystyle=1-\dfrac{r}{H\left(t\right)+r-1}=\dfrac{H\left(t\right)-1}{H\left(t\right)+r-1}=\dfrac{H\left(t\right)-1}{H\left(t\right)+q}

(since
r-1=q
(because
r=q+1
)). This proves Proposition

4.23
.

Proposition 4.25
.

Let
k\in\mathbb{N}
. Then,

G\left(t\right)^{k}=\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\beta\in\operatorname*{Comp};\\
\ell\left(\beta\right)=k\end{subarray}}\eta_{\beta}^{\ast\left(q\right)}t^{\left|\beta\right|}.

(69)

Proof 4.26
.

From
G\left(t\right)=\sum\limits_{n\geq 1}\eta_{n}^{\ast\left(q\right)}t^{n}
, we obtain

\displaystyle G\left(t\right)^{k}

\displaystyle=\left(\sum\limits_{n\geq 1}\eta_{n}^{\ast\left(q\right)}t^{n}\right)^{k}=\sum\limits_{n_{1},n_{2},\ldots,n_{k}\geq 1}\underbrace{\left(\eta_{n_{1}}^{\ast\left(q\right)}t^{n_{1}}\right)\left(\eta_{n_{2}}^{\ast\left(q\right)}t^{n_{2}}\right)\cdots\left(\eta_{n_{k}}^{\ast\left(q\right)}t^{n_{k}}\right)}_{=\eta_{n_{1}}^{\ast\left(q\right)}\eta_{n_{2}}^{\ast\left(q\right)}\cdots\eta_{n_{k}}^{\ast\left(q\right)}t^{n_{1}+n_{2}+\cdots+n_{k}}}

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \left(\text{by the product
rule}\right)

\displaystyle=\sum\limits_{n_{1},n_{2},\ldots,n_{k}\geq 1}\eta_{n_{1}}^{\ast\left(q\right)}\eta_{n_{2}}^{\ast\left(q\right)}\cdots\eta_{n_{k}}^{\ast\left(q\right)}t^{n_{1}+n_{2}+\cdots+n_{k}}

\displaystyle=\sum\limits_{\beta=\left(\beta_{1},\beta_{2},\ldots,\beta_{k}\right)\in\operatorname*{Comp}}\ \ \underbrace{\eta_{\beta_{1}}^{\ast\left(q\right)}\eta_{\beta_{2}}^{\ast\left(q\right)}\cdots\eta_{\beta_{k}}^{\ast\left(q\right)}}_{\begin{subarray}{c}=\eta_{\beta}^{\ast\left(q\right)}\\
\text{(by Proposition \ref{prop.etastar.mult})}\end{subarray}}\ \ \underbrace{t^{\beta_{1}+\beta_{2}+\cdots+\beta_{k}}}_{=t^{\left|\beta\right|}}

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \left(\text{here, we have renamed
}n_{1},n_{2},\ldots,n_{k}\text{ as }\beta_{1},\beta_{2},\ldots,\beta_{k}\right)

\displaystyle=\underbrace{\sum\limits_{\beta=\left(\beta_{1},\beta_{2},\ldots,\beta_{k}\right)\in\operatorname*{Comp}}}_{=\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\beta\in\operatorname*{Comp};\\
\ell\left(\beta\right)=k\end{subarray}}}\eta_{\beta}^{\ast\left(q\right)}t^{\left|\beta\right|}=\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\beta\in\operatorname*{Comp};\\
\ell\left(\beta\right)=k\end{subarray}}\eta_{\beta}^{\ast\left(q\right)}t^{\left|\beta\right|}.

This proves Proposition
4.25
.

4.5
The dual eta basis: coproduct

Consider the comultiplication
\Delta:\operatorname*{NSym}\rightarrow\operatorname*{NSym}\otimes\operatorname*{NSym}
of the Hopf algebra

\operatorname*{NSym}
. We again recall the Iverson bracket notation
(Convention
3.2
).

Theorem 4.27
.

For any positive integer
n
, we have

\Delta\left(\eta_{n}^{\ast\left(q\right)}\right)=\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\beta,\gamma\in\operatorname*{Comp};\\
\left|\beta\right|+\left|\gamma\right|=n;\\
\left|\ell\left(\beta\right)-\ell\left(\gamma\right)\right|\leq 1\end{subarray}}\left(-q\right)^{\max\left\{\ell\left(\beta\right),\ell\left(\gamma\right)\right\}-1}\left(q-1\right)^{\left[\ell\left(\beta\right)=\ell\left(\gamma\right)\right]}\eta_{\beta}^{\ast\left(q\right)}\otimes\eta_{\gamma}^{\ast\left(q\right)}.

Example 4.28
.

For
n=2
, there are exactly three pairs
\left(\beta,\gamma\right)
of
compositions
\beta,\gamma\in\operatorname*{Comp}
satisfying
\left|\beta\right|+\left|\gamma\right|=n
and
\left|\ell\left(\beta\right)-\ell\left(\gamma\right)\right|\leq 1
: namely, the
pairs
\left(\varnothing,\left(2\right)\right)
,
\left(\left(1\right),\left(1\right)\right)
and
\left(\left(2\right),\varnothing\right)
. Hence, Theorem
4.27
(applied to

n=2
) yields

\displaystyle\Delta\left(\eta_{2}^{\ast\left(q\right)}\right)

\displaystyle=\left(-q\right)^{1-1}\left(q-1\right)^{0}\eta_{\varnothing}^{\ast\left(q\right)}\otimes\eta_{\left(2\right)}^{\ast\left(q\right)}+\left(-q\right)^{1-1}\left(q-1\right)^{1}\eta_{\left(1\right)}^{\ast\left(q\right)}\otimes\eta_{\left(1\right)}^{\ast\left(q\right)}

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ +\left(-q\right)^{1-1}\left(q-1\right)^{0}\eta_{\left(2\right)}^{\ast\left(q\right)}\otimes\eta_{\varnothing}^{\ast\left(q\right)}

\displaystyle=\eta_{\varnothing}^{\ast\left(q\right)}\otimes\eta_{\left(2\right)}^{\ast\left(q\right)}+\left(q-1\right)\eta_{\left(1\right)}^{\ast\left(q\right)}\otimes\eta_{\left(1\right)}^{\ast\left(q\right)}+\eta_{\left(2\right)}^{\ast\left(q\right)}\otimes\eta_{\varnothing}^{\ast\left(q\right)}

\displaystyle=1\otimes\eta_{2}^{\ast\left(q\right)}+\left(q-1\right)\eta_{1}^{\ast\left(q\right)}\otimes\eta_{1}^{\ast\left(q\right)}+\eta_{2}^{\ast\left(q\right)}\otimes 1

(since
\eta_{\left(2\right)}^{\ast\left(q\right)}=\eta_{2}
and

\eta_{\left(1\right)}^{\ast\left(q\right)}=\eta_{1}
and

\eta_{\varnothing}^{\ast\left(q\right)}=1
).

Similar computations show that

\Delta\left(\eta_{1}^{\ast\left(q\right)}\right)=1\otimes\eta_{1}^{\ast\left(q\right)}+\eta_{1}^{\ast\left(q\right)}\otimes 1

and

\displaystyle\Delta\left(\eta_{3}^{\ast\left(q\right)}\right)

\displaystyle=1\otimes\eta_{3}^{\ast\left(q\right)}+\left(q-1\right)\eta_{1}^{\ast\left(q\right)}\otimes\eta_{2}^{\ast\left(q\right)}-q\eta_{1}^{\ast\left(q\right)}\otimes\left(\eta_{1}^{\ast\left(q\right)}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ -q\left(\eta_{1}^{\ast\left(q\right)}\right)^{2}\otimes\eta_{1}^{\ast\left(q\right)}+\left(q-1\right)\eta_{2}^{\ast\left(q\right)}\otimes\eta_{1}^{\ast\left(q\right)}+\eta_{3}^{\ast\left(q\right)}\otimes 1

(since Proposition
4.13
yields
\eta_{\left(1,1\right)}^{\ast\left(q\right)}=\left(\eta_{1}^{\ast\left(q\right)}\right)^{2}
).

Our proof of Theorem
4.27
will use the following general
fact from abstract algebra:

Lemma 4.29
.

Let
A
and
B
be any two
\mathbf{k}
-algebras. Then, there
is a canonical
\mathbf{k}
-algebra homomorphism

\displaystyle\iota:A\left[\left[t\right]\right]\otimes_{\mathbf{k}\left[\left[t\right]\right]}B\left[\left[t\right]\right]

\displaystyle\rightarrow\left(A\otimes B\right)\left[\left[t\right]\right],

\displaystyle\left(\sum\limits_{i\in\mathbb{N}}a_{i}t^{i}\right)\otimes\left(\sum\limits_{j\in\mathbb{N}}b_{j}t^{j}\right)

\displaystyle\mapsto\left(\sum\limits_{i\in\mathbb{N}}\left(a_{i}\otimes 1\right)t^{i}\right)\left(\sum\limits_{j\in\mathbb{N}}\left(1\otimes b_{j}\right)t^{j}\right).

Proof 4.30

(Proof of Lemma
4.29
(sketched).)
.

To construct the map
\iota
, we
need
A
and
B
only to be
\mathbf{k}
-modules, not
\mathbf{k}
-algebras.
The well-definedness follows easily from the fact that
\sum\limits_{k\in\mathbb{N}}\left(\lambda_{k}1\otimes 1\right)t^{k}=\sum\limits_{k\in\mathbb{N}}\left(1\otimes\lambda_{k}1\right)t^{k}
for any formal power series
\sum\limits_{k\in\mathbb{N}}\lambda_{k}t^{k}\in\mathbf{k}\left[\left[t\right]\right]
. Finally, to prove that
\iota
is a
\mathbf{k}
-algebra
homomorphism, observe that any power series of the form
\sum\limits_{i\in\mathbb{N}}\left(a_{i}\otimes 1\right)t^{i}\in\left(A\otimes B\right)\left[\left[t\right]\right]
commutes with any power series of the form

\sum\limits_{j\in\mathbb{N}}\left(1\otimes b_{j}\right)t^{j}\in\left(A\otimes B\right)\left[\left[t\right]\right]
. The details are left to the reader.

Proof 4.31

(Proof of Theorem
4.27
.)
.

The comultiplication
\Delta:\operatorname*{NSym}\rightarrow\operatorname*{NSym}\otimes\operatorname*{NSym}
is a
\mathbf{k}
-algebra homomorphism (since

\operatorname*{NSym}
is a
\mathbf{k}
-bialgebra), and thus induces a

\mathbf{k}\left[\left[t\right]\right]
-algebra homomorphism

\Delta_{t}:\operatorname*{NSym}\left[\left[t\right]\right]\rightarrow\left(\operatorname*{NSym}\otimes\operatorname*{NSym}\right)\left[\left[t\right]\right]

that sends each formal power series
\sum\limits_{i\in\mathbb{N}}a_{i}t^{i}
to

\sum\limits_{i\in\mathbb{N}}\Delta\left(a_{i}\right)t^{i}
. This
\Delta_{t}
is
a
\mathbf{k}
-algebra homomorphism as well (since any
\mathbf{k}\left[\left[t\right]\right]
-algebra homomorphism is a
\mathbf{k}
-algebra homomorphism).

Furthermore, Lemma
4.29
shows that there is a canonical
\mathbf{k}
-algebra homomorphism

\displaystyle\iota:\operatorname*{NSym}\left[\left[t\right]\right]\otimes_{\mathbf{k}\left[\left[t\right]\right]}\operatorname*{NSym}\left[\left[t\right]\right]

\displaystyle\rightarrow\left(\operatorname*{NSym}\otimes\operatorname*{NSym}\right)\left[\left[t\right]\right],

\displaystyle\left(\sum\limits_{i\in\mathbb{N}}a_{i}t^{i}\right)\otimes\left(\sum\limits_{j\in\mathbb{N}}b_{j}t^{j}\right)

\displaystyle\mapsto\left(\sum\limits_{i\in\mathbb{N}}\left(a_{i}\otimes 1\right)t^{i}\right)\left(\sum\limits_{j\in\mathbb{N}}\left(1\otimes b_{j}\right)t^{j}\right).

Unlike some authors, we will not treat
\iota
as an embedding, but we will
often use the fact that
\iota
is a
\mathbf{k}
-algebra homomorphism.

We recall that
\mathbf{k}
-algebra homomorphisms are always ring
homomorphisms, and thus respect quotients. That is, if
f:A\rightarrow B
is a

\mathbf{k}
-algebra homomorphism, and if
a_{1}
and
a_{2}
are two elements
of
A
such that
a_{2}
is invertible in
A
, then
f\left(a_{2}\right)

is again invertible and we have
f\left(\dfrac{a_{1}}{a_{2}}\right)=\dfrac{f\left(a_{1}\right)}{f\left(a_{2}\right)}
. We will use this
fact without saying a few times.

For the sake of brevity, we define the shorthands

\mathbf{G}:=G\left(t\right)\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \text{and}\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \mathbf{H}:=H\left(t\right).

Proposition
4.23
says that
G\left(t\right)=\dfrac{H\left(t\right)-1}{H\left(t\right)+q}
. Using our
abbreviations
\mathbf{G}
and
\mathbf{H}
, we can rewrite this as

\mathbf{G}=\dfrac{\mathbf{H}-1}{\mathbf{H}+q}.

(70)

Hence,

\Delta_{t}\left(\mathbf{G}\right)=\Delta_{t}\left(\dfrac{\mathbf{H}-1}{\mathbf{H}+q}\right)=\dfrac{\Delta_{t}\left(\mathbf{H}\right)-1}{\Delta_{t}\left(\mathbf{H}\right)+q}

(71)

(since
\Delta_{t}
is a
\mathbf{k}
-algebra homomorphism).

Next, we observe the following:

Claim 1:
We have

\Delta_{t}\left(\mathbf{H}\right)=\iota\left(\mathbf{H}\otimes\mathbf{H}\right).

(72)

[
Proof of Claim 1:
From
\mathbf{H}=H\left(t\right)=\sum\limits_{n\in\mathbb{N}}H_{n}t^{n}=\sum\limits_{i\in\mathbb{N}}H_{i}t^{i}
and

\mathbf{H}=H\left(t\right)=\sum\limits_{n\in\mathbb{N}}H_{n}t^{n}=\sum\limits_{j\in\mathbb{N}}H_{j}t^{j}
, we obtain

\mathbf{H}\otimes\mathbf{H}=\left(\sum\limits_{i\in\mathbb{N}}H_{i}t^{i}\right)\otimes\left(\sum\limits_{j\in\mathbb{N}}H_{j}t^{j}\right).

By the definition of
\iota
, this entails

\displaystyle\iota\left(\mathbf{H}\otimes\mathbf{H}\right)

\displaystyle=\left(\sum\limits_{i\in\mathbb{N}}\left(H_{i}\otimes 1\right)t^{i}\right)\left(\sum\limits_{j\in\mathbb{N}}\left(1\otimes H_{j}\right)t^{j}\right)

\displaystyle=\sum\limits_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\left(\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}i,j\in\mathbb{N};\\
i+j=n\end{subarray}}\left(H_{i}\otimes 1\right)\left(1\otimes H_{j}\right)\right)t^{n}

(73)

(by the definition of the product of two power series). On the other hand,

\mathbf{H}=\sum\limits_{n\in\mathbb{N}}H_{n}t^{n}
. Thus,

\Delta_{t}\left(\mathbf{H}\right)=\Delta_{t}\left(\sum\limits_{n\in\mathbb{N}}H_{n}t^{n}\right)=\sum\limits_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\Delta\left(H_{n}\right)t^{n}

(74)

(by the definition of
\Delta_{t}
). However, for each
n\in\mathbb{N}
, we
have

\displaystyle\Delta\left(H_{n}\right)

\displaystyle=\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}i,j\in\mathbb{N};\\
i+j=n\end{subarray}}\ \ \underbrace{H_{i}\otimes H_{j}}_{=\left(H_{i}\otimes 1\right)\left(1\otimes H_{j}\right)}\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \left(\text{by
\cite[cite]{[\@@bibref{}{GriRei}{}{}, (5.4.2)]}}\right)

\displaystyle=\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}i,j\in\mathbb{N};\\
i+j=n\end{subarray}}\left(H_{i}\otimes 1\right)\left(1\otimes H_{j}\right).

Hence, the right hand sides of the equalities
(
74
) and
(
73
) are equal. Therefore, so are their
left hand sides. In other words, we have
\Delta_{t}\left(\mathbf{H}\right)=\iota\left(\mathbf{H}\otimes\mathbf{H}\right)
. This proves Claim 1.]

Define four elements
h_{1}
,
h_{2}
,
g_{1}
and
g_{2}
of
\left(\operatorname*{NSym}\otimes\operatorname*{NSym}\right)\left[\left[t\right]\right]
by

\displaystyle h_{1}

\displaystyle=\iota\left(\mathbf{H}\otimes 1\right)\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \text{and}\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ h_{2}=\iota\left(1\otimes\mathbf{H}\right)\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \text{and}

\displaystyle g_{1}

\displaystyle=\iota\left(\mathbf{G}\otimes 1\right)\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \text{and}\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ g_{2}=\iota\left(1\otimes\mathbf{G}\right).

The equality (
72
) becomes

\displaystyle\Delta_{t}\left(\mathbf{H}\right)

\displaystyle=\iota\left(\underbrace{\mathbf{H}\otimes\mathbf{H}}_{=\left(\mathbf{H}\otimes 1\right)\left(1\otimes\mathbf{H}\right)}\right)=\iota\left(\left(\mathbf{H}\otimes 1\right)\left(1\otimes\mathbf{H}\right)\right)

\displaystyle=\underbrace{\iota\left(\mathbf{H}\otimes 1\right)}_{=h_{1}}\cdot\underbrace{\iota\left(1\otimes\mathbf{H}\right)}_{=h_{2}}\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \left(\text{since }\iota\text{ is a ring homomorphism}\right)

\displaystyle=h_{1}h_{2}

(75)

and

\displaystyle\Delta_{t}\left(\mathbf{H}\right)

\displaystyle=\iota\left(\underbrace{\mathbf{H}\otimes\mathbf{H}}_{=\left(1\otimes\mathbf{H}\right)\left(\mathbf{H}\otimes 1\right)}\right)=\iota\left(\left(1\otimes\mathbf{H}\right)\left(\mathbf{H}\otimes 1\right)\right)

\displaystyle=\underbrace{\iota\left(1\otimes\mathbf{H}\right)}_{=h_{2}}\cdot\underbrace{\iota\left(\mathbf{H}\otimes 1\right)}_{=h_{1}}\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \left(\text{since }\iota\text{ is a ring homomorphism}\right)

\displaystyle=h_{2}h_{1}.

Comparing these two equalities, we obtain
h_{1}h_{2}=h_{2}h_{1}
. In other
words, the elements
h_{1}
and
h_{2}
commute. The elements
\dfrac{1}{h_{1}+q}
,
\dfrac{1}{h_{2}+q}
and
\dfrac{1}{h_{1}h_{2}+q}
(which are
easily seen to be well-defined
13
13

13

Proof.
The power series

h_{1}=\iota\left(\mathbf{H}\otimes 1\right)
has constant term
1
(since

\mathbf{H}=\sum\limits_{n\in\mathbb{N}}H_{n}t^{n}
entails
\iota\left(\mathbf{H}\otimes 1\right)=\sum\limits_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\left(H_{n}\otimes 1\right)t^{n}
, and this latter series has constant term
\underbrace{H_{0}}_{=1}\otimes 1=1\otimes 1=1
). Thus, the power series
h_{1}+q
has constant
term
1+q=q+1=r
, which is invertible (by Convention
4.1
). Thus,
the power series
h_{1}+q
itself is invertible (since a formal power series
whose constant term is invertible must itself be invertible). In other words,

\dfrac{1}{h_{1}+q}
is well-defined. Similarly,
\dfrac{1}{h_{2}+q}
and

\dfrac{1}{h_{1}h_{2}+q}
are well-defined.
) are rational functions in these
commuting elements
h_{1}
and
h_{2}
, and therefore also commute with them
(and with each other). Thus, the five elements
h_{1}
,
h_{2}
,
\dfrac{1}{h_{1}+q}
,
\dfrac{1}{h_{2}+q}
and
\dfrac{1}{h_{1}h_{2}+q}

\mathbf{k}
-subalgebra of
\left(\operatorname*{NSym}\otimes\operatorname*{NSym}\right)\left[\left[t\right]\right]
. Let

\mathbf{k}
-subalgebra by
\mathcal{H}
. Clearly,
the elements
h_{1}+q
,
h_{2}+q
and
h_{1}h_{2}+q
are invertible in

\mathcal{H}
. Also, the element
q+1=r
is invertible in
\mathcal{H}
(since

\mathbf{k}
already).

From (
70
), we obtain

g_{1}=\dfrac{h_{1}-1}{h_{1}+q}\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \text{and}\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ g_{2}=\dfrac{h_{2}-1}{h_{2}+q}

(76)

(since
\iota
is a
\mathbf{k}
-algebra homomorphism
14
14

14

Let us give
some more details here: Let
\iota_{1}
be the map

\displaystyle\operatorname*{NSym}\left[\left[t\right]\right]

\displaystyle\rightarrow\operatorname*{NSym}\left[\left[t\right]\right]\otimes_{\mathbf{k}\left[\left[t\right]\right]}\operatorname*{NSym}\left[\left[t\right]\right],

\displaystyle z

\displaystyle\mapsto z\otimes 1.

Then,
\iota_{1}
is a
\mathbf{k}
-algebra homomorphism. Since
\iota

\mathbf{k}

\iota\circ\iota_{1}

\mathbf{k}

, so that

. Now, applying the map

), we obtain

\left(\iota\circ\iota_{1}\right)\left(\mathbf{G}\right)=\left(\iota\circ\iota_{1}\right)\left(\dfrac{\mathbf{H}-1}{\mathbf{H}+q}\right)=\dfrac{\left(\iota\circ\iota_{1}\right)\left(\mathbf{H}\right)-1}{\left(\iota\circ\iota_{1}\right)\left(\mathbf{H}\right)+q}

(since

. Similarly, we can show

g_{2}=\dfrac{h_{2}-1}{h_{2}+q}
. Thus, (

)
is proved.
).

Thus, the elements
g_{1}
and

also belong to the commutative

\mathbf{k}

\mathcal{H}
generated by
h_{1}
,

\dfrac{1}{h_{1}+q}
,

and
\dfrac{1}{h_{1}h_{2}+q}
.
Straightforward computations using (

) (and the

) show that

1+qg_{1}g_{2}=\dfrac{\left(q+1\right)\left(h_{1}h_{2}+q\right)}{\left(h_{1}+q\right)\left(h_{2}+q\right)}.

Thus,
1+qg_{1}g_{2}
is invertible in
\mathcal{H}
(since

,

,

and

are invertible in
\mathcal{H}
).

From (
75
), we obtain

\displaystyle\dfrac{\Delta_{t}\left(\mathbf{H}\right)-1}{\Delta_{t}\left(\mathbf{H}\right)+q}

\displaystyle=\dfrac{h_{1}h_{2}-1}{h_{1}h_{2}+q}

\displaystyle=\dfrac{g_{1}+g_{2}+\left(q-1\right)g_{1}g_{2}}{1+qg_{1}g_{2}}.

(77)

(Indeed, the last equality sign can easily be verified by straightforward

\mathbf{k}

\mathcal{H}
, using the

). For example, you can plug

) into
\dfrac{g_{1}+g_{2}+\left(q-1\right)g_{1}g_{2}}{1+qg_{1}g_{2}}
and simplify; the result will be
\dfrac{h_{1}h_{2}-1}{h_{1}h_{2}+q}
.)

From
g_{1}=\iota\left(\mathbf{G}\otimes 1\right)
and
g_{2}=\iota\left(1\otimes\mathbf{G}\right)
, we obtain

\displaystyle g_{1}g_{2}

\displaystyle=\iota\left(\mathbf{G}\otimes 1\right)\cdot\iota\left(1\otimes\mathbf{G}\right)=\iota\left(\underbrace{\left(\mathbf{G}\otimes 1\right)\cdot\left(1\otimes\mathbf{G}\right)}_{=\mathbf{G}\otimes\mathbf{G}}\right)

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \left(\text{since }\iota\text{ is
a }\mathbf{k}\text{-algebra homomorphism}\right)

\displaystyle=\iota\left(\mathbf{G}\otimes\mathbf{G}\right).

(78)

Note that the formal power series
qg_{1}g_{2}
has constant term
0
(since
it is easy to see that both
g_{1}
and
g_{2}
have constant term
0
).

Now, (
71
) becomes

\displaystyle\Delta_{t}\left(\mathbf{G}\right)

\displaystyle=\dfrac{\Delta_{t}\left(\mathbf{H}\right)-1}{\Delta_{t}\left(\mathbf{H}\right)+q}=\dfrac{g_{1}+g_{2}+\left(q-1\right)g_{1}g_{2}}{1+qg_{1}g_{2}}\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \left(\text{by (\ref{pf.thm.Deltaetastara.abab2})}\right)

\displaystyle=\underbrace{\dfrac{1}{1+qg_{1}g_{2}}}_{\begin{subarray}{c}=\sum\limits_{i\in\mathbb{N}}\left(-qg_{1}g_{2}\right)^{i}\\
\text{(by the geometric series formula)}\end{subarray}}\cdot\left(g_{1}+g_{2}+\left(q-1\right)g_{1}g_{2}\right)

\displaystyle=\sum\limits_{i\in\mathbb{N}}\underbrace{\left(-qg_{1}g_{2}\right)^{i}}_{=\left(-q\right)^{i}\left(g_{1}g_{2}\right)^{i}}\left(g_{1}+g_{2}+\left(q-1\right)g_{1}g_{2}\right)

\displaystyle=\sum\limits_{i\in\mathbb{N}}\left(-q\right)^{i}\left(\underbrace{g_{1}g_{2}}_{\begin{subarray}{c}=\iota\left(\mathbf{G}\otimes\mathbf{G}\right)\\
\text{(by (\ref{pf.thm.Deltaetastara.g1g2o}))}\end{subarray}}\right)^{i}\left(\underbrace{g_{1}}_{=\iota\left(\mathbf{G}\otimes 1\right)}+\underbrace{g_{2}}_{=\iota\left(1\otimes\mathbf{G}\right)}+\left(q-1\right)\underbrace{g_{1}g_{2}}_{\begin{subarray}{c}=\iota\left(\mathbf{G}\otimes\mathbf{G}\right)\\
\text{(by (\ref{pf.thm.Deltaetastara.g1g2o}))}\end{subarray}}\right)

\displaystyle=\sum\limits_{i\in\mathbb{N}}\left(-q\right)^{i}\underbrace{\left(\iota\left(\mathbf{G}\otimes\mathbf{G}\right)\right)^{i}\cdot\left(\iota\left(\mathbf{G}\otimes 1\right)+\iota\left(1\otimes\mathbf{G}\right)+\left(q-1\right)\iota\left(\mathbf{G}\otimes\mathbf{G}\right)\right)}_{\begin{subarray}{c}=\iota\left(\left(\mathbf{G}\otimes\mathbf{G}\right)^{i}\left(\mathbf{G}\otimes 1+1\otimes\mathbf{G}+\left(q-1\right)\mathbf{G}\otimes\mathbf{G}\right)\right)\\
\text{(since }\iota\text{ is a
}\mathbf{k}\text{-algebra homomorphism)}\end{subarray}}

\displaystyle=\sum\limits_{i\in\mathbb{N}}\left(-q\right)^{i}\iota\left(\left(\mathbf{G}\otimes\mathbf{G}\right)^{i}\left(\mathbf{G}\otimes 1+1\otimes\mathbf{G}+\left(q-1\right)\mathbf{G}\otimes\mathbf{G}\right)\right).

In order to simplify the right hand side, we need two further claims:

Claim 2:
Let
u,v\in\mathbb{N}
. Then,

\iota\left(\mathbf{G}^{u}\otimes\mathbf{G}^{v}\right)=\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\beta,\gamma\in\operatorname*{Comp};\\
\ell\left(\beta\right)=u\text{ and }\ell\left(\gamma\right)=v\end{subarray}}\left(\eta_{\beta}^{\ast\left(q\right)}\otimes\eta_{\gamma}^{\ast\left(q\right)}\right)t^{\left|\beta\right|+\left|\gamma\right|}.

[
Proof of Claim 2:
From
\mathbf{G}=G\left(t\right)
, we obtain

\displaystyle\mathbf{G}^{u}

\displaystyle=G\left(t\right)^{u}=\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\beta\in\operatorname*{Comp};\\
\ell\left(\beta\right)=u\end{subarray}}\eta_{\beta}^{\ast\left(q\right)}t^{\left|\beta\right|}\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \left(\text{by (\ref{pf.thm.Deltaetastara.Gtk}), applied
to }k=u\right)

\displaystyle=\sum\limits_{i\in\mathbb{N}}\ \ \sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\beta\in\operatorname*{Comp};\\
\ell\left(\beta\right)=u;\\
\left|\beta\right|=i\end{subarray}}\eta_{\beta}^{\ast\left(q\right)}\underbrace{t^{\left|\beta\right|}}_{\begin{subarray}{c}=t^{i}\\
\text{(since }\left|\beta\right|=i\text{)}\end{subarray}}

\displaystyle=\sum\limits_{i\in\mathbb{N}}\left(\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\beta\in\operatorname*{Comp};\\
\ell\left(\beta\right)=u;\\
\left|\beta\right|=i\end{subarray}}\eta_{\beta}^{\ast\left(q\right)}\right)t^{i}.

(79)

The same argument (applied to
v
instead of
u
) shows that

\mathbf{G}^{v}=\sum\limits_{i\in\mathbb{N}}\left(\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\beta\in\operatorname*{Comp};\\

\left|\beta\right|=i\end{subarray}}\eta_{\beta}^{\ast\left(q\right)}\right)t^{i}=\sum\limits_{j\in\mathbb{N}}\left(\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\gamma\in\operatorname*{Comp};\\

(here, we have renamed the summation indices

j
and

\mathbf{G}^{u}\otimes\mathbf{G}^{v}=\left(\sum\limits_{i\in\mathbb{N}}\left(\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\beta\in\operatorname*{Comp};\\

(note that the two outer sums here are infinite, so that we cannot simply

to both sides of this equality, we obtain

\displaystyle\iota\left(\mathbf{G}^{u}\otimes\mathbf{G}^{v}\right)

\displaystyle=\iota\left(\left(\sum\limits_{i\in\mathbb{N}}\left(\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\beta\in\operatorname*{Comp};\\
\ell\left(\beta\right)=u;\\

\ell\left(\gamma\right)=v;\\
\left|\gamma\right|=j\end{subarray}}\eta_{\gamma}^{\ast\left(q\right)}\right)t^{j}\right)\right)

\displaystyle=\left(\sum\limits_{i\in\mathbb{N}}\left(\left(\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\beta\in\operatorname*{Comp};\\
\ell\left(\beta\right)=u;\\
\left|\beta\right|=i\end{subarray}}\eta_{\beta}^{\ast\left(q\right)}\right)\otimes 1\right)t^{i}\right)\left(\sum\limits_{j\in\mathbb{N}}\left(1\otimes\left(\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\gamma\in\operatorname*{Comp};\\
\ell\left(\gamma\right)=v;\\
\left|\gamma\right|=j\end{subarray}}\eta_{\gamma}^{\ast\left(q\right)}\right)\right)t^{j}\right)

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \left(\text{by the definition of
}\iota\right)

\displaystyle=\sum\limits_{i\in\mathbb{N}}\ \ \sum\limits_{j\in\mathbb{N}}\underbrace{\left(\left(\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\beta\in\operatorname*{Comp};\\
\ell\left(\beta\right)=u;\\
\left|\beta\right|=i\end{subarray}}\eta_{\beta}^{\ast\left(q\right)}\right)\otimes 1\right)\left(1\otimes\left(\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\gamma\in\operatorname*{Comp};\\
\ell\left(\gamma\right)=v;\\
\left|\gamma\right|=j\end{subarray}}\eta_{\gamma}^{\ast\left(q\right)}\right)\right)}_{\begin{subarray}{c}=\left(\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\beta\in\operatorname*{Comp};\\
\ell\left(\beta\right)=u;\\
\left|\beta\right|=i\end{subarray}}\eta_{\beta}^{\ast\left(q\right)}\right)\otimes\left(\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\gamma\in\operatorname*{Comp};\\
\ell\left(\gamma\right)=v;\\
\left|\gamma\right|=j\end{subarray}}\eta_{\gamma}^{\ast\left(q\right)}\right)\\
=\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\beta,\gamma\in\operatorname*{Comp};\\
\ell\left(\beta\right)=u\text{ and }\ell\left(\gamma\right)=v;\\
\left|\beta\right|=i\text{ and }\left|\gamma\right|=j\end{subarray}}\eta_{\beta}^{\ast\left(q\right)}\otimes\eta_{\gamma}^{\ast\left(q\right)}\\
\text{(here, we expanded the tensor product, }\\
\text{since both sums
involved are finite)}\end{subarray}}\underbrace{t^{i}t^{j}}_{=t^{i+j}}

\displaystyle=\sum\limits_{i\in\mathbb{N}}\ \ \sum\limits_{j\in\mathbb{N}}\ \ \sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\beta,\gamma\in\operatorname*{Comp};\\
\ell\left(\beta\right)=u\text{ and }\ell\left(\gamma\right)=v;\\
\left|\beta\right|=i\text{ and

\text{(since }i=\left|\beta\right|\text{ and }j=\left|\gamma\right|\text{)}\end{subarray}}

\displaystyle=\underbrace{\sum\limits_{i\in\mathbb{N}}\ \ \sum\limits_{j\in\mathbb{N}}\ \ \sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\beta,\gamma\in\operatorname*{Comp};\\
\ell\left(\beta\right)=u\text{ and }\ell\left(\gamma\right)=v;\\
\left|\beta\right|=i\text{ and }\left|\gamma\right|=j\end{subarray}}}_{=\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\beta,\gamma\in\operatorname*{Comp};\\
\ell\left(\beta\right)=u\text{ and }\ell\left(\gamma\right)=v\end{subarray}}}\left(\eta_{\beta}^{\ast\left(q\right)}\otimes\eta_{\gamma}^{\ast\left(q\right)}\right)t^{\left|\beta\right|+\left|\gamma\right|}

\displaystyle=\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\beta,\gamma\in\operatorname*{Comp};\\
\ell\left(\beta\right)=u\text{ and }\ell\left(\gamma\right)=v\end{subarray}}\left(\eta_{\beta}^{\ast\left(q\right)}\otimes\eta_{\gamma}^{\ast\left(q\right)}\right)t^{\left|\beta\right|+\left|\gamma\right|}.

This proves Claim 2.]

Claim 3:
Let
i\in\mathbb{N}
. Then,

\displaystyle\iota\left(\left(\mathbf{G}\otimes\mathbf{G}\right)^{i}\left(\mathbf{G}\otimes 1+1\otimes\mathbf{G}+\left(q-1\right)\mathbf{G}\otimes\mathbf{G}\right)\right)

\displaystyle=\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\beta,\gamma\in\operatorname*{Comp};\\
\left|\ell\left(\beta\right)-\ell\left(\gamma\right)\right|\leq 1;\\
\max\left\{\ell\left(\beta\right),\ell\left(\gamma\right)\right\}=i+1\end{subarray}}\left(q-1\right)^{\left[\ell\left(\beta\right)=\ell\left(\gamma\right)\right]}\left(\eta_{\beta}^{\ast\left(q\right)}\otimes\eta_{\gamma}^{\ast\left(q\right)}\right)t^{\left|\beta\right|+\left|\gamma\right|}.

[
Proof of Claim 3:
We have

\displaystyle\underbrace{\left(\mathbf{G}\otimes\mathbf{G}\right)^{i}}_{=\mathbf{G}^{i}\otimes\mathbf{G}^{i}}\left(\mathbf{G}\otimes 1+1\otimes\mathbf{G}+\left(q-1\right)\mathbf{G}\otimes\mathbf{G}\right)

\displaystyle=\left(\mathbf{G}^{i}\otimes\mathbf{G}^{i}\right)\left(\mathbf{G}\otimes 1+1\otimes\mathbf{G}+\left(q-1\right)\mathbf{G}\otimes\mathbf{G}\right)

\displaystyle=\mathbf{G}^{i+1}\otimes\mathbf{G}^{i}+\mathbf{G}^{i}\otimes\mathbf{G}^{i+1}+\left(q-1\right)\mathbf{G}^{i+1}\otimes\mathbf{G}^{i+1}.

Applying the map
\iota
to both sides of this equality, we obtain

\displaystyle\iota\left(\left(\mathbf{G}\otimes\mathbf{G}\right)^{i}\left(\mathbf{G}\otimes 1+1\otimes\mathbf{G}+\left(q-1\right)\mathbf{G}\otimes\mathbf{G}\right)\right)

\displaystyle=\iota\left(\mathbf{G}^{i+1}\otimes\mathbf{G}^{i}+\mathbf{G}^{i}\otimes\mathbf{G}^{i+1}+\left(q-1\right)\mathbf{G}^{i+1}\otimes\mathbf{G}^{i+1}\right)

\displaystyle=\underbrace{\iota\left(\mathbf{G}^{i+1}\otimes\mathbf{G}^{i}\right)}_{\begin{subarray}{c}=\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\beta,\gamma\in\operatorname*{Comp};\\
\ell\left(\beta\right)=i+1\text{ and }\ell\left(\gamma\right)=i\end{subarray}}\left(\eta_{\beta}^{\ast\left(q\right)}\otimes\eta_{\gamma}^{\ast\left(q\right)}\right)t^{\left|\beta\right|+\left|\gamma\right|}\\
\text{(by Claim 2)}\end{subarray}}+\underbrace{\iota\left(\mathbf{G}^{i}\otimes\mathbf{G}^{i+1}\right)}_{\begin{subarray}{c}=\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\beta,\gamma\in\operatorname*{Comp};\\
\ell\left(\beta\right)=i\text{ and }\ell\left(\gamma\right)=i+1\end{subarray}}\left(\eta_{\beta}^{\ast\left(q\right)}\otimes\eta_{\gamma}^{\ast\left(q\right)}\right)t^{\left|\beta\right|+\left|\gamma\right|}\\
\text{(by
Claim 2)}\end{subarray}}

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ +\left(q-1\right)\underbrace{\iota\left(\mathbf{G}^{i+1}\otimes\mathbf{G}^{i+1}\right)}_{\begin{subarray}{c}=\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\beta,\gamma\in\operatorname*{Comp};\\
\ell\left(\beta\right)=i+1\text{ and }\ell\left(\gamma\right)=i+1\end{subarray}}\left(\eta_{\beta}^{\ast\left(q\right)}\otimes\eta_{\gamma}^{\ast\left(q\right)}\right)t^{\left|\beta\right|+\left|\gamma\right|}\\
\text{(by
Claim 2)}\end{subarray}}

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \left(\text{since }\iota\text{ is
a }\mathbf{k}\text{-algebra homomorphism}\right)

\displaystyle=\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\beta,\gamma\in\operatorname*{Comp};\\
\ell\left(\beta\right)=i+1\text{ and }\ell\left(\gamma\right)=i\end{subarray}}\left(\eta_{\beta}^{\ast\left(q\right)}\otimes\eta_{\gamma}^{\ast\left(q\right)}\right)t^{\left|\beta\right|+\left|\gamma\right|}+\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\beta,\gamma\in\operatorname*{Comp};\\
\ell\left(\beta\right)=i\text{ and }\ell\left(\gamma\right)=i+1\end{subarray}}\left(\eta_{\beta}^{\ast\left(q\right)}\otimes\eta_{\gamma}^{\ast\left(q\right)}\right)t^{\left|\beta\right|+\left|\gamma\right|}

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ +\left(q-1\right)\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\beta,\gamma\in\operatorname*{Comp};\\
\ell\left(\beta\right)=i+1\text{ and }\ell\left(\gamma\right)=i+1\end{subarray}}\left(\eta_{\beta}^{\ast\left(q\right)}\otimes\eta_{\gamma}^{\ast\left(q\right)}\right)t^{\left|\beta\right|+\left|\gamma\right|}.

(80)

On the other hand, let us observe that two integers
u
and
v
satisfy the
two conditions

\left|u-v\right|\leq 1\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \text{and}\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \max\left\{u,v\right\}=i+1

if and only if they satisfy one of the three mutually exclusive conditions

\displaystyle\left(u=i+1\text{ and }v=i\right),

\displaystyle\left(u=i\text{ and }v=i+1\right)\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \text{and}

\displaystyle\left(u=i+1\text{ and }v=i+1\right).

Hence, two compositions
\beta,\gamma\in\operatorname*{Comp}
satisfy the two
conditions

\left|\ell\left(\beta\right)-\ell\left(\gamma\right)\right|\leq 1\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \text{and}\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \max\left\{\ell\left(\beta\right),\ell\left(\gamma\right)\right\}=i+1

if and only if they satisfy one of the three mutually exclusive conditions

\displaystyle\left(\ell\left(\beta\right)=i+1\text{ and }\ell\left(\gamma\right)=i\right),

\displaystyle\left(\ell\left(\beta\right)=i\text{ and }\ell\left(\gamma\right)=i+1\right)\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \text{and}

\displaystyle\left(\ell\left(\beta\right)=i+1\text{ and }\ell\left(\gamma\right)=i+1\right).

Hence,

\displaystyle\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\beta,\gamma\in\operatorname*{Comp};\\
\left|\ell\left(\beta\right)-\ell\left(\gamma\right)\right|\leq 1;\\
\max\left\{\ell\left(\beta\right),\ell\left(\gamma\right)\right\}=i+1\end{subarray}}\left(q-1\right)^{\left[\ell\left(\beta\right)=\ell\left(\gamma\right)\right]}\left(\eta_{\beta}^{\ast\left(q\right)}\otimes\eta_{\gamma}^{\ast\left(q\right)}\right)t^{\left|\beta\right|+\left|\gamma\right|}

\displaystyle=\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\beta,\gamma\in\operatorname*{Comp};\\
\ell\left(\beta\right)=i+1\text{ and }\ell\left(\gamma\right)=i\end{subarray}}\ \ \underbrace{\left(q-1\right)^{\left[\ell\left(\beta\right)=\ell\left(\gamma\right)\right]}}_{\begin{subarray}{c}=1\\
\text{(since }\ell\left(\beta\right)=i+1>i=\ell\left(\gamma\right)\\
\text{and thus
}\left[\ell\left(\beta\right)=\ell\left(\gamma\right)\right]=0\text{)}\end{subarray}}\left(\eta_{\beta}^{\ast\left(q\right)}\otimes\eta_{\gamma}^{\ast\left(q\right)}\right)t^{\left|\beta\right|+\left|\gamma\right|}

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ +\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\beta,\gamma\in\operatorname*{Comp};\\
\ell\left(\beta\right)=i\text{ and }\ell\left(\gamma\right)=i+1\end{subarray}}\ \ \underbrace{\left(q-1\right)^{\left[\ell\left(\beta\right)=\ell\left(\gamma\right)\right]}}_{\begin{subarray}{c}=1\\
\text{(since }\ell\left(\beta\right)=i<i+1=\ell\left(\gamma\right)\\
\text{and thus
}\left[\ell\left(\beta\right)=\ell\left(\gamma\right)\right]=0\text{)}\end{subarray}}\left(\eta_{\beta}^{\ast\left(q\right)}\otimes\eta_{\gamma}^{\ast\left(q\right)}\right)t^{\left|\beta\right|+\left|\gamma\right|}

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ +\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\beta,\gamma\in\operatorname*{Comp};\\
\ell\left(\beta\right)=i+1\text{ and }\ell\left(\gamma\right)=i+1\end{subarray}}\ \ \underbrace{\left(q-1\right)^{\left[\ell\left(\beta\right)=\ell\left(\gamma\right)\right]}}_{\begin{subarray}{c}=q-1\\
\text{(since }\ell\left(\beta\right)=i+1=\ell\left(\gamma\right)\\
\text{and thus
}\left[\ell\left(\beta\right)=\ell\left(\gamma\right)\right]=1\text{)}\end{subarray}}\left(\eta_{\beta}^{\ast\left(q\right)}\otimes\eta_{\gamma}^{\ast\left(q\right)}\right)t^{\left|\beta\right|+\left|\gamma\right|}

\displaystyle=\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\beta,\gamma\in\operatorname*{Comp};\\
\ell\left(\beta\right)=i+1\text{ and }\ell\left(\gamma\right)=i\end{subarray}}\left(\eta_{\beta}^{\ast\left(q\right)}\otimes\eta_{\gamma}^{\ast\left(q\right)}\right)t^{\left|\beta\right|+\left|\gamma\right|}+\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\beta,\gamma\in\operatorname*{Comp};\\
\ell\left(\beta\right)=i\text{ and }\ell\left(\gamma\right)=i+1\end{subarray}}\left(\eta_{\beta}^{\ast\left(q\right)}\otimes\eta_{\gamma}^{\ast\left(q\right)}\right)t^{\left|\beta\right|+\left|\gamma\right|}

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ +\left(q-1\right)\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\beta,\gamma\in\operatorname*{Comp};\\
\ell\left(\beta\right)=i+1\text{ and }\ell\left(\gamma\right)=i+1\end{subarray}}\left(\eta_{\beta}^{\ast\left(q\right)}\otimes\eta_{\gamma}^{\ast\left(q\right)}\right)t^{\left|\beta\right|+\left|\gamma\right|}.

Comparing this with (
80
), we obtain

\displaystyle\iota\left(\left(\mathbf{G}\otimes\mathbf{G}\right)^{i}\left(\mathbf{G}\otimes 1+1\otimes\mathbf{G}+\left(q-1\right)\mathbf{G}\otimes\mathbf{G}\right)\right)

\displaystyle=\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\beta,\gamma\in\operatorname*{Comp};\\
\left|\ell\left(\beta\right)-\ell\left(\gamma\right)\right|\leq 1;\\
\max\left\{\ell\left(\beta\right),\ell\left(\gamma\right)\right\}=i+1\end{subarray}}\left(q-1\right)^{\left[\ell\left(\beta\right)=\ell\left(\gamma\right)\right]}\left(\eta_{\beta}^{\ast\left(q\right)}\otimes\eta_{\gamma}^{\ast\left(q\right)}\right)t^{\left|\beta\right|+\left|\gamma\right|}.

This proves Claim 3.]

Now, we can finish our computation of
\Delta_{t}\left(\mathbf{G}\right)
:
As we know,

\displaystyle\Delta_{t}\left(\mathbf{G}\right)

\displaystyle=\sum\limits_{i\in\mathbb{N}}\left(-q\right)^{i}\underbrace{\iota\left(\left(\mathbf{G}\otimes\mathbf{G}\right)^{i}\left(\mathbf{G}\otimes 1+1\otimes\mathbf{G}+\left(q-1\right)\mathbf{G}\otimes\mathbf{G}\right)\right)}_{\begin{subarray}{c}=\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\beta,\gamma\in\operatorname*{Comp};\\
\left|\ell\left(\beta\right)-\ell\left(\gamma\right)\right|\leq 1;\\
\max\left\{\ell\left(\beta\right),\ell\left(\gamma\right)\right\}=i+1\end{subarray}}\left(q-1\right)^{\left[\ell\left(\beta\right)=\ell\left(\gamma\right)\right]}\left(\eta_{\beta}^{\ast\left(q\right)}\otimes\eta_{\gamma}^{\ast\left(q\right)}\right)t^{\left|\beta\right|+\left|\gamma\right|}\\
\text{(by Claim 3)}\end{subarray}}

\displaystyle=\sum\limits_{i\in\mathbb{N}}\left(-q\right)^{i}\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\beta,\gamma\in\operatorname*{Comp};\\
\left|\ell\left(\beta\right)-\ell\left(\gamma\right)\right|\leq 1;\\
\max\left\{\ell\left(\beta\right),\ell\left(\gamma\right)\right\}=i+1\end{subarray}}\left(q-1\right)^{\left[\ell\left(\beta\right)=\ell\left(\gamma\right)\right]}\left(\eta_{\beta}^{\ast\left(q\right)}\otimes\eta_{\gamma}^{\ast\left(q\right)}\right)t^{\left|\beta\right|+\left|\gamma\right|}

\displaystyle=\sum\limits_{j>0}\left(-q\right)^{j-1}\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\beta,\gamma\in\operatorname*{Comp};\\
\left|\ell\left(\beta\right)-\ell\left(\gamma\right)\right|\leq 1;\\
\max\left\{\ell\left(\beta\right),\ell\left(\gamma\right)\right\}=j\end{subarray}}\left(q-1\right)^{\left[\ell\left(\beta\right)=\ell\left(\gamma\right)\right]}\left(\eta_{\beta}^{\ast\left(q\right)}\otimes\eta_{\gamma}^{\ast\left(q\right)}\right)t^{\left|\beta\right|+\left|\gamma\right|}

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \left(\text{here, we have
substituted }j-1\text{ for }i\text{ in the outer sum}\right)

\displaystyle=\sum\limits_{j>0}\ \ \sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\beta,\gamma\in\operatorname*{Comp};\\
\left|\ell\left(\beta\right)-\ell\left(\gamma\right)\right|\leq 1;\\
\max\left\{\ell\left(\beta\right),\ell\left(\gamma\right)\right\}=j\end{subarray}}\ \ \underbrace{\left(-q\right)^{j-1}}_{\begin{subarray}{c}=\left(-q\right)^{\max\left\{\ell\left(\beta\right),\ell\left(\gamma\right)\right\}-1}\\
\text{(since }j=\max\left\{\ell\left(\beta\right),\ell\left(\gamma\right)\right\}\text{)}\end{subarray}}\left(q-1\right)^{\left[\ell\left(\beta\right)=\ell\left(\gamma\right)\right]}\left(\eta_{\beta}^{\ast\left(q\right)}\otimes\eta_{\gamma}^{\ast\left(q\right)}\right)t^{\left|\beta\right|+\left|\gamma\right|}

\displaystyle=\underbrace{\sum\limits_{j>0}\ \ \sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\beta,\gamma\in\operatorname*{Comp};\\
\left|\ell\left(\beta\right)-\ell\left(\gamma\right)\right|\leq 1;\\
\max\left\{\ell\left(\beta\right),\ell\left(\gamma\right)\right\}=j\end{subarray}}}_{=\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\beta,\gamma\in\operatorname*{Comp};\\
\left|\ell\left(\beta\right)-\ell\left(\gamma\right)\right|\leq 1;\\
\max\left\{\ell\left(\beta\right),\ell\left(\gamma\right)\right\}>0\end{subarray}}}\left(-q\right)^{\max\left\{\ell\left(\beta\right),\ell\left(\gamma\right)\right\}-1}\left(q-1\right)^{\left[\ell\left(\beta\right)=\ell\left(\gamma\right)\right]}\left(\eta_{\beta}^{\ast\left(q\right)}\otimes\eta_{\gamma}^{\ast\left(q\right)}\right)t^{\left|\beta\right|+\left|\gamma\right|}

\displaystyle=\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\beta,\gamma\in\operatorname*{Comp};\\
\left|\ell\left(\beta\right)-\ell\left(\gamma\right)\right|\leq 1;\\
\max\left\{\ell\left(\beta\right),\ell\left(\gamma\right)\right\}>0\end{subarray}}\left(-q\right)^{\max\left\{\ell\left(\beta\right),\ell\left(\gamma\right)\right\}-1}\left(q-1\right)^{\left[\ell\left(\beta\right)=\ell\left(\gamma\right)\right]}\left(\eta_{\beta}^{\ast\left(q\right)}\otimes\eta_{\gamma}^{\ast\left(q\right)}\right)t^{\left|\beta\right|+\left|\gamma\right|}.

Comparing this with

\displaystyle\Delta_{t}\left(\mathbf{G}\right)

\displaystyle=\Delta_{t}\left(\sum\limits_{n\geq 1}\eta_{n}^{\ast\left(q\right)}t^{n}\right)\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \left(\text{since }\mathbf{G}=G\left(t\right)=\sum\limits_{n\geq 1}\eta_{n}^{\ast\left(q\right)}t^{n}\right)

\displaystyle=\sum\limits_{n\geq 1}\Delta\left(\eta_{n}^{\ast\left(q\right)}\right)t^{n}\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \left(\text{by the definition of }\Delta_{t}\right),

we obtain

\displaystyle\sum\limits_{n\geq 1}\Delta\left(\eta_{n}^{\ast\left(q\right)}\right)t^{n}

\displaystyle=\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\beta,\gamma\in\operatorname*{Comp};\\
\left|\ell\left(\beta\right)-\ell\left(\gamma\right)\right|\leq 1;\\
\max\left\{\ell\left(\beta\right),\ell\left(\gamma\right)\right\}>0\end{subarray}}\left(-q\right)^{\max\left\{\ell\left(\beta\right),\ell\left(\gamma\right)\right\}-1}\left(q-1\right)^{\left[\ell\left(\beta\right)=\ell\left(\gamma\right)\right]}\left(\eta_{\beta}^{\ast\left(q\right)}\otimes\eta_{\gamma}^{\ast\left(q\right)}\right)t^{\left|\beta\right|+\left|\gamma\right|}.

Comparing coefficients of
t^{n}
on both sides of this equality, we obtain

\Delta\left(\eta_{n}^{\ast\left(q\right)}\right)=\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\beta,\gamma\in\operatorname*{Comp};\\
\left|\ell\left(\beta\right)-\ell\left(\gamma\right)\right|\leq 1;\\
\max\left\{\ell\left(\beta\right),\ell\left(\gamma\right)\right\}>0;\\
\left|\beta\right|+\left|\gamma\right|=n\end{subarray}}\left(-q\right)^{\max\left\{\ell\left(\beta\right),\ell\left(\gamma\right)\right\}-1}\left(q-1\right)^{\left[\ell\left(\beta\right)=\ell\left(\gamma\right)\right]}\eta_{\beta}^{\ast\left(q\right)}\otimes\eta_{\gamma}^{\ast\left(q\right)}

(81)

for each
n\geq 1
(in fact, the condition “
\left|\beta\right|+\left|\gamma\right|=n
” under
the summation sign ensures that the monomial
t^{\left|\beta\right|+\left|\gamma\right|}
is
t^{n}
).

Now, fix a positive integer
n
. Then, any two compositions
\beta
and

\gamma
that satisfy
\left|\beta\right|+\left|\gamma\right|=n
will automatically satisfy
\max\left\{\ell\left(\beta\right),\ell\left(\gamma\right)\right\}>0
(since otherwise, they
would satisfy
\max\left\{\ell\left(\beta\right),\ell\left(\gamma\right)\right\}=0
and thus
\beta=\varnothing
and
\gamma=\varnothing
, which would lead to
\left|\beta\right|+\left|\gamma\right|=\left|\varnothing\right|+\left|\varnothing\right|=0+0=0
, but this would contradict
\left|\beta\right|+\left|\gamma\right|=n>0
). Hence, in the summation
sign on the right hand side of (
81
), the condition
“
\max\left\{\ell\left(\beta\right),\ell\left(\gamma\right)\right\}>0
” is redundant. We can thus
rewrite this summation sign as follows:

\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\beta,\gamma\in\operatorname*{Comp};\\
\left|\ell\left(\beta\right)-\ell\left(\gamma\right)\right|\leq 1;\\
\max\left\{\ell\left(\beta\right),\ell\left(\gamma\right)\right\}>0;\\
\left|\beta\right|+\left|\gamma\right|=n\end{subarray}}=\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\beta,\gamma\in\operatorname*{Comp};\\
\left|\ell\left(\beta\right)-\ell\left(\gamma\right)\right|\leq 1;\\
\left|\beta\right|+\left|\gamma\right|=n\end{subarray}}=\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\beta,\gamma\in\operatorname*{Comp};\\
\left|\beta\right|+\left|\gamma\right|=n;\\
\left|\ell\left(\beta\right)-\ell\left(\gamma\right)\right|\leq 1\end{subarray}}.

Hence, (
81
) rewrites as

\Delta\left(\eta_{n}^{\ast\left(q\right)}\right)=\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\beta,\gamma\in\operatorname*{Comp};\\
\left|\beta\right|+\left|\gamma\right|=n;\\
\left|\ell\left(\beta\right)-\ell\left(\gamma\right)\right|\leq 1\end{subarray}}\left(-q\right)^{\max\left\{\ell\left(\beta\right),\ell\left(\gamma\right)\right\}-1}\left(q-1\right)^{\left[\ell\left(\beta\right)=\ell\left(\gamma\right)\right]}\eta_{\beta}^{\ast\left(q\right)}\otimes\eta_{\gamma}^{\ast\left(q\right)}.

This proves Theorem
4.27
.

Using Theorem
4.27
, we can easily compute the coproduct of
any
\eta_{\alpha}^{\ast\left(q\right)}
:
15
15

15

The symbol
“#” means “number”. Thus, e.g., we have
\left(\text{\# of odd numbers
}i\in\left[2n\right]\right)=n
for each
n\in\mathbb{N}
.

Corollary 4.32
.

Let
\alpha=\left(\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2},\ldots,\alpha_{k}\right)
be any composition. Then,

\displaystyle\Delta\left(\eta_{\alpha}^{\ast\left(q\right)}\right)

\displaystyle=\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\beta_{1},\beta_{2},\ldots,\beta_{k}\in\operatorname*{Comp};\\
\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2},\ldots,\gamma_{k}\in\operatorname*{Comp};\\
\left|\beta_{s}\right|+\left|\gamma_{s}\right|=\alpha_{s}\text{ for
all }s;\\
\left|\ell\left(\beta_{s}\right)-\ell\left(\gamma_{s}\right)\right|\leq 1\text{ for all }s\end{subarray}}\left(-q\right)^{\sum\limits_{s=1}^{k}\max\left\{\ell\left(\beta_{s}\right),\ell\left(\gamma_{s}\right)\right\}-k}

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \cdot\left(q-1\right)^{\left(\text{\# of all }s\in\left[k\right]\text{ such that }\ell\left(\beta_{s}\right)=\ell\left(\gamma_{s}\right)\right)}\left(\eta_{\beta_{1}\beta_{2}\cdots\beta_{k}}^{\ast\left(q\right)}\otimes\eta_{\gamma_{1}\gamma_{2}\cdots\gamma_{k}}^{\ast\left(q\right)}\right).

Proof 4.33
.

We agree to understand an expression of the form
\prod\limits_{s=1}^{k}u_{s}
to
mean
u_{1}u_{2}\cdots u_{k}
whenever
u_{1},u_{2},\ldots,u_{k}
are any
k

elements of any (not necessarily commutative) ring.

The comultiplication
\Delta
of the
\mathbf{k}
-bialgebra

\operatorname*{NSym}
is a
\mathbf{k}
-algebra homomorphism (indeed, this is
true for any
\mathbf{k}
-bialgebra), and thus respects products. However,
Proposition
4.13
yields
\eta_{\alpha}^{\ast\left(q\right)}=\eta_{\alpha_{1}}^{\ast\left(q\right)}\eta_{\alpha_{2}}^{\ast\left(q\right)}\cdots\eta_{\alpha_{k}}^{\ast\left(q\right)}
.
Hence,

\Delta\left(\eta_{\alpha}^{\ast\left(q\right)}\right)=\Delta\left(\eta_{\alpha_{1}}^{\ast\left(q\right)}\eta_{\alpha_{2}}^{\ast\left(q\right)}\cdots\eta_{\alpha_{k}}^{\ast\left(q\right)}\right)=\Delta\left(\eta_{\alpha_{1}}^{\ast\left(q\right)}\right)\Delta\left(\eta_{\alpha_{2}}^{\ast\left(q\right)}\right)\cdots\Delta\left(\eta_{\alpha_{k}}^{\ast\left(q\right)}\right)

(since
\Delta
respects products). In other words (using the notation

\prod\limits_{s=1}^{k}u_{s}
as explained above),

\Delta\left(\eta_{\alpha}^{\ast\left(q\right)}\right)=\prod\limits_{s=1}^{k}\Delta\left(\eta_{\alpha_{s}}^{\ast\left(q\right)}\right).

(82)

However, Theorem
4.27
shows that for each
s\in\left[k\right]
, we have

\Delta\left(\eta_{\alpha_{s}}^{\ast\left(q\right)}\right)=\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\beta,\gamma\in\operatorname*{Comp};\\
\left|\beta\right|+\left|\gamma\right|=\alpha_{s};\\
\left|\ell\left(\beta\right)-\ell\left(\gamma\right)\right|\leq 1\end{subarray}}\left(-q\right)^{\max\left\{\ell\left(\beta\right),\ell\left(\gamma\right)\right\}-1}\left(q-1\right)^{\left[\ell\left(\beta\right)=\ell\left(\gamma\right)\right]}\eta_{\beta}^{\ast\left(q\right)}\otimes\eta_{\gamma}^{\ast\left(q\right)}.

Multiplying these equalities for all
s\in\left[k\right]
, we obtain

\displaystyle\prod\limits_{s=1}^{k}\Delta\left(\eta_{\alpha_{s}}^{\ast\left(q\right)}\right)

\displaystyle=\prod\limits_{s=1}^{k}\ \ \sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\beta,\gamma\in\operatorname*{Comp};\\
\left|\beta\right|+\left|\gamma\right|=\alpha_{s};\\
\left|\ell\left(\beta\right)-\ell\left(\gamma\right)\right|\leq 1\end{subarray}}\left(-q\right)^{\max\left\{\ell\left(\beta\right),\ell\left(\gamma\right)\right\}-1}\left(q-1\right)^{\left[\ell\left(\beta\right)=\ell\left(\gamma\right)\right]}\eta_{\beta}^{\ast\left(q\right)}\otimes\eta_{\gamma}^{\ast\left(q\right)}

\displaystyle=\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\beta_{1},\beta_{2},\ldots,\beta_{k}\in\operatorname*{Comp};\\
\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2},\ldots,\gamma_{k}\in\operatorname*{Comp};\\
\left|\beta_{s}\right|+\left|\gamma_{s}\right|=\alpha_{s}\text{ for all }s;\\
\left|\ell\left(\beta_{s}\right)-\ell\left(\gamma_{s}\right)\right|\leq 1\text{ for
all }s\end{subarray}}\ \ \prod\limits_{s=1}^{k}\left(\left(-q\right)^{\max\left\{\ell\left(\beta_{s}\right),\ell\left(\gamma_{s}\right)\right\}-1}\left(q-1\right)^{\left[\ell\left(\beta_{s}\right)=\ell\left(\gamma_{s}\right)\right]}\eta_{\beta_{s}}^{\ast\left(q\right)}\otimes\eta_{\gamma_{s}}^{\ast\left(q\right)}\right)

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \left(\text{by the product
rule}\right)

\displaystyle=\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\beta_{1},\beta_{2},\ldots,\beta_{k}\in\operatorname*{Comp};\\
\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2},\ldots,\gamma_{k}\in\operatorname*{Comp};\\
\left|\beta_{s}\right|+\left|\gamma_{s}\right|=\alpha_{s}\text{ for all }s;\\
\left|\ell\left(\beta_{s}\right)-\ell\left(\gamma_{s}\right)\right|\leq 1\text{ for
all }s\end{subarray}}\underbrace{\left(\prod\limits_{s=1}^{k}\left(-q\right)^{\max\left\{\ell\left(\beta_{s}\right),\ell\left(\gamma_{s}\right)\right\}-1}\right)}_{\begin{subarray}{c}=\left(-q\right)^{\sum\limits_{s=1}^{k}\left(\max\left\{\ell\left(\beta_{s}\right),\ell\left(\gamma_{s}\right)\right\}-1\right)}\\
=\left(-q\right)^{\sum\limits_{s=1}^{k}\max\left\{\ell\left(\beta_{s}\right),\ell\left(\gamma_{s}\right)\right\}-k}\end{subarray}}

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \cdot\underbrace{\left(\prod\limits_{s=1}^{k}\left(q-1\right)^{\left[\ell\left(\beta_{s}\right)=\ell\left(\gamma_{s}\right)\right]}\right)}_{\begin{subarray}{c}=\left(q-1\right)^{\sum\limits_{s=1}^{k}\left[\ell\left(\beta_{s}\right)=\ell\left(\gamma_{s}\right)\right]}\\
=\left(q-1\right)^{\left(\text{\# of all
}s\in\left[k\right]\text{ such that }\ell\left(\beta_{s}\right)=\ell\left(\gamma_{s}\right)\right)}\end{subarray}}\underbrace{\left(\prod\limits_{s=1}^{k}\left(\eta_{\beta_{s}}^{\ast\left(q\right)}\otimes\eta_{\gamma_{s}}^{\ast\left(q\right)}\right)\right)}_{\begin{subarray}{c}=\eta_{\beta_{1}}^{\ast\left(q\right)}\eta_{\beta_{2}}^{\ast\left(q\right)}\cdots\eta_{\beta_{k}}^{\ast\left(q\right)}\otimes\eta_{\gamma_{1}}^{\ast\left(q\right)}\eta_{\gamma_{2}}^{\ast\left(q\right)}\cdots\eta_{\gamma_{k}}^{\ast\left(q\right)}\\
=\eta_{\beta_{1}\beta_{2}\cdots\beta_{k}}^{\ast\left(q\right)}\otimes\eta_{\gamma_{1}\gamma_{2}\cdots\gamma_{k}}^{\ast\left(q\right)}\\
\text{(since Corollary
\ref{cor.etastar.concat}}\\
\text{yields }\eta_{\beta_{1}}^{\ast\left(q\right)}\eta_{\beta_{2}}^{\ast\left(q\right)}\cdots\eta_{\beta_{k}}^{\ast\left(q\right)}=\eta_{\beta_{1}\beta_{2}\cdots\beta_{k}}^{\ast\left(q\right)}\\
\text{and }\eta_{\gamma_{1}}^{\ast\left(q\right)}\eta_{\gamma_{2}}^{\ast\left(q\right)}\cdots\eta_{\gamma_{k}}^{\ast\left(q\right)}=\eta_{\gamma_{1}\gamma_{2}\cdots\gamma_{k}}^{\ast\left(q\right)}\text{)}\end{subarray}}

\displaystyle=\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\beta_{1},\beta_{2},\ldots,\beta_{k}\in\operatorname*{Comp};\\
\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2},\ldots,\gamma_{k}\in\operatorname*{Comp};\\
\left|\beta_{s}\right|+\left|\gamma_{s}\right|=\alpha_{s}\text{ for all }s;\\
\left|\ell\left(\beta_{s}\right)-\ell\left(\gamma_{s}\right)\right|\leq 1\text{ for
all }s\end{subarray}}\left(-q\right)^{\sum\limits_{s=1}^{k}\max\left\{\ell\left(\beta_{s}\right),\ell\left(\gamma_{s}\right)\right\}-k}

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \cdot\left(q-1\right)^{\left(\text{\# of all }s\in\left[k\right]\text{ such that }\ell\left(\beta_{s}\right)=\ell\left(\gamma_{s}\right)\right)}\left(\eta_{\beta_{1}\beta_{2}\cdots\beta_{k}}^{\ast\left(q\right)}\otimes\eta_{\gamma_{1}\gamma_{2}\cdots\gamma_{k}}^{\ast\left(q\right)}\right).

In view of (
82
), we can rewrite this as

\displaystyle\Delta\left(\eta_{\alpha}^{\ast\left(q\right)}\right)

\displaystyle=\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\beta_{1},\beta_{2},\ldots,\beta_{k}\in\operatorname*{Comp};\\
\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2},\ldots,\gamma_{k}\in\operatorname*{Comp};\\
\left|\beta_{s}\right|+\left|\gamma_{s}\right|=\alpha_{s}\text{ for
all }s;\\
\left|\ell\left(\beta_{s}\right)-\ell\left(\gamma_{s}\right)\right|\leq 1\text{ for all }s\end{subarray}}\left(-q\right)^{\sum\limits_{s=1}^{k}\max\left\{\ell\left(\beta_{s}\right),\ell\left(\gamma_{s}\right)\right\}-k}

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \cdot\left(q-1\right)^{\left(\text{\# of all }s\in\left[k\right]\text{ such that }\ell\left(\beta_{s}\right)=\ell\left(\gamma_{s}\right)\right)}\left(\eta_{\beta_{1}\beta_{2}\cdots\beta_{k}}^{\ast\left(q\right)}\otimes\eta_{\gamma_{1}\gamma_{2}\cdots\gamma_{k}}^{\ast\left(q\right)}\right).

This proves Corollary
4.32
.

5
The product rule for
\eta_{\alpha}^{\left(q\right)}

We now approach the most intricate of the rules for the
\eta_{\alpha}^{\left(q\right)}
functions: the product rule, i.e., the expression of a
product
\eta_{\delta}^{\left(q\right)}\eta_{\varepsilon}^{\left(q\right)}
as a
\mathbb{Z}\left[q\right]
-linear combination of other

\eta_{\alpha}^{\left(q\right)}
’s. We shall give three different versions
of this rule, all equivalent but using somewhat different indexing sets. Only
the first version will be proved in detail, as it suffices for the
applications we have in mind.

5.1
The product rule in terms of compositions

Our first version of the product rule is as follows:
16
16

16

The symbol
“#” means “number” (so that, e.g., we have
\left(\text{\# of odd
numbers }i\in\left[2n\right]\right)=n
for each
n\in\mathbb{N}
).

Theorem 5.1
.

Let
\delta
and
\varepsilon
be two compositions. Then,

\displaystyle\eta_{\delta}^{\left(q\right)}\eta_{\varepsilon}^{\left(q\right)}

\displaystyle=\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}k\in\mathbb{N};\\
\beta_{1},\beta_{2},\ldots,\beta_{k}\in\operatorname*{Comp};\\
\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2},\ldots,\gamma_{k}\in\operatorname*{Comp};\\
\beta_{1}\beta_{2}\cdots\beta_{k}=\delta;\\
\gamma_{1}\gamma_{2}\cdots\gamma_{k}=\varepsilon;\\
\left|\ell\left(\beta_{s}\right)-\ell\left(\gamma_{s}\right)\right|\leq 1\text{ for
all }s;\\
\ell\left(\beta_{s}\right)+\ell\left(\gamma_{s}\right)>0\text{ for all }s\end{subarray}}\left(-q\right)^{\sum\limits_{s=1}^{k}\max\left\{\ell\left(\beta_{s}\right),\ell\left(\gamma_{s}\right)\right\}-k}

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \cdot\left(q-1\right)^{\left(\text{\# of all }s\in\left[k\right]\text{ such that }\ell\left(\beta_{s}\right)=\ell\left(\gamma_{s}\right)\right)}

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \cdot\eta_{\left(\left|\beta_{1}\right|+\left|\gamma_{1}\right|,\ \left|\beta_{2}\right|+\left|\gamma_{2}\right|,\ \ldots,\ \left|\beta_{k}\right|+\left|\gamma_{k}\right|\right)}^{\left(q\right)}.

Remark 5.2
.

The compositions
\beta_{1},\beta_{2},\ldots,\beta_{k}
and
\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2},\ldots,\gamma_{k}
in the sum on the right hand side of Theorem

5.1
are allowed to be empty. Nevertheless, the sum is finite.
Indeed, if
k\in\mathbb{N}
and
\beta_{1},\beta_{2},\ldots,\beta_{k}\in\operatorname*{Comp}
and
\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2},\ldots,\gamma_{k}\in\operatorname*{Comp}
satisfy

\displaystyle\beta_{1}\beta_{2}\cdots\beta_{k}

\displaystyle=\delta\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \text{and}\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \gamma_{1}\gamma_{2}\cdots\gamma_{k}=\varepsilon\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \text{and}

\displaystyle\left|\ell\left(\beta_{s}\right)-\ell\left(\gamma_{s}\right)\right|

\displaystyle\leq 1\text{ for all }s\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \text{and}\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ell\left(\beta_{s}\right)+\ell\left(\gamma_{s}\right)>0\text{ for all }s,

then
k\leq\ell\left(\delta\right)+\ell\left(\varepsilon\right)
,
because

\displaystyle\underbrace{\ell\left(\delta\right)}_{\begin{subarray}{c}=\ell\left(\beta_{1}\right)+\ell\left(\beta_{2}\right)+\cdots+\ell\left(\beta_{k}\right)\\
\text{(since }\delta=\beta_{1}\beta_{2}\cdots\beta_{k}\text{)}\end{subarray}}+\underbrace{\ell\left(\varepsilon\right)}_{\begin{subarray}{c}=\ell\left(\gamma_{1}\right)+\ell\left(\gamma_{2}\right)+\cdots+\ell\left(\gamma_{k}\right)\\
\text{(since }\varepsilon=\gamma_{1}\gamma_{2}\cdots\gamma_{k}\text{)}\end{subarray}}

\displaystyle=\left(\ell\left(\beta_{1}\right)+\ell\left(\beta_{2}\right)+\cdots+\ell\left(\beta_{k}\right)\right)+\left(\ell\left(\gamma_{1}\right)+\ell\left(\gamma_{2}\right)+\cdots+\ell\left(\gamma_{k}\right)\right)

\displaystyle=\sum\limits_{s=1}^{k}\ell\left(\beta_{s}\right)+\sum\limits_{s=1}^{k}\ell\left(\gamma_{s}\right)=\sum\limits_{s=1}^{k}\underbrace{\left(\ell\left(\beta_{s}\right)+\ell\left(\gamma_{s}\right)\right)}_{\begin{subarray}{c}\geq 1\\
\text{(since our above assumptions}\\
\text{yield }\ell\left(\beta_{s}\right)+\ell\left(\gamma_{s}\right)>0\text{,}\\
\text{but }\ell\left(\beta_{s}\right)+\ell\left(\gamma_{s}\right)\text{ is an
integer)}\end{subarray}}\geq\sum\limits_{s=1}^{k}1=k.

This narrows down the options for
k
to the finite set
\left\{0,1,\ldots,\ell\left(\delta\right)+\ell\left(\varepsilon\right)\right\}
, and thus leaves only finitely many options for
\beta_{1},\beta_{2},\ldots,\beta_{k}
(since there are only finitely many ways to
decompose the composition
\delta
as a concatenation
\delta=\beta_{1}\beta_{2}\cdots\beta_{k}
when
k
is fixed) and for
\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2},\ldots,\gamma_{k}
(similarly). Thus, the sum is finite.

Example 5.3
.

Let
\delta
and
\varepsilon
be two compositions
of the form
\delta=\left(a,b\right)
and
\varepsilon=\left(c\right)

for some positive integers
a,b,c
. Then, Theorem
5.1

expresses the product
\eta_{\delta}^{\left(q\right)}\eta_{\varepsilon}^{\left(q\right)}=\eta_{\left(a,b\right)}^{\left(q\right)}\eta_{\left(c\right)}^{\left(q\right)}
as a sum over all choices of

k\in\mathbb{N}
and of
k
compositions
\beta_{1},\beta_{2},\ldots,\beta_{k}\in\operatorname*{Comp}
and of
k
further compositions
\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2},\ldots,\gamma_{k}\in\operatorname*{Comp}
satisfying

\displaystyle\beta_{1}\beta_{2}\cdots\beta_{k}

\displaystyle=\delta\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \text{and}\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \gamma_{1}\gamma_{2}\cdots\gamma_{k}=\varepsilon\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \text{and}

\displaystyle\left|\ell\left(\beta_{s}\right)-\ell\left(\gamma_{s}\right)\right|

\displaystyle\leq 1\text{ for all }s\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \text{and}\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ell\left(\beta_{s}\right)+\ell\left(\gamma_{s}\right)>0\text{ for all }s.

These choices are

1.

having
k=1
and
\beta_{1}=\delta=\left(a,b\right)
and
\gamma_{1}=\varepsilon=\left(c\right)
;

2.

having
k=2
and
\beta_{1}=\left(a\right)
and
\beta_{2}=\left(b\right)
and
\gamma_{1}=\varnothing
and
\gamma_{2}=\left(c\right)
;

3.

having
k=2
and
\beta_{1}=\left(a\right)
and
\beta_{2}=\left(b\right)
and
\gamma_{1}=\left(c\right)
and
\gamma_{2}=\varnothing
;

4.

having
k=3
and
\beta_{1}=\varnothing
and
\beta_{2}=\left(a\right)
and
\beta_{3}=\left(b\right)
and
\gamma_{1}=\left(c\right)
and
\gamma_{2}=\varnothing
and
\gamma_{3}=\varnothing
;

5.

having
k=3
and
\beta_{1}=\left(a\right)
and
\beta_{2}=\varnothing
and
\beta_{3}=\left(b\right)
and
\gamma_{1}=\varnothing
and
\gamma_{2}=\left(c\right)
and
\gamma_{3}=\varnothing
;

6.

having
k=3
and
\beta_{1}=\left(a\right)
and
\beta_{2}=\left(b\right)
and
\beta_{3}=\varnothing
and
\gamma_{1}=\varnothing
and

\gamma_{2}=\varnothing
and
\gamma_{3}=\left(c\right)
.

Thus, Theorem
5.1
yields

\displaystyle\eta_{\left(a,b\right)}^{\left(q\right)}\eta_{\left(c\right)}^{\left(q\right)}

\displaystyle=\left(-q\right)^{2-1}\left(q-1\right)^{0}\eta_{\left(a+b+c\right)}^{\left(q\right)}+\left(-q\right)^{1+1-2}\left(q-1\right)^{1}\eta_{\left(a,\ b+c\right)}^{\left(q\right)}

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ +\left(-q\right)^{1+1-2}\left(q-1\right)^{1}\eta_{\left(a+c,\ b\right)}^{\left(q\right)}+\left(-q\right)^{1+1+1-3}\left(q-1\right)^{0}\eta_{\left(c,a,b\right)}^{\left(q\right)}

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ +\left(-q\right)^{1+1+1-3}\left(q-1\right)^{0}\eta_{\left(a,c,b\right)}^{\left(q\right)}+\left(-q\right)^{1+1+1-3}\left(q-1\right)^{0}\eta_{\left(a,b,c\right)}^{\left(q\right)}

\displaystyle=-q\eta_{\left(a+b+c\right)}^{\left(q\right)}+\left(q-1\right)\eta_{\left(a,\ b+c\right)}^{\left(q\right)}+\left(q-1\right)\eta_{\left(a+c,\ b\right)}^{\left(q\right)}+\eta_{\left(c,a,b\right)}^{\left(q\right)}+\eta_{\left(a,c,b\right)}^{\left(q\right)}+\eta_{\left(a,b,c\right)}^{\left(q\right)}.

Note that the last three addends
\eta_{\left(c,a,b\right)}^{\left(q\right)},\ \eta_{\left(a,c,b\right)}^{\left(q\right)},\ \eta_{\left(a,b,c\right)}^{\left(q\right)}
here come from those
choices in which
\min\left\{\ell\left(\beta_{s}\right),\ell\left(\gamma_{s}\right)\right\}=0
for each
s\in\left[k\right]
(that is,
for each
s\in\left[k\right]
, one of the two compositions
\beta_{s}
and

\gamma_{s}
is empty). In these choices, the two powers

\left(-q\right)^{\sum\limits_{s=1}^{k}\max\left\{\ell\left(\beta_{s}\right),\ell\left(\gamma_{s}\right)\right\}-k}\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \text{and}\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \left(q-1\right)^{\left(\text{\# of all }s\in\left[k\right]\text{ such that }\ell\left(\beta_{s}\right)=\ell\left(\gamma_{s}\right)\right)}

are equal to
1
(because the exponents are easily seen to be
0
), whereas
the composition
\left(\left|\beta_{1}\right|+\left|\gamma_{1}\right|,\ \left|\beta_{2}\right|+\left|\gamma_{2}\right|,\ \ldots,\ \left|\beta_{k}\right|+\left|\gamma_{k}\right|\right)
is a shuffle of
\delta
with
\varepsilon
.
Thus, these choices contribute terms of the form
\eta_{\varphi}^{\left(q\right)}
, where
\varphi
is a shuffle of
\delta
with
\varepsilon
, to
the right hand side of Theorem
5.1
, and these terms all have
coefficient
1
. These are the only choices of
k,\ \beta_{1},\beta_{2},\ldots,\beta_{k},\ \gamma_{1},\gamma_{2},\ldots,\gamma_{k}
that have

k=\ell\left(\delta\right)+\ell\left(\varepsilon\right)
. All other
choices have
k<\ell\left(\delta\right)+\ell\left(\varepsilon\right)
,
and these choices lead to addends that involve either a nontrivial power of

-q
or a nontrivial power of
q-1
(or both). In this sense, we can view
Theorem
5.1
as a deformation of the overlapping shuffle
product formula for
M_{\delta}M_{\varepsilon}
(see, e.g.,
[
GriRei20
, Proposition
5.1.3]
), although the concept of a “deformation” must be understood in a wide sense (we cannot
obtain the latter just by specializing the former).

We will derive Theorem
5.1
from Corollary

4.32
. For this, we will again use the duality between

\operatorname*{NSym}
and
\operatorname*{QSym}
:

Lemma 5.4
.

Let
f,g\in\operatorname*{QSym}
and
h\in\operatorname*{NSym}
be arbitrary. Let the tensor
\Delta\left(h\right)\in\operatorname*{NSym}\otimes\operatorname*{NSym}
be written in the form

\Delta\left(h\right)=\sum\limits_{i\in I}s_{i}\otimes t_{i}
, where
I
is a
finite set and where
s_{i},t_{i}\in\operatorname*{NSym}
for each
i\in I
.
Then,

\left\langle h,fg\right\rangle=\sum\limits_{i\in I}\left\langle s_{i},f\right\rangle\left\langle t_{i},g\right\rangle.

Proof 5.5
.

This is analogous to Lemma
4.16
, except that the roles of

\operatorname*{QSym}
and
\operatorname*{NSym}
have now been switched.

For the sake of convenience, let us extend Lemma
5.4
to
infinite sums with only finitely many infinite addends:

Lemma 5.6
.

Let
f,g\in\operatorname*{QSym}
and

h\in\operatorname*{NSym}
be arbitrary. Let the tensor
\Delta\left(h\right)\in\operatorname*{NSym}\otimes\operatorname*{NSym}
be written in
the form
\Delta\left(h\right)=\sum\limits_{i\in I}s_{i}\otimes t_{i}
, where
I

is a set and where
s_{i},t_{i}\in\operatorname*{NSym}
for each
i\in I
are
chosen such that only finitely many
i\in I
satisfy
s_{i}\neq 0
. Then,

\left\langle h,fg\right\rangle=\sum\limits_{i\in I}\left\langle s_{i},f\right\rangle\left\langle t_{i},g\right\rangle.

Proof 5.7
.

This is easily reduced to Lemma
5.4
(just replace the set

I
by its subset
I^{\prime}:=\left\{i\in I\ \mid\ s_{i}\neq 0\right\}
).

Proof 5.8

(Proof of Theorem
5.1
.)
.

Forget that we fixed
\delta
and

\varepsilon
. For any three compositions
\alpha=\left(\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2},\ldots,\alpha_{k}\right)
,
\delta
and
\varepsilon
, we define
a polynomial

\displaystyle d_{\delta,\varepsilon}^{\alpha}\left(X\right)

\displaystyle:=\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\beta_{1},\beta_{2},\ldots,\beta_{k}\in\operatorname*{Comp};\\
\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2},\ldots,\gamma_{k}\in\operatorname*{Comp};\\
\beta_{1}\beta_{2}\cdots\beta_{k}=\delta;\\
\gamma_{1}\gamma_{2}\cdots\gamma_{k}=\varepsilon;\\
\left|\ell\left(\beta_{s}\right)-\ell\left(\gamma_{s}\right)\right|\leq 1\text{ for all }s;\\
\left|\beta_{s}\right|+\left|\gamma_{s}\right|=\alpha_{s}\text{ for all }s\end{subarray}}\left(-X\right)^{\sum\limits_{s=1}^{k}\max\left\{\ell\left(\beta_{s}\right),\ell\left(\gamma_{s}\right)\right\}-k}

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \cdot\left(X-1\right)^{\left(\text{\# of all }s\in\left[k\right]\text{ such that }\ell\left(\beta_{s}\right)=\ell\left(\gamma_{s}\right)\right)}

(83)

\displaystyle\in\mathbb{Z}\left[X\right]

(this really is a polynomial, since the exponent
\sum\limits_{s=1}^{k}\max\left\{\ell\left(\beta_{s}\right),\ell\left(\gamma_{s}\right)\right\}-k
is
easily seen to be a nonnegative integer). Thus, clearly, for any three
compositions
\alpha=\left(\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2},\ldots,\alpha_{k}\right)
,
\delta
and
\varepsilon
, we have

\displaystyle d_{\delta,\varepsilon}^{\alpha}\left(q\right)

\displaystyle=\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\beta_{1},\beta_{2},\ldots,\beta_{k}\in\operatorname*{Comp};\\
\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2},\ldots,\gamma_{k}\in\operatorname*{Comp};\\
\beta_{1}\beta_{2}\cdots\beta_{k}=\delta;\\
\gamma_{1}\gamma_{2}\cdots\gamma_{k}=\varepsilon;\\
\left|\ell\left(\beta_{s}\right)-\ell\left(\gamma_{s}\right)\right|\leq 1\text{ for all }s;\\
\left|\beta_{s}\right|+\left|\gamma_{s}\right|=\alpha_{s}\text{ for all }s\end{subarray}}\left(-q\right)^{\sum\limits_{s=1}^{k}\max\left\{\ell\left(\beta_{s}\right),\ell\left(\gamma_{s}\right)\right\}-k}

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \cdot\left(q-1\right)^{\left(\text{\# of all }s\in\left[k\right]\text{ such that }\ell\left(\beta_{s}\right)=\ell\left(\gamma_{s}\right)\right)}

(84)

Note that the sums on the right hand sides of (
83
) and
(
84
) are finite (because for a given
k\in\mathbb{N}

and given compositions
\delta
and
\varepsilon
, there are only finitely
many ways to decompose
\delta
as
\delta=\beta_{1}\beta_{2}\cdots\beta_{k}
,
and only finitely many ways to decompose
\varepsilon
as
\varepsilon=\gamma_{1}\gamma_{2}\cdots\gamma_{k}
). All sums that will appear in this
proof will be finite or essentially finite (i.e., have only finitely many
nonzero addends). We note that
\mathbf{k}
-linear maps always respect such sums.

Now, we shall proceed by proving several claims. Our first claim is a
restatement of Corollary
4.32
:

Claim 1:
Let
\alpha
be any composition. Assume that
r
is
invertible. Then,

\Delta\left(\eta_{\alpha}^{\ast\left(q\right)}\right)=\sum\limits_{\delta,\varepsilon\in\operatorname*{Comp}}d_{\delta,\varepsilon}^{\alpha}\left(q\right)\eta_{\delta}^{\ast\left(q\right)}\otimes\eta_{\varepsilon}^{\ast\left(q\right)}.

(85)

Proof 5.9

(Proof of Claim 1.)
.

Write the composition
\alpha
as
\alpha=\left(\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2},\ldots,\alpha_{k}\right)
. Then, Corollary

4.32
yields

\displaystyle\Delta\left(\eta_{\alpha}^{\ast\left(q\right)}\right)

\displaystyle=\underbrace{\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\beta_{1},\beta_{2},\ldots,\beta_{k}\in\operatorname*{Comp};\\
\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2},\ldots,\gamma_{k}\in\operatorname*{Comp};\\
\left|\beta_{s}\right|+\left|\gamma_{s}\right|=\alpha_{s}\text{ for all }s;\\
\left|\ell\left(\beta_{s}\right)-\ell\left(\gamma_{s}\right)\right|\leq 1\text{ for
all }s\end{subarray}}}_{\begin{subarray}{c}=\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\beta_{1},\beta_{2},\ldots,\beta_{k}\in\operatorname*{Comp};\\
\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2},\ldots,\gamma_{k}\in\operatorname*{Comp};\\
\left|\ell\left(\beta_{s}\right)-\ell\left(\gamma_{s}\right)\right|\leq 1\text{ for all }s;\\
\left|\beta_{s}\right|+\left|\gamma_{s}\right|=\alpha_{s}\text{ for all }s\end{subarray}}\\
=\sum\limits_{\delta,\varepsilon\in\operatorname*{Comp}}\ \ \sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}{}_{\begin{subarray}{c}\beta_{1},\beta_{2},\ldots,\beta_{k}\in\operatorname*{Comp};\\
\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2},\ldots,\gamma_{k}\in\operatorname*{Comp};\\
\beta_{1}\beta_{2}\cdots\beta_{k}=\delta;\\
\gamma_{1}\gamma_{2}\cdots\gamma_{k}=\varepsilon;\\
\left|\ell\left(\beta_{s}\right)-\ell\left(\gamma_{s}\right)\right|\leq 1\text{ for all }s;\\
\left|\beta_{s}\right|+\left|\gamma_{s}\right|=\alpha_{s}\text{ for all }s\end{subarray}}\\
\text{(here, we have
split up the sum according}\\
\text{to the values of }\beta_{1}\beta_{2}\cdots\beta_{k}\text{ and }\gamma_{1}\gamma_{2}\cdots\gamma_{k}\text{)}\end{subarray}}\end{subarray}}\left(-q\right)^{\sum\limits_{s=1}^{k}\max\left\{\ell\left(\beta_{s}\right),\ell\left(\gamma_{s}\right)\right\}-k}

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \cdot\left(q-1\right)^{\left(\text{\# of all }s\in\left[k\right]\text{ such that }\ell\left(\beta_{s}\right)=\ell\left(\gamma_{s}\right)\right)}\left(\eta_{\beta_{1}\beta_{2}\cdots\beta_{k}}^{\ast\left(q\right)}\otimes\eta_{\gamma_{1}\gamma_{2}\cdots\gamma_{k}}^{\ast\left(q\right)}\right)

\displaystyle=\sum\limits_{\delta,\varepsilon\in\operatorname*{Comp}}\ \ \sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\beta_{1},\beta_{2},\ldots,\beta_{k}\in\operatorname*{Comp};\\
\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2},\ldots,\gamma_{k}\in\operatorname*{Comp};\\
\beta_{1}\beta_{2}\cdots\beta_{k}=\delta;\\
\gamma_{1}\gamma_{2}\cdots\gamma_{k}=\varepsilon;\\
\left|\ell\left(\beta_{s}\right)-\ell\left(\gamma_{s}\right)\right|\leq 1\text{ for all }s;\\
\left|\beta_{s}\right|+\left|\gamma_{s}\right|=\alpha_{s}\text{ for all }s\end{subarray}}\left(-q\right)^{\sum\limits_{s=1}^{k}\max\left\{\ell\left(\beta_{s}\right),\ell\left(\gamma_{s}\right)\right\}-k}

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \cdot\left(q-1\right)^{\left(\text{\# of all }s\in\left[k\right]\text{ such that }\ell\left(\beta_{s}\right)=\ell\left(\gamma_{s}\right)\right)}\underbrace{\left(\eta_{\beta_{1}\beta_{2}\cdots\beta_{k}}^{\ast\left(q\right)}\otimes\eta_{\gamma_{1}\gamma_{2}\cdots\gamma_{k}}^{\ast\left(q\right)}\right)}_{\begin{subarray}{c}=\eta_{\delta}^{\ast\left(q\right)}\otimes\eta_{\varepsilon}^{\ast\left(q\right)}\\
\text{(since }\beta_{1}\beta_{2}\cdots\beta_{k}=\delta\\
\text{and }\gamma_{1}\gamma_{2}\cdots\gamma_{k}=\varepsilon\text{)}\end{subarray}}

\displaystyle=\sum\limits_{\delta,\varepsilon\in\operatorname*{Comp}}\ \ \sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\beta_{1},\beta_{2},\ldots,\beta_{k}\in\operatorname*{Comp};\\
